Last updated: 2026-09-11, migration 130 APPLIED AND VERIFIED IN PRODUCTION + frontend wiring (**Code+tests: commit `54758fd`. Docs: commit `e55a11f`.**
E ran both `backend/supabase/migrations/130_atomic_equipment_recipe_save.sql` and `backend/supabase/migration_130_recipe_save_tests.sql` directly against the production Supabase project. E's own words: "Migration 130 and its complete verification script both ran successfully. No SQL error occurred, so every assertion passed and zero sections were skipped." Per E's explicit instruction, this is recorded as fully verified without probing for NOTICE message text.

**Frontend wiring completed this pass** (`src/persistence.ts`, `src/main.tsx`): `saveDeviceRecipes` now calls `rpc/save_equipment_recipe` once per recipe instead of its old separate PATCH/INSERT/upsert/delete sequence.
- Sends an existing recipe's real `equipmentTypeId` as `p_equipment_type_id` (a new recipe sends `null`) -- closes the rename-creates-a-duplicate risk `PRODUCT_EQUIPMENT_RECIPE_ATOMIC_SAVE_PLAN.md` §4 flagged, since the RPC now updates the exact row instead of re-resolving by name.
- Maps every RPC response via `mapSaveEquipmentRecipeResult` and returns the saved recipes (was `Promise<void>`); `main.tsx`'s debounce-save effect uses that to backfill a newly created recipe's real id into local state, applying only the one changed field and only when it actually changed (returns the same array reference otherwise) so this can't loop the debounce-save effect forever.
- Per-recipe atomic behavior preserved: one RPC call per recipe, sequential, stops at the first failing recipe (no skip-and-continue) -- matches the decided design (§3 Option A).
- Errors stay plain to the caller (`"Some equipment recipes could not be saved."`); the real status/body (`message`/`code`/`details`/`hint`) is logged via `console.error` -- same convention as every other checked write in this file.

**Regression tests rewritten**: `src/device-recipes-write-verification.test.ts` -- the old suite tested the now-deleted multi-request sequence's own request ordering and no longer applied to the RPC-based implementation. New coverage: new recipe save, existing recipe save, renamed recipe save (name sent alongside the existing id), a failed save (RPC error surfaced as a plain message, real detail logged), and a multiple-recipe save (one RPC call per recipe in order, stopping at the first failure).

`npx tsc -b` clean. `npx vitest run` (this environment's default parallel pool hits an OOM crash unrelated to these changes -- re-run with `--no-file-parallelism` to work around it): **264/264 passing** (was 277 against the old, now-deleted test suite; the new suite has 12 tests covering the same functional ground plus the new call shape, replacing the ~24 that tested request sequencing that no longer exists). `npx eslint .`: 0 errors, 73 pre-existing warnings (unchanged baseline). `npm run build`: clean. `npm run test:smoke`: 6/6 passing.

**Explicitly deferred, not attempted this pass, per direct instruction**: the two-session concurrency verification (test script Section 16; the proposed Node.js `pg`-based harness). Not built, not run. Still a documented decision point for E, not a step skipped by mistake.

Pushed, deployed, and production-verified -- see the entry above this one for the full deployment record.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11, migration 130 THIRD correction pass (**STILL NOT RUN, NOT APPROVED, NOTHING COMMITTED.** E found four more issues in the test script only (the migration itself was unchanged this round); all corrected:
1. **Non-privileged-user fixture made deterministic.** The old fixture query required finding a real member who ALREADY had zero roles -- a precondition this test couldn't guarantee, risking a skip through no fault of the RPC. Now: ANY non-admin member's current roles are snapshotted and temporarily stripped for the span of Sections 1b/1c, restored immediately after (with nested exception protection, see #4). Deterministic as long as the workspace has any second real member at all.
2. **Section 14 now checks the actual JSON type of every field** (`jsonb_typeof`), not just key presence -- key presence alone could not have caught e.g. `retired` coming back as the string `"true"` instead of a real JSON boolean. Two calls: one with a real image (`imageUrl` must be JSON type `string`), one without (`imageUrl` must be JSON type `null` specifically).
3. **Section 9 now snapshots and compares state after EACH individual rejection**, not once across the whole ~15-case group -- a single before/after pair could only prove the net state matched at the end, not that every individual rejection left state unchanged. Added a reusable `pg_temp.zz_snapshot_recipe()` helper (one combined row+components snapshot per call) to keep this manageable.
4. **Nested restoration protection added** for both the admin's temporarily-removed warehouse role (Section 2) and the non-privileged fixture's temporarily-stripped roles (Sections 1b/1c): the risky call is now wrapped in its own `begin...exception...end` with the restore logic duplicated in both the success path and the exception handler (which restores then re-raises) -- not just "restore after," which would skip the restore entirely if the call unexpectedly failed.

`npx tsc -b` clean. `npx vitest run`: **277/277 passing** (unchanged -- no frontend files touched this round). Nothing run against Supabase; HEAD and `origin/main` unchanged.

**Still exactly two steps for E, in order, once review is complete**: (1) `backend/supabase/migrations/130_atomic_equipment_recipe_save.sql`; (2) `backend/supabase/migration_130_recipe_save_tests.sql`, expecting the final notice "ALL MIGRATION 130 RECIPE SAVE TESTS PASSED -- ZERO SECTIONS SKIPPED". Two-session concurrency remains a documented decision point, not a step.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11, migration 130 SECOND correction pass (**STILL NOT RUN, NOT APPROVED, NOTHING COMMITTED.** E reviewed the corrected migration 130 draft and its test script and found nine more issues; all corrected in this pass:
1. **`p_components = SQL NULL` is now REJECTED**, not silently treated as `[]`. Only an explicit JSON array (including an explicit `[]`) is accepted; SQL NULL, JSON `null`, an object, a string, a number, and a boolean are all rejected with `EC011`. This closes a real risk: a frontend bug that accidentally omitted the field could otherwise have silently cleared an existing recipe's components.
2. **`imageUrl` contract resolved via an explicit mapper** (E's stated preference): a new `SaveEquipmentRecipeResult` type plus `mapSaveEquipmentRecipeResult()` (`src/persistence.ts`) converts the RPC's raw `imageUrl: string | null` into `BuildRecipe`'s `imageUrl?: string`, preserving `BuildRecipe`'s existing null-to-undefined convention. 4 new tests (`src/equipment-recipe-rpc-mapper.test.ts`). Not wired into any save path yet.
3. **Section 2 (workspace-admin authorization) now independently proves the `is_workspace_admin()` branch** — the admin fixture's `warehouse` role (if any) is temporarily removed for the duration of that one call and restored immediately after, so the success can't be explained by a warehouse-role pass-through.
4. **Invalid-quantity tests corrected**: every case now asserts `SQLSTATE = 'EC011'` specifically (not just "an exception occurred"); the quantity is passed as JSON text so the numeric cast happens inside the RPC, not in the test script's own argument construction; `-Infinity` added as its own case; a `p_retired = NULL` case added, also asserting `EC011`.
5. **Unchanged-state assertions strengthened**: the component-validation section, the forced-failure rollback test, and the duplicate-name-collision test now all snapshot and compare the COMPLETE relevant row/collection (recipe row(s) AND the full ordered component collection — ids, inventory ids, quantities, line order, active state), not just a row or a count. The invalid-output-item-id test gained the same full before/after treatment.
6. **New recipe-name normalization test**: a name with leading/trailing whitespace is stored and returned trimmed, and a later save using the already-trimmed name resolves to the same row rather than creating a duplicate.
7. **"Cross-record id" wording corrected**: that section proves a supplied real `equipment_type_id` is authoritative (the function renames/updates that specific record) — it was never a rejection case, and is now described that way. The actual name-collision rejection is Section 7, unchanged.
8. **Fixed a duplicated sentence** left over from the prior pass's edit to `PRODUCT_PROJECT_BOM_ATOMIC_REPLACE_PLAN.md`.
9. **Two-session concurrency (Section 16) stays a documented limitation, not something E is asked to run manually right now.** Added a concrete plan: a standalone Node.js script using the `pg` client library (not currently a dependency of this project) could automate both cases as one command with no manual tab-coordination — proposed for E's decision, nothing added yet. The manual two-tab process remains documented as the fallback if E would rather not add a dependency.

Last updated: 2026-09-11, migration 130 correction pass (**STILL LOCAL/UNRUN — no SQL executed against any database, nothing committed.** E reviewed the first draft of migration 130 and found six real issues, all corrected in this pass:
1. **`min(id)`/`count(*)` on `inventory_items.id` (uuid) replaced with `array_agg(id)` + array-length/first-element reads**, everywhere — whether a given Postgres build even provides a `MIN`/`MAX` aggregate for `uuid` is version-dependent; `array_agg` has no such risk.
2. **Authorization was checking `is_app_admin(auth.uid())` — the legacy GLOBAL admin system — not "warehouse and WORKSPACE administrators" as decided.** Replaced with `public.is_workspace_admin(v_caller_workspace_id)` (migration 115, already hardened, already granted to `authenticated`). Traced and confirmed: `workspace_members.is_workspace_admin` was backfilled from `app_admins` at migration-115 time, and every admin grant/revoke since has been routed through `bridge_grant_admin()`/`bridge_revoke_admin()` (migration 124, confirmed live in `persistence.ts`) — so the real, current production admin is already correctly bridged, not newly locked out.
3. **Added validation before any write**: `p_components` must be a real JSON array (object/scalar/explicit JSON `null` now rejected with `EC011`, not a raw error); `p_retired` must not be null; `p_equipment_name` is trimmed once and that trimmed value used consistently everywhere (lock key, lookup, stored row); a quantity that is NaN, Infinity, `-Infinity`, or too large for `numeric(12,2)` is now rejected explicitly (`> 0` alone does not catch NaN, since Postgres numeric NaN sorts larger than any real number).
4. **Advisory lock switched to `hashtextextended(text, seed)`** (a real 64-bit hash) instead of `hashtext()`'s 32-bit one — noted for the record that this is a genuine improvement, not a copy of an existing pattern (migration 124's own admin-revoke lock still uses plain 32-bit `hashtext()` on a single shared key, which is a different, lower-risk situation than a per-recipe-name key). The "different names never contend" claim is corrected to "a hash collision would only cause harmless temporary blocking, not an incorrect result."
5. **Returned JSON changed to match `BuildRecipe`'s actual frontend shape directly** (`equipmentTypeId`/`name`/`outputName`/`description`/`imageUrl`/`retired`/`components: [{itemName, qty}]`) instead of database column names and bare ids with no item names, which `saveDeviceRecipes` could not have consumed without an unwritten mapping layer.
6. **`EC008` is no longer "next free" for any other RPC** — migration 130 claims `EC008`-`EC016`. `PRODUCT_PROJECT_BOM_ATOMIC_REPLACE_PLAN.md` updated: the Project BOM RPC starts at `EC017` once actually drafted, re-verified at that time.

**Frontend contract change made this pass (type + load mapping only, NOT the save-path wiring)**: `BuildRecipe` (`src/persistence.ts`) gains `equipmentTypeId?: string`; `loadDeviceRecipes`'s `EQUIPMENT_TYPE_SELECT`/`mapEquipmentTypeRow` now select and map `equipment_types.id` into it. Existing recipe-edit handlers in `main.tsx` already spread `{ ...recipe, ... }`, so the field is retained through edits with no handler changes needed. `saveDeviceRecipes` is **not yet wired** to call the new RPC or send this id back — that remains a separate, later step. `npx tsc -b` and `npx vitest run` both clean/passing after this change (273/273).

**Complete verification script written**: `backend/supabase/migration_130_recipe_save_tests.sql` (~970 lines, mirrors migration 127/124's own established test conventions exactly — real users/workspace via the one real active workspace, `ZZ_TEST_`-prefixed synthetic fixtures, before/after snapshots on every rejection, `begin;...rollback;`, a skip-tracking mechanism that hard-fails if anything was skipped). Covers: anon/unauthorized rejection; warehouse authorization; workspace-admin authorization; the global-admin-only drift case (proving the fix in #2 above actually changed behavior, not just its wording) plus an explicit assertion that the real production admin IS properly bridged; zero/suspended/multiple-workspace failure (mirrors migration 124's own live-tested + structural-proof technique for the zero-workspace case); new recipe creation; rename preserving the same id; unknown and cross-record ids; duplicate-name collision; empty-components-clears-one-recipe-only; all six component-payload validation cases; invalid output item id; output-name zero/one/multiple-match tolerance; a forced mid-transaction component failure (via a temporary, scoped, dropped-after-use trigger) proving the recipe's own row also rolls back, not just its components; identical-retry identity preservation; documented (not automated — cannot run from one connection) two-session concurrency instructions for both a new and an existing recipe; full `BuildRecipe` JSON-shape mapping; `search_path`/grant verification. **This script has been carefully hand-reviewed (including two real bugs found and fixed during that review — a dead/redundant trigger-function definition, and an incorrect quote-doubling inside a dollar-quoted dynamic-SQL template) but NOT executed against any Postgres instance** — no `psql`/Postgres is available in this environment to run it. E's own run in Supabase's SQL editor is the actual validation step, consistent with the standing one-step-at-a-time process.

**Exactly what E will run, in order, once review is complete**: (1) `backend/supabase/migrations/130_atomic_equipment_recipe_save.sql`; (2) `backend/supabase/migration_130_recipe_save_tests.sql`, expecting the final notice "ALL MIGRATION 130 RECIPE SAVE TESTS PASSED -- ZERO SECTIONS SKIPPED"; (3) the two-session concurrency check documented in the test script's own Section 16, run separately via an external two-connection harness (not part of the SQL file). Last updated: 2026-09-11, second correction pass (**STILL LOCAL ONLY — NOT COMMITTED OR DEPLOYED. Two code corrections and one wording correction, all reviewed and applied on top of the 2026-09-11 correction pass below.**
1. **`saveInventoryMovements` now rejects a movement with a missing or whitespace-only sku before any lookup or write is attempted** — checked and logged first, ahead of everything else in the function. This closes a real data-loss gap the prior pass left open (it only fixed a nonempty-but-unresolved sku, not a blank one) and needed no product decision: `inventory_item_id` is `NOT NULL` on `inventory_movements`, so a blank sku can never be written correctly regardless of any lookup outcome. Whitespace-only is treated identically to empty, via one shared `isBlankSku` check reused everywhere a sku is validated in this function, so a stray-space value can't slip past a truthiness check. A mixed batch (one bad movement among several good ones) rejects the whole save, not just the bad line.
2. **Four `main.tsx` handlers (`handleUpdateVendor`, `handleUpdateSalesQuoteBomLineCatalogLink`, `handleUpdateSalesQuoteInfo`, `handleUpdateSiteHardwareRule`) were optimistic and never reverted on failure** — a failed save left the unsaved value displayed as if it had saved, with only a status message (easy to miss) as the only sign anything went wrong. All four are now pessimistic: local React state updates only after the persistence call succeeds; on failure the existing value stays exactly as it was (nothing to revert, since nothing was changed yet) and the existing status message still shows. No rollback/array-snapshot pattern used — this sidesteps that whole class of concurrency risk by not touching local state until the database has confirmed the write. `handleUpdateVendor` additionally no longer touches local state at all when there's no authenticated session (it used to update the UI unconditionally, session or not). Verified by direct code trace only — this repository has no component-handler test harness, and none was added (no new UI-testing dependency introduced).
3. **Wording correction, not a behavior change**: `PRODUCT_ERROR_VISIBILITY_AUDIT.md` previously described the OPTIONAL, unmatched-association behavior (an unresolved equipment/project/build NAME saving as `null`) as "documented behavior" and "schema-valid, documented" — implying the nullable foreign keys settle the question of what the product wants. They don't: a nullable FK proves only that the database will accept a null value there, not that silently saving one for an unmatched name is the intended rule. Corrected to describe this, in both `PRODUCT_ERROR_VISIBILITY_AUDIT.md` and here, as: existing behavior preserved (not changed) in this pass; permitted by the current schema; NOT yet confirmed as the desired product rule; and a documented, still-open future data-quality decision. No optional-association behavior was changed tonight — only the claim about why it's acceptable was corrected.

3 new tests for the blank-sku preflight (`src/task5-restore-write-verification.test.ts`, now 28 tests in that file, up from 25). Full suite: **273/273 passing** (was 270 before this pass). `npx tsc -b`, `npx eslint .` (0 errors, 73 pre-existing warnings), `npm run build`, and `npm run test:smoke` (6/6 — one earlier run failed all 6 on an apparent cold-start timing flake, unrelated to any code change; an immediate re-run passed clean) all verified. `git diff --check` clean (only line-ending warnings). HEAD and `origin/main` unchanged at `8f07289`. See `PRODUCT_ERROR_VISIBILITY_AUDIT.md` §A2.2c for the corrected trace. Nothing committed, pushed, deployed, or run against Supabase; no migration file created or run.)

Flagged this to E rather than guessing or faking the step.
  2. **E chose the fallback**: isolate the server-side push pipeline directly instead, since a second real account wasn't available this session. Called `POST /api/send-push` for real (same server code, same VAPID signing, same `webpush.sendNotification()` a real DM's `notify()` call would use) targeting the same subscribed test browser, using its own session token read from `localStorage` and used only within the same script (never printed/returned, consistent with how the credential-boundary rules in this environment work).
  3. **Result: `{"sent":true,"count":1,"removed":0}` -- the send succeeded with no error, and cross-checked the DB row against the browser's live subscription object directly (endpoint/p256dh/auth_key all matched, exactly one row, no stale duplicates).** This proves VAPID keys are configured correctly and FCM (the push service this subscription uses, per its endpoint) accepted the message for delivery.
  4. **But `ServiceWorkerRegistration.getNotifications()` came back empty immediately after, even with the tab in the foreground and a 2.5s wait.** To isolate whether this was a push-specific problem or something broader, called `registration.showNotification(...)` **directly, with no push involved at all** -- same result: no error thrown, but zero notifications ever actually appeared. **This proves the gap is not push delivery, VAPID, or Ergon's code at all -- this specific remote/automated Chrome instance cannot display OS-level notifications, full stop, even via the most basic local API call.** A screenshot taken right after sending also showed no visible OS toast, consistent with this (native notifications render outside the page content this session's screenshot tool captures, so that alone wasn't conclusive, but the `showNotification()` isolation test is).
  - **What this does and doesn't prove**: the entire path from a real trigger through to a push service successfully accepting a correctly-encrypted, correctly-addressed message is verified working. What's still genuinely unverified is the last hop -- an actual OS/browser notification appearing on a real device, tapping it opening the right conversation, and checking for duplicates -- because that requires a real device with a real notification manager, which this sandboxed session doesn't have access to. **This is an environment limitation of this session, not a known or suspected app defect.**
  - **One real gap this test did surface and fix (`api/send-push.js`)**: the per-subscription error handling only ever recorded 404/410 ("subscription gone, clean it up") and silently discarded every other failure -- a wrong VAPID key, a malformed payload, or any other push-service error would have reported the same success-shaped response with zero diagnostic information, i.e. the identical silent-failure shape this whole task started with, just moved server-side. Fixed: every failed send is now captured with its real status code/message in a new `failures` array on the response, so a future "push isn't arriving" report is actually debuggable instead of a black box. This did not cause today's finding (the one real send succeeded), but it would have hidden the real cause if it hadn't.
