## Standing rules (always in force)
- **Do not replace, reorder, or automate an existing business process without discussing the proposed change first.** Before a material process change, document the current workflow, affected roles, exceptions, approvals, downstream effects, proposed workflow, and tradeoffs; then record the approved decision. Small usability improvements may proceed only when they do not change responsibility, approvals, financial controls, data ownership, or operational sequence. See `PRODUCT_PLAN.md` and `PRODUCT_START_PLAN.md`.
- **Treat Ergon as a sellable, configurable product and apply product readiness alongside mobile readiness to all new or redesigned work.** Routine company setup, branding, catalogs, templates, forms, roles, module access, workflow settings, and notifications should not require code. Do not assume the existing singleton branding row or `default` workspace makes the app multi-tenant: a second customer company must not be onboarded until workspace membership, database/API/storage isolation, provisioning, configuration versioning, and migration behavior are designed and verified. Product-account subscription billing (plans/cards/seat billing for use of Ergon) is explicitly deferred; operational Billing, deposits, Client Ledger, and SaaS services sold by an Ergon customer remain in scope. See the productization sections in `PRODUCT_PLAN.md` and `PRODUCT_START_PLAN.md`.
- Build things completely and correctly. Flag genuinely ambiguous requests instead of guessing — ask via AskUserQuestion before starting large/ambiguous features, not after.
- Fix small/quick issues immediately instead of deferring them.
- **Never leave a work-log entry as `(pending commit)` after the commit actually happens.** Confirmed 2026-08-25 as a standing requirement, after 36 entries had piled up needing a dedicated cleanup pass (E: "make a note to always keep the doc updated as we make changes, so that doesn't happen again" — this was real double-work E had to ask for once already). The work-log entry necessarily gets written *before* the code commit exists (it's part of the same commit, describing what that commit does), so it naturally goes in with a `(pending commit...)` placeholder -- the fix is a mandatory second step, same turn, not deferred: after the code commit succeeds, take the real short hash `git commit` prints, `Edit` HANDOFF.md's just-added entry to replace `(pending commit...)` with `` (`<hash>`, ...) ``, and push a small HANDOFF-only follow-up commit (exactly the pattern already used for "Mark migration NNN confirmed run" commits elsewhere in this doc -- never `git commit --amend`/force-push on something already pushed). Do this immediately after every commit, not batched into a later cleanup pass.
- **Mobile use is a core requirement, not a follow-up pass, for anything new or redesigned.** E (2026-08-22): "it should be a core function to make sure anything new or redesign keep mobile use in mind." Before calling any new feature or reworked page done, check it against the patterns below (don't wait for a dedicated "mobile rollout" batch to catch it later): wrap any new `<table>` in `.table-scroll` or give it `.stack-table-mobile` with `data-label`s (rule below), avoid fixed-width multi-column grids without a mobile 1fr override, avoid flex rows with unbounded text and no `min-width: 0`, and prefer reusing already-audited shared classes (`.compact-edit-section`, `.tag-picker-grid`, `.site-gallery-grid`, `.mobile-card-list`, etc.) over inventing new layout from scratch. See the "Mobile table -> card-list rollout" section below for the full list of what's already been checked and fixed.
- Before every commit/push: `npx tsc -b` (clean), `npx vite build` (clean), and check CSS brace balance if `styles.css` was touched.
- No local dev environment exists for the user — everything happens in this sandbox. Git push needs a credential helper pointed at the PAT stored in `.git-credentials-ergon` in the project root:
  `git config credential.helper "store --file='$(pwd)/.git-credentials-ergon'"` (must single-quote the path — it contains a space).
- **Any new migration that creates a table meant to be re-acquired/reused by key (locks, dedupe-by-key patterns, etc.) must use an atomic upsert-reclaim RPC, not a plain `INSERT`.** See "Transaction lock gotcha" below — this exact mistake caused every second-ever save on a given key to silently fail for an unknown length of time.
- Any function/handler that creates a new row a user will look back on later (inventory movements, audit-style records) should stamp who did it (`authSession.email`) at creation time. See `recordMovements()` in `Inventory` state for the established pattern — don't add a new inventory-movement-creating call site without routing it through that helper.
- **Any new "delete" action on something worth keeping a record of must soft-delete (`deleted_by_email`/`deleted_at` PATCH) and write a `deletion_log` row, never a plain DB `DELETE`.** Confirmed 2026-08-22 as a site-wide requirement (E: "Any deleted items are tracked and logged... Site Wide"), built as migration 088 across 15 entities. When adding a new deletable entity: give its table the same two columns, filter it out of normal loaders (`&deleted_at=is.null`, or the embedded-filter form for nested resources — see `PROJECT_LOCATIONS_DELETED_AT_FILTERS`/`SALES_QUOTE_DELETED_AT_FILTERS` in `persistence.ts`), and call `logDeletionEvent` from the delete/restore function itself so it can never be forgotten at the call site. If the entity stores a file in Storage, do **not** call `deleteStorageObject` on delete — leave the file in place so a restore isn't just an empty pointer (see `deleteProjectLocationImage`/`deleteSalesQuoteLocationImage`/`deletePurchaseOrderFile`). Restore UI is optional (add it if the entity is high-value enough to justify one) but the soft-delete + log is not. **Exception:** if the entity already has its own retire/archive flag as the deliberate "hide but keep for history" path (e.g. `equipment_types.is_retired`), Delete can stay a real hard delete instead of adding a second, redundant soft-delete layer — just still log it to `deletion_log`. See `deleteEquipmentType` (Device Recipes) for the reference implementation of this exception, including the friendly error when a FK with no `ON DELETE` clause blocks the delete.
- **Any raw `<table>` on a page reachable on mobile must be wrapped in a `.table-scroll` div** (`overflow-x: auto`). `html`/`body` now have `overflow-x: hidden` (2026-08-17 fix, see work log) specifically so a wide unwrapped table can't push the whole page wider than the viewport again — an unwrapped table doesn't just get its own horizontal scrollbar, it corrupts where `position: fixed` elements (the mobile bottom nav) render on that page. If you add a new data table, wrap it from the start.
- **When a feature ported from VLTD doesn't behave the same in Ergon, diff against VLTD's actual working config/code before inventing a theory.** Wasted a round-trip guessing "DevTools emulation is flaky" for the install banner not showing when the real answer (a manifest icon below Chrome's 192px minimum) was one `diff` against VLTD's `manifest.json` away. VLTD is right there on disk (`C:\Users\EK\VLTD`) -- check it first.
- **When E says "do the whole site"/"every page" and gives a standing go-ahead to keep working without checking in, do the whole thing — don't silently narrow it to a curated "high value" subset.** Converting only 3 "high-traffic" tables to mobile cards and quietly skipping ~18 admin/back-office ones (on the assumption they're desktop-only) cost E an hour: they came back expecting the full rollout done, found gaps, and had to spend time re-discovering and re-reporting what was missed instead of that hour being productive. If full bespoke treatment for every item is too slow to do in one pass, ship a lighter universal fallback for the rest in the same batch (see `.stack-table-mobile`) rather than leaving some silently undone — a complete-but-less-polished pass beats a polished-but-partial one when the user has explicitly asked for "everything" and stepped away.
- **Before declaring a CSS fix "sitewide" or "done," check whether another rule elsewhere in `styles.css` already targets the same class.** The `.metric-grid` mobile fix (tightened stat tiles, 2026-08-18) was correct in isolation but got silently overridden the whole time by a separate, pre-existing `@media (max-width: 760px)` block further down the file that also listed `.metric-grid` and forced it back to 1 column -- later rules win ties in the CSS cascade, and E had to point out the fix visibly wasn't working before this was caught. `grep -n "\.classname"` across the whole stylesheet before trusting a fix, not just the block you just wrote.
- **`.stack-table-mobile` hiding `<thead>` is only safe for self-descriptive text cells (names, emails, statuses).** A bare checkbox or a lone number means nothing once separated from its column header -- Notification Rules' channel-toggle checkboxes were completely ambiguous with the header gone (E: "wasted space... not tightening up and being readable"). Use the `data-label="<same text as the <th>>"` + `::before { content: attr(data-label) }` pattern (`styles.css`, in the `.stack-table-mobile` mobile block) on any `<td>` whose value needs the column context restored -- see Notification Rules, the Form Builder's Required checkbox, Project BOM, Site Hardware/Pre-Sales Rules, Standard Install Times, Schedule Template phases, and Catalog Price Change Requests for the pattern. When adding a new `stack-table-mobile` table, default to adding `data-label` on every non-obvious cell rather than assuming it'll be fine.
- **A Postgres column added as "a direct FK" in a comment is not actually a FK** unless the DDL has `references <table>(id)` on it. `inventory_movements.build_transaction_id` (migration 021) was silently a bare `uuid` for a long time because of exactly this — see migration 075. When adding a column meant to be embedded via PostgREST (`alias:table(cols)` select syntax), double-check the constraint actually exists, not just the comment claiming it does.
- **Row-action convention for any table/list, confirmed 2026-08-22:** long/wide rows never get an explicit "Edit" button — the row itself is the click target (`.clickable-row` on desktop `<tr>`/`<div>`, `role="button" tabIndex={0} onClick/onKeyDown` on a mobile card that also contains real interactive children, e.g. Inventory's card at `main.tsx` ~7638) that opens the edit view/modal. Any select/input/checkbox left directly on the row needs `onClick={(e) => e.stopPropagation()}` so it doesn't also fire the row's edit-open. **Delete/Remove is always a trash-can icon** (`<Trash2/>` in an `.icon-button`/`.icon-button compact-remove`), never button text -- E: "it very simple to me but some are this way and some aren't." Fixed sitewide in the same pass: Tasks (List rows, mobile cards, Board cards), Project BOM lines, and every standalone "Remove" button (PO paperwork, form builder fields, schedule template phases, pre-sales rules, site hardware rules, task hardware dependencies, purchase draft lines) -- see work log.
- **Tasks specifically: Delete lives only inside the Edit modal, not on the row/card at all** (`TaskEditorModal`'s footer, trash icon next to Close/Reopen Task) -- E: "On the task rows, Delete only lives in Edit." This only moved *where* the button is; Task deletion was already fully tracked before and after this change (soft delete stamping `deletedByEmail`/`deletedAt`, restorable from the Deleted Tasks panel, logged to the task activity log) -- see the site-wide deletion-tracking audit below before assuming any *other* entity has the same protection.
- **Any DELETE or PATCH that represents a single-record user action (delete, soft-delete, restore, retire, cancel, hold/resume, approve/reject, status change) must confirm a row was actually affected before treating it as a success.** `response.ok` is not enough -- PostgREST returns 200/204 for a write that an RLS policy silently blocked (0 rows changed) exactly the same as one that changed a real row. Add `Prefer: return=representation` to the request and check the returned array is non-empty before calling `logDeletionEvent` or updating local state; see `deleteEquipmentType` (persistence.ts) for the reference shape. Confirmed 2026-08-24 as a site-wide requirement (E: "we cant have local memory on these type of issues") after this exact gap made Delete Equipment Type silently no-op -- see the audit work-log entry for the full list of ~21 functions fixed in the same pass. Any *new* delete/status-change function must follow this pattern from the start, not get audited in later.
- **A failure needs a channel a logged-in user will actually see -- `setAuthStatus` is not one.** `authStatus` only renders on the signed-out login screen (`.auth-gate-status`) and, weakly, as a hover tooltip on the top-nav sync pill when paired with `setSyncStatus("error")`. Calling `setAuthStatus` alone from a handler that runs while logged in is a silent no-op to the user. Use `window.alert(error)` for one-off actions with no existing status surface, or an entity's own already-rendered local status state (e.g. `taskStatusMessage`, `inviteStatus`, `catalogStatus`) where one exists -- don't invent a new state variable without also rendering it somewhere.
- **Every new `api/*.js` serverless route must call `requireAuth(req, res)` (from `api/_lib/requireAuth.js`) as its first line, before touching `req.body`.** Confirmed 2026-08-27: all 7 existing routes (`send-invite-email`, `send-notification-email`, `send-notification-slack`, `send-proposal-email`, `send-submittal-email`, `send-push`, `sales-quote-extract`) were built and shipped with zero auth checks -- fully public POST endpoints on a public GitHub repo, discovered during a security review E asked for after a real incident on the VLTD sister project. Worst case found: `send-invite-email` let anyone on the internet send an email with an attacker-controlled link through this app's own trusted mailer, styled as a legitimate invite -- a phishing vector. The fix pattern (`requireAuth` verifies the caller's Supabase access token against `/auth/v1/user`, no new secrets needed) is now the required baseline for any route with no per-role logic; every client-side `fetch("/api/...")` call site must send `authorization: Bearer <accessToken>`. If a route ever needs stricter (e.g. admin-only) enforcement, add a role check on top of `requireAuth`, don't replace it. See the full work-log entry below for the complete list of what was checked and confirmed clean in the same pass (git history, `.env` handling, storage buckets, old RLS policies).

## Questions / Decisions Needed (added 2026-09-06)

Every unresolved product/permission question found during this session's security and mobile-shell work, with the default actually shipped so behavior is never silently ambiguous. Where a question was fully resolved this session, it's marked **Decided** with what shipped instead of left open.

1. **Manager role's mobile bottom-nav center action — Decided.**
   - **Problem**: it was set to `{ view: "dashboard", label: "Dashboard" }`, identical to the adjacent "Dashboard" tab already in the nav — a real duplicate destination, one of two nav bugs this session was asked to fix.
   - **Shipped default**: changed to `{ view: "tasks", label: "Tasks" }` (`ROLE_QUICK_ACTION.manager`, `main.tsx`). Manager already has a `tasks` tab in its allowed-tabs list, and Tasks is the one high-frequency destination not already covered by the two flanking nav slots.
   - **Why it matters**: the center action is the most prominent, thumb-reachable button in the mobile shell — pointing it at a real destination instead of a duplicate is the whole point of the button existing.
   - **Affected**: `MobileBottomNav` component, manager role only (every other role's quick action was already unique).
   - **Migration needed to change later?** No — it's a pure frontend constant (`ROLE_QUICK_ACTION`), any role's quick-action target can be changed with a one-line edit.

2. **Which roles may send user invitations (`api/send-invite-email.js`) — Decided.**
   - **Shipped default**: admin-only (`requireRole(req, res, user, [])` — an empty allowed-role-key list means only `app_admins` passes). Previously: any signed-in user, no role check at all.
   - **Recommended answer / reasoning**: inviting someone grants them access to the whole app — the same trust level as every other Admin > user-management action (approve, assign role, deactivate), which are already admin-only in the UI. Matching that on the server closes the gap between "hidden behind an admin-only button" and "actually enforced."
   - **Why it matters**: before this fix, any authenticated account (including a brand-new, not-yet-approved one) could invite anyone to the app with an attacker-controlled invite link, styled as a legitimate email from Ergon's own mailer — a phishing vector, not just an inconvenience.
   - **Affected**: `api/send-invite-email.js`; Admin's invite UI already only shows this action to admins, so no client-facing behavior change for legitimate use.
   - **Migration needed to change later?** No — `app_admins`/`app_user_roles` already exist; widening this to a specific non-admin role is a one-line change to the `requireRole(...)` call's allow-list.

3. **Which roles may trigger email/Slack/push notifications (`send-notification-email.js`, `send-notification-slack.js`, `send-push.js`) — Decided 2026-09-07, superseding the 2026-09-06 entry below.**
   - **2026-09-06 status (superseded)**: recipient validation only, no content/role tightening — a caller could still send arbitrary title/body to a real known recipient.
   - **Shipped default 2026-09-07**: no role gate (unchanged reasoning: matches how DMs/@mentions already work, any teammate can message/@mention any other) -- but the routes no longer accept `to`/`subject`/`body`/`slackUserId`/`title` from the request at all. They take only a `notificationId` and load the recipient/title/body themselves (service-role key) from the `notifications` row `main.tsx`'s `notify()` already created for the same event -- see `api/_lib/notificationLookup.js`. A caller can no longer send *anything they want* to a real recipient; delivery is always exactly what's on record for that event.
   - **Why it matters**: closes the "two independently-trusted payloads for one event" gap -- previously a client could write one (innocuous) thing to the audit-trail `notifications` table and push/email/Slack something completely different through the same request.
   - **Still open, deliberately not closed this pass (see item 9 below)**: `notifications`' own INSERT policy is `with check (true)` -- any authenticated user can still write a *fabricated* notification row (any recipient, any content) that these tightened routes will then faithfully deliver, since there's no `created_by`/relationship column to check it against. This pass closes "the delivery channel diverges from the record," not "the record itself can be fabricated." See item 9.
   - **Affected**: the three routes above, plus `main.tsx`'s `notify()` (now sends only `{notificationId}`).
   - **Migration needed to change later?** No for what shipped. Closing the remaining gap in item 9 would need one (a `created_by` column + tightened RLS, or moving notification creation server-side).

4. **May a signed-in user push-notify any user, or only through a validated application event — Decided (DMs), Open (everything else), 2026-09-07, superseding the 2026-09-06 entry below.**
   - **2026-09-06 status (superseded)**: recipient had to be a real known user, but the caller still fully chose the recipient (`userId`) and content directly -- true of every event type, DMs included.
   - **Shipped default 2026-09-07, direct-message push specifically**: fully closed as asked. `api/send-push.js` now has a dedicated `{ directMessageId }` mode: it reads the real `direct_messages` row with the **caller's own access token** (that table's RLS, migration 094, already restricts reads to conversation participants), confirms `sender_id === caller`, and derives the recipient from the conversation's *other* participant -- the caller cannot name an arbitrary recipient for a DM push even in principle anymore, only "whoever is actually on the other side of this specific message." Title/body are built server-side from the message's own content and the caller's own verified email, not from anything in the request body. `main.tsx`'s `handleSendDirectMessage` now passes the real message id through `notify(..., directMessageId)` instead of a client-resolved partner email.
   - **Still open, every non-DM event type (task assigned, mentioned, status changes, ...)**: these go through the `notificationId` path (item 3) -- content/recipient can no longer diverge from what's on record, but what's on record can still be freely authored by any signed-in user (see item 9). Moving these onto "only a validated server-side event can create the underlying record" is the deeper fix, not done this pass -- would mean reimplementing each event type's business logic (task assignment, @mention resolution, etc.) server-side instead of client-side, a materially larger and riskier change than fit in this session.
   - **Why it matters**: push notifications land on a physical device outside the app's own UI -- DMs were the highest-value, most concretely-scoped target (explicitly named), and are now fully closed; the residual risk for other event types is the same one already accepted for the in-app bell (item 9), not a new or larger one.
   - **Affected**: `api/send-push.js`, `main.tsx`'s `notify()`/`handleSendDirectMessage`.
   - **Migration needed to change later?** No for DMs (done). Closing the rest would need the same server-side-event-authoring change noted in item 9, no new schema by itself.

5. **Which roles may trigger paid AI sales-quote extraction (`api/sales-quote-extract.js`) — Decided.**
   - **Shipped default**: `sales`, `pm`, `manager` roles (plus admin). Previously: any signed-in user, no role check.
   - **Recommended answer / reasoning**: this route costs real money per call (an AI extraction API) and its output only makes sense in a sales/PM workflow — the three roles that actually touch sales quotes and projects.
   - **Why it matters**: an unrestricted paid endpoint reachable by any signed-in account (including warehouse/support/marketing roles with no reason to call it) is a real, metered cost-abuse surface, not just a permissions tidiness issue.
   - **Affected**: `api/sales-quote-extract.js`; the client-side quote-builder UI that calls it is already only reachable from Sales-adjacent views, so this mainly closes a direct-API-call gap rather than changing anyone's visible workflow.
   - **Migration needed to change later?** No — same one-line `requireRole()` allow-list change.

6. **Should the Dashboard's new "Needs Attention" panel show all-company exceptions, or only the signed-in user's own — Decided.**
   - **Shipped default**: all-company (every overdue project, held PO, overdue/urgent task, unread message count, etc. — not filtered to "assigned to me").
   - **Reasoning**: matches every other number already on the Dashboard (KPI row, Manager Focus, Recent Activity) — none of those are per-user filtered today, so a per-user-filtered "Needs Attention" panel would be the one inconsistent exception on the page, and this team is small enough (per the roadmap doc above, "E + one other person" scale as of the merge discussion) that all-company and "relevant to me" are nearly the same set most of the time.
   - **Why it matters**: as the team grows, an all-company panel could get noisy for a role that only cares about their own slice — worth revisiting once there's more than a couple of concurrent users per role.
   - **Affected**: `Dashboard` component's new `attentionItems` derivation, `main.tsx`.
   - **Migration needed to change later?** No — purely a client-side filter change (e.g. filtering tasks/projects by assignee email) once there's a real per-user ownership field to filter by consistently.

7. **Add to Home Screen: automatic vs. account-menu-only vs. both with cooldown — Already decided, not reopened this session.** The existing code (`InstallAppBanner`, `INSTALL_DISMISSED_KEY`/`INSTALL_COOLDOWN_MS`, dated 2026-08-18 in its own comment) already answers this: the banner always shows unless already installed or explicitly dismissed, with a 7-day cooldown after a dismissal. This session only fixed a **layout** bug in that banner (it could visually overlap the mobile bottom nav's raised center button by a few pixels — see the mobile-shell fix below) — the show/hide *policy* itself was not touched or reconsidered.

8. **What should happen when one data domain fails but the rest of the app remains usable — Decided.**
   - **Shipped default**: a failed critical loader (Inventory Movement Ledger, Project Documents) throws instead of silently returning an empty list; the App level catches it into a `criticalLoadErrors` registry, renders a visible inline error banner with a Retry button in place of that specific panel's content, and flips the top-nav sync pill into a "Sync issue (N)" degraded state (hover tooltip names which area). Every other panel/tab keeps working normally — a documents-load failure does not block Inventory, Projects, Messages, etc.
   - **Why it matters**: this was the literal example bug named in this task (`loadInventoryMovements` returning `[]` on failure looked identical to "no data yet," and 068/070's real production outage was invisible for days because of exactly this shape).
   - **Affected**: `loadInventoryMovements`/`loadProjectDocuments` (`persistence.ts`), `criticalLoadErrors` state + `DataLoadErrorBanner` component + sync-pill degraded state (`main.tsx`, `src/components/DataLoadErrorBanner.tsx`). Only these two loaders are wired into the shared pattern so far — see "Remaining risks / deferred work" below for what else should eventually use it (push-subscription status already has its own equivalent visible-error handling, see `handleSubscribeToPush`/`messagesStatus`).
   - **Migration needed to change later?** No — this is purely an error-handling/UI pattern, independent of schema.

9. **The `notifications` table's own INSERT policy being fully open (`with check (true)`) -- Decided/Closed 2026-09-08, superseding the 2026-09-07 entry below.**
   - **2026-09-07 status (superseded)**: any signed-in user could write a `notifications` row addressed to anyone with any content -- the delivery routes (item 3) stopped that content from diverging once written, but nothing stopped the row itself from being fabricated in the first place.
   - **Shipped 2026-09-08 (option (b) from the prior entry -- the deeper, complete fix, not just a `created_by` audit column)**: `main.tsx`'s old `notify()` (a direct client-token `POST notifications`) is gone, along with every one of its per-event wrapper helpers (`notifyRoleAssignment`, `notifyCurrentTaskAssignees`, `resolveMentionEmails`, `notifyPurchaseRequestStatusChanged`, `notifyBuildStageChanged`, `notifyLowStockReached`) and the `createNotification()` function in `persistence.ts` itself (deleted, not just unused -- there is no client-side fallback left that can write this table at all). The ONLY way to create a `notifications` row now is `POST /api/create-notification`, which takes just an `eventType` + a real entity id (or, for a DM, the real message id) and independently re-derives recipient(s) and content from real stored data for each of the 11 event types that have a client trigger -- see `api/_lib/notificationEvents.js` for the full per-event dispatch table, and the permission matrix and migration section below for what each one actually checks.
   - **Why this is the complete fix, not a partial one**: every event type either (a) re-checks the SAME role/relationship that already gates the real underlying write (purchasing role for purchase-request changes, warehouse for builds, manager for price-change review, the requester's own email for price-change requests, real `direct_messages`/`channel_messages` RLS for DMs and channel mentions), or (b) is authorized as "any signed-in user" specifically because the underlying action's own RLS is *itself* that open (tasks, canvas) -- this doesn't invent new restrictions beyond what the data model already enforces, it just makes sure the notification can never diverge from it. `task_overdue` (which never had any "acting user" concept at all -- any signed-in browser with tasks loaded could trigger it for anyone) is now a real scheduled job (`api/cron/task-overdue.js`, Vercel Cron, `CRON_SECRET`-gated, see the migration section below), not a per-user client check.
   - **The one real, accepted data-model limitation**: `build_stage_changed`'s "stage" was never a persisted column on `build_transactions` (it's client-side/planned-build state) -- so unlike every other event, its label can't be independently re-derived from a stored value. The route still requires the real `warehouse`/admin role AND a real existing build row, and constrains `stage` to a fixed 5-value enum (`planned`/`kitting`/`assembled`/`tested`/`complete`) -- not "arbitrary content," but also not fully re-derived the way everything else is. Flagging honestly rather than glossing over it.
   - **Why it matters**: this closes the last meaningfully-sized gap from the 2026-09-06/07 authorization passes -- a signed-in but malicious or compromised account can no longer fabricate a plausible-looking internal notification (wrong recipient, wrong event, wrong content) for ANY of the 11 client-triggered event types, and the two that were already fully server-side (`quote_proposal_responded`, `submittal_responded` -- real security-definer RPCs, untouched by this pass) were never affected.
   - **Affected**: `notifications` RLS (migration 114), `api/create-notification.js` (new), `api/_lib/notificationEvents.js` (new), `api/_lib/directMessage.js` (new, extracted from `send-push.js`), `api/cron/task-overdue.js` (new), `vercel.json` (new, cron schedule), `main.tsx`'s `triggerNotification()` (replaces `notify()` and every wrapper), `persistence.ts` (`createNotification()` deleted).
   - **Migration needed?** Yes -- `114_trusted_notification_creation.sql`. **✅ Run by E 2026-09-08 ("Success. No rows returned.") and live-verified immediately after -- see the second live-verification pass in the `f4b877f` work-log entry below (a direct authenticated-client INSERT attempt against production now returns a real `403` RLS rejection).** `notifications.created_by` now exists; the `authenticated` role has no INSERT policy on this table at all -- only the service-role key (used exclusively by `api/create-notification.js`) can write it. **This item is now fully closed, not just shipped-pending-migration.**

10. **`sales-quote-extract.js`'s role list: `pm` kept despite the recommended default explicitly saying sales/manager/admin only — Decided/Closed 2026-09-08, E confirmed.**
    - **The recommended default originally given for this task**: "Sales, Manager, and Admin roles."
    - **What shipped, and is now the confirmed final answer**: `sales`, `pm`, `manager` (+ admin) -- unchanged from before this session's work, and E has now explicitly signed off on keeping it this way rather than the literal recommended default: "Keep PM access to sales-quote-extract. This preserves the existing workflow."
    - **Why**: the upload button ("Build Sales BOM and Scope") lives on the **Projects** detail page, not the Sales section. `pm` has the `projects` tab in its default set and encounters this button in normal daily use; `sales` does **not** have the `projects` tab by default and would need a role override just to reach the page this feature lives on. Removing `pm`'s server-side access would have silently broken a currently-working flow for the role that actually uses it.
    - **Affected**: `api/sales-quote-extract.js`, its test file. No code change needed for this decision -- the role list was already correct, this just closes the open question.
    - **Migration needed to change later?** No.

11. **Rate limiting is best-effort and non-durable — Upgraded 2026-09-08, superseding the entry below. Code shipped; still needs one E-only infra step to actually activate.**
    - **2026-09-06/07 status (superseded)**: `api/_lib/rateLimit.js` was purely an in-memory sliding window keyed per caller -- real protection against a runaway script/retry loop hitting one warm instance, but a caller spread across cold starts or multiple regions could exceed it, since nothing was shared across instances.
    - **Shipped 2026-09-08**: `api/_lib/rateLimit.js` now backs onto Upstash Redis (the `@upstash/redis` REST client -- no persistent TCP connection to manage, the right fit for a function that may live for one request) whenever `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL`/`UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` are set, using `INCR`+`EXPIRE` fixed-window counters so the count is shared across every instance/region/cold start instead of living in one process's memory. Same public function signature (`checkRateLimit(key, maxCount, windowMs)`, now `async`) so every one of the 8 call sites needed only a one-line `await` added, not a rewrite. **Applied to all 8 routes that rate-limit today** -- E's explicit priority list (paid AI extraction, invitations, outbound email/Slack/push) plus `api/create-notification.js` for consistency (same helper, would otherwise be the one route silently left on the old mechanism): `sales-quote-extract.js` (paid OpenAI extraction), `send-invite-email.js`, `send-proposal-email.js`, `send-submittal-email.js`, `send-notification-email.js`, `send-notification-slack.js`, `send-push.js`, `create-notification.js`.
    - **Honest fallback, same pattern as every other optional integration in this app**: with no Redis connected (true today -- see "Action needed" below), `checkRateLimit` transparently keeps using the original in-memory limiter, unchanged -- zero behavior change until E connects a store. If Redis IS connected but a call to it errors (network blip, Upstash outage), that one call also falls back to the in-memory limiter for itself (logged, see item 6's logging-clarity precedent) rather than either blocking real traffic or silently applying no limit at all.
    - **The one accepted trade-off**: a fixed window (not a sliding one) -- a caller can get up to ~2x `maxCount` through across a window boundary. Not worth the extra Redis round-trips a true sliding window needs for the abuse pattern this actually guards against (a runaway script or retry loop, not a precisely-timed attacker).
    - **Action needed from E to actually activate this** (until then, behavior is identical to before -- not broken, just not yet durable): connect a Redis/KV store to this Vercel project (`Project > Storage > Connect Database`, e.g. the Upstash Marketplace integration) and set `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL`/`UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` as project env vars, then redeploy (same env-var-bakes-in-at-build-time caveat as `CRON_SECRET`). I did not create a database or click through the Marketplace integration myself -- provisioning shared infrastructure/billing is the same category of action `CRON_SECRET` was, E's call to make, not mine.
    - **Tests**: `tests/api/rateLimit.test.js` (new) -- the in-memory fallback still enforces its count and keys stay independent; the Redis path is verified against a mocked `@upstash/redis` client (INCR/EXPIRE called correctly, count enforced); a dedicated test proves the actual point of this upgrade -- two/three separate fresh module instances (simulating real separate cold-started Vercel functions, which never share process memory) sharing one mocked Redis backing store correctly see each other's counts, something the old in-memory-only version could never do; and a Redis-failure case proves it fails open to in-memory rather than blocking real traffic, with the failure logged and the Upstash token never appearing in that log.
    - **Affected**: `api/_lib/rateLimit.js`, all 8 call sites (one-line `await` each), `package.json`/`package-lock.json` (`@upstash/redis` added), `tests/api/rateLimit.test.js` (new).
    - **Migration needed?** No schema migration; needs the Vercel Storage connection + two env vars above, E-only.

12. **DM push notifications land on the Messages hub, not the specific conversation — Decided/Closed 2026-09-08, superseding the entry below.**
    - **2026-09-06/07 status (superseded)**: the DM push's `url` was `/#messages` -- an improvement over the prior generic `#dashboard` fallback, but not a deep link into the exact conversation.
    - **Shipped 2026-09-08**: `api/send-push.js`'s DM-mode `url` is now `/#messages/<conversationId>` -- the real `conversation_id` off the actual `direct_messages` row (via `api/_lib/directMessage.js`, which already loaded it and now also returns it), never anything the request body could name. `main.tsx` gained a route effect (right next to `selectConversation()`) that reads a `#messages/<id>` hash segment on mount and on `hashchange` and selects that conversation -- mirrors the existing `#projects/<slug>` route pattern (`handleProjectRoute`) rather than inventing a new URL scheme. The service worker (`public/sw.js`) needed no change at all -- its `notificationclick` handler already navigates to whatever `url` the push payload names; the only missing piece was ever naming the conversation.
    - **Authorization**: no new server-side check was needed or added. `resolveDirectMessage` already verifies the caller is the message's real sender (`direct_messages`' own RLS via the caller's token) before it will resolve a `conversationId` at all, so the deep-link value itself can only ever be a conversation the caller was genuinely part of. Client-side, opening a `#messages/<id>` link whose conversation the current viewer is NOT part of degrades safely to the existing "no conversation selected" state -- `activeConversation` is looked up in the `conversations` array, which `loadConversations` already scopes server-side (RLS) to the real signed-in participant; a bogus or foreign id just isn't found there, same as it already behaved for any other invalid id before this change. No new attack surface.
    - **Deduplication**: untouched, deliberately. The temptation was to also make the *notification row's* `related_entity_id` the conversation id (to match `related_entity_type: "conversation"`) instead of the message id it already stores -- rejected, because `api/create-notification.js`'s dedupe key is built from that same field (`${eventType}:${relatedEntityId}:${email}`); switching it to a per-conversation id would collapse every message in an ongoing conversation onto one shared dedupe key, so only the very first DM in a conversation would ever notify its recipient again. `send-push.js`'s DM-mode branch gets `conversationId` directly from `resolveDirectMessage`'s own return value instead, entirely independent of the notification row and its dedupe key -- zero change to dedupe behavior.
    - **Affected**: `api/_lib/directMessage.js` (returns `conversationId`), `api/send-push.js` (`resolveDirectMessagePush`'s `url`), `main.tsx` (new route effect near `selectConversation`), `public/sw.js` (unchanged -- confirmed no change needed).
    - **Migration needed?** No.

## App-wide layout convention (confirmed standard, 2026-08-11, Project detail page updated 2026-08-14)
Every page follows: **Header (title/subtitle) → Stats/metric-grid row → Tasks mini-panel for that tab → everything else.**
Reference/confirmed-correct example: Sales page (`SalesHome`).

Status per page as of this handoff:
- Sales — correct (metric-grid → Sales Tasks → Product Catalog → Site Builder).
- Purchasing/Procurement — correct (metric-grid → Procurement Tasks → rest).
- Projects (list mode) — correct (metric-grid before Projects Tasks).
- **Projects (detail mode) — reworked 2026-08-14/15, layout convention above no longer literally applies here.** New shape, top to bottom:
  1. Top row: **Project Information** card (renamed from "Site Information" 2026-08-15 — click the card or its edit icon to open the "Project Details" editable form — name/client/type/owner/address/status/due/package/notes — in a modal, same `modal-panel-wide` pattern as the tile grid below), **Project Snapshot** card, **Project Progress** card (Build Sales BOM and Project Documents used to live in this row — they're tiles now, see below).
  2. Project Tasks mini-panel.
  3. **`ProjectLocationsSection` (Locations), always visible, full table -- not a tile.** Un-tiled 2026-08-22/23 (E: "I'd like the Locations return to full line items like it is in the Sales Tab, they should mimic each other") -- Sales never tiled its equivalent Site Builder location table, so this un-does the one place Locations had drifted from that parity. Same component, same props, just rendered directly on the page instead of behind `showLocationsModal`.
  4. `project-tile-grid` at the **very bottom** of the page (moved there 2026-08-14 per E's request — "page scrolls forever, tighten it up"): 9 clickable stat tiles — Build Sales BOM, Project Documents, SOW, BOM, Shipping, **Images** (was Locations until 2026-08-22/23 -- see below), Submittals, After-Sales Handover, SaaS. Each shows 2-4 quick stats; clicking opens the real section in a modal (`showXModal` state + `{showXModal && (<div className="modal-backdrop">...)}`, unchanged content, just wrapped). New sections needing the same treatment should follow this pattern: tile in the grid + modal wrapper elsewhere in the render (modal position in JSX doesn't matter, they're `position: fixed`).
  - **Images tile (new 2026-08-22/23)** took the vacated Locations slot -- every photo across every garage/lot on this one project (`ProjectImagesModal`), split into two folders: **PM** and **Sales** (origin tag from migration 087/088), PM listed first, both always shown even at 0 count -- E was explicit neither folder should ever be hidden. Each folder expands into a flat grid (not sub-grouped by location -- E confirmed this over the per-location-grouped alternative), captioned by location name, with the same click-to-expand lightbox/multi-select/download interaction as Marketing's gallery (2026-08-21).
  - **"Project Transfers"** (cards for every OTHER project, shown at the bottom of every single project's detail page) was **removed entirely** 2026-08-14 — it never belonged there; Reports' "Project Allocation History" already covers this properly.
- Inventory — has a metric-grid (Total SKUs, Low Stock, Inventory Value, Planned Builds) before Inventory Tasks.
- Dashboard, Reports, Admin, Library, Tasks board, **SaaS Calendar (new)** — no tasks/metrics concept applies (self-contained pages); left as-is, no deviation.

Project detail page header: the App-level `<h1>`/`<p>` shows `Project: <Name>` / `Ref <ref>` when a specific project is open, instead of the generic "Projects" title+subtitle. Wired via `Projects`'s `onDetailContextChange` prop → App-level `projectDetailContext` state.

## Procurement + Inventory nav merge (decided and built 2026-08-19)
E discussed this architecture with a separate chat first (pasted the full conversation in), then we worked through it together. Decision, in E's own words and mine, for continuity if a session picks this up mid-build:
- **Merge Procurement and Inventory into one nav entry** ("Inventory & Purchasing" or similar -- exact label TBD), sub-tabs inside instead of two separate top-level tabs. E: team is small (E + one other person), doesn't want more places to look. This reverses my initial recommendation to keep them separate -- E's actual constraint (small team wants fewer places to look, not more) outweighed the "avoid nav disruption" concern once stated.
- **Sub-tab structure**: Stock (today's Inventory page body, basically unchanged), Purchasing (today's Procurement page body, basically unchanged), Vendors (**new** -- doesn't exist yet, PO vendor is just a free-text name today, no real directory).
- **Shipments stay split, not unified.** Two genuinely different flows: inbound (vendor -> us, tracked via PO status Ordered/Received, lives in Purchasing) and outbound (us -> project site, tracked via each Project's own Shipping tab, Requested/Packed/Shipped). E confirmed keep them where they are -- explicitly said they'd "come across this when testing" if merged wrong, i.e. don't force a unified view that doesn't match how the two flows actually work.
- **Permission gating survives the merge, not optional.** `inventory` and `purchasing` are separate grants today (`DEFAULT_TABS_BY_ROLE`, main.tsx ~360) -- e.g. `warehouse` role has `inventory` but not `purchasing` (can count/receive stock, can't spend money); `pm` role same split (can check stock, can't independently cut a PO). This is deliberate access control, not accidental overlap, and it must keep working at the sub-tab/action level inside the merged view even though there's now only one nav entry. E: "we can add rules within" -- not urgent to perfect right now (just the two of them), but the mechanism needs to still be there, not ripped out.
- **The actual trigger for the merge conversation** was a workflow gap E caught, not the merge itself: Purchasing status changes (Ordered/Received/Cancelled) weren't syncing back onto the Project BOM line that spawned the request -- fixed first, see work log (`syncBomLineStatusFromRequest`), before starting the larger nav restructuring.

## Mobile table -> card-list rollout (in progress, 2026-08-18)
E: "the entire site should be optimized this way" (referring to the Product Catalog mobile card list) and gave a standing go-ahead to keep converting the rest of the wide desktop-style tables without checking in each time, as long as each one follows the same pattern -- just keep this doc updated per batch. **If you're picking this up mid-rollout, check the task list (TaskList tool) for the current in-progress/pending items**, then keep going with the pattern below.

**The pattern** (established on Product Catalog, `main.tsx` ~12251, and Site Builder, ~15242): below 760px, hide the existing `<table>` (wrapped in a `*-table-scroll` div, `display: none` in the same `@media (max-width: 760px)` block the table already had overrides in -- don't create a new duplicate media query, extend the existing one for that page) and show a parallel `.mobile-card-list` built from the *same* array/`.map()` the table uses, so there's no new data plumbing. Card building blocks are shared generic classes now (added 2026-08-18, `styles.css` ~5893): `.mobile-card-list` (container), `.mobile-card` (one card), `.mobile-card-row` (icon/title row), `.mobile-card-title`, `.mobile-card-meta` (small muted line), `.mobile-card-pills` (status badges), `.mobile-card-filters` (search/filter inputs pulled out of the table's `<thead>` filter row, since those were themselves only reachable via horizontal scroll -- always put filters at the top of the mobile list, not buried). Product Catalog and Site Builder predate the shared classes and use their own prefixed set (`.catalog-mobile-*`, `.site-builder-mobile-*`) -- don't bother retrofitting those, just use the shared classes for everything converted after Inventory.

**Nested-button gotcha:** if a row has real action buttons (Receive/Adjust, Delete, etc.), do NOT make the outer card a `<button>` -- HTML doesn't allow nesting interactive elements, and while React's direct-DOM rendering (not innerHTML parsing) means the browser mostly tolerates it, it's invalid markup and risky. Use `<div className="mobile-card" role="button" tabIndex={0} onClick={...} onKeyDown={(e) => (e.key === "Enter" || e.key === " ") && ...}>` instead, with real `<button>`s nested inside wrapped in a `<span onClick={(e) => e.stopPropagation()}>`. See Inventory's card (`main.tsx` ~7638) for the reference implementation. Site Builder's card (built one batch before this rule was written) does nest a delete `<button>` inside the outer `<button>` -- it works in practice but isn't the pattern to copy; worth fixing if touching that card again.

Status (updated after E called out that curating which tables got done, silently, wasted their time verifying -- see standing rule below):
- **Full bespoke mobile-card treatment (table + filters + click-to-edit, matching Product Catalog):** Product Catalog (Sales), Site Builder/Sales quotes list, Inventory items table, Purchase Orders table, Projects list table, Task Board list view (all groups).
- **Every other raw `<table>` in the app** (18 of them -- Team Roster, Notification Rules, Standard Install Times, Schedule Template phases, Pre-Sales Rules, Site Hardware Rules, Catalog Price Change Requests, User Directory, Pending Approvals, form schema builder, BOM/catalog import previews, Deleted Tasks panel, Project Locations, Sales Quote Locations, Project BOM, the two public submittal/proposal pages) **now has the generic `.stack-table-mobile` CSS class** (`styles.css`, added 2026-08-18 batch 2) -- header hides, each row becomes its own bordered block with cells stacked top-to-bottom. No JSX restructuring per table, so it covers all of them in one pass; less polished than the bespoke cards (no custom icons/field labels) but nothing is cut off or unreachable anymore. If a specific one of these gets enough real mobile use to justify it, upgrade it to a full card list the same way Product Catalog was done.
- **Also fixed:** `.roster-add-row` (Team Roster's invite form, Standard Install Times' and Schedule Templates' add-row) was missing from the mobile grid-stacking list other similar forms already used -- this was the actual cause of E's screenshot showing Category/Hours/Notes inputs cut off on the right. Added to the existing stacking list.
- **Audit correction (2026-08-18, later batch):** the "Reports/Purchasing already responsive" claim below was only partly right. `.report-table-row`/`.request-row` (the CSS-grid, non-`<table>` pattern) really were already fine. But `.report-filter-row` -- a *different*, easily-confused-for-`.request-filter-row` class, used in **10 places** (Reports panels, Product Catalog toolbar, Purchasing, Task board, Project Locations gallery, Admin) -- was missed in that same audit and had a real ~754px minimum-width overflow. Also fixed `.manufactured-inventory-head`/`-row` (Inventory's Manufactured Equipment list), found the same way. **Lesson: re-verify "already responsive" claims by grepping the stylesheet for the exact class in use, not by name-similarity to a class you already checked.**
- **Fixed (2026-08-18, final batch, `c5dc3c0`):** Reports' 6 tabs, Purchasing's Request Queue, Shipping, and SaaS Calendar's `.report-table-row`/`.request-row`/`.report-table-head`/`.request-queue-head` pattern (CSS grid, not `<table>`) had the same orphaned-header problem `.stack-table-mobile` had before its `data-label` fix -- the head's labels stacked into their own block on mobile, disconnected from the row values. Same fix applied: `data-label` on every non-self-evident `<span>` (11 report-table head/row pairs + the request queue), head divs hidden on mobile since they're now redundant. E explicitly asked for this not to be left as a flagged gap.
- **Fixed (2026-08-18, final batch, `c5dc3c0`):** Task Board's Calendar view (`TaskCalendar`, `main.tsx` ~16858) -- a 7-column month grid genuinely has no room for task-title chips at phone width, so "just stack to 1 column" (the fix for form rows) doesn't apply here. Real fix instead: on mobile, day cells become tap targets (day number + a small count badge, chips hidden), and tapping a day shows that day's tasks in a proper agenda list below the grid (new `selectedDay` state, defaults to today if the current month is shown). Desktop keeps the original full grid with inline chips, completely unchanged -- the agenda section only renders under 640px.
- **Fixed (2026-08-18, audit batch, `0f9229d`/`90ade46`):** after E pushed for a real "is it actually all done" answer, ran a systematic audit beyond tables -- every unconditional (non-media-query) CSS rule in `styles.css` with a fixed `min-width`/`width` >=200px, every multi-column `grid-template-columns` not already in the mobile-stacking list, and every `white-space: nowrap` without a paired truncation. Found and fixed two more real bugs this way: **`.account-menu-panel`** (Role view/Admin/sign-out dropdown) had the exact same shape as the notification-panel bug from earlier in this session -- fixed 260px width, `position: absolute` anchored to a header trigger, no mobile override -- same fix applied (`position: fixed`, viewport-relative insets). **`.camera-capture-name-prefix`** (the "<Garage/Lot name> - " filename prefix in the camera modal) is unbounded user-entered text with `flex-shrink: 0` -- a long garage/lot name could genuinely overflow a narrow modal; now truncates with an ellipsis. Also made **Task Gantt's** label column sticky (it already scrolled horizontally safely, but the task name scrolled away with it, so a bar positioned later in the timeline couldn't be identified). Everything else checked out already safe (contained in a scroll container, `max-width` not `min-width`, `flex-wrap: wrap`, or short fixed-vocabulary text) -- see the commit messages for the specific list.
- **What "done" means as of this audit:** every literal `<table>`, every `.report-table-row`/`.request-row`-style CSS-grid row, the stat-tile grid, Task Calendar, Task Gantt, and every CSS-detectable fixed-width/nowrap overflow risk in the stylesheet. **What it does not mean:** this was a code-level audit (grep + reasoning), not visual verification -- there is no login access to the live app from this sandbox, so nothing was confirmed by actually looking at a rendered phone screen. If E finds something after this, it's real signal, not a sign the audit was skipped.
- **Literal modal-by-modal audit completed (2026-08-18, follow-up batch).** E specifically asked "did you do the full modal-by-modal audit?" and the honest answer at the time was no -- the CSS-pattern scripts above check selector-level rules across the whole stylesheet, but hadn't walked each of the ~35 `.modal-panel` instances' actual JSX one by one. Did that walkthrough (every modal from `receive-request-panel` through `task-editor-modal`, full line list was in the grep of `className="modal-panel`) and it caught several real bugs the pattern scripts missed, because they're a different bug shape (flex rows with unbounded text, not grid columns):
  - **`.location-line-fields`** (the 4-column Location/Item+Qty/Accessory/Qty row inside `LocationItemLineSection`, used by every Camera/Sign/Sensor/Misc line on both the Project and Sales Quote location-detail modals) had a hard `minmax(110px,...)  minmax(150px,...) minmax(150px,...) 80px` grid -- ~520px minimum width, never overridden on mobile. This is exactly the class of bug the earlier automated grid-scan script was supposed to catch and didn't (it's a real gap in that script, not just an unlucky miss). Added to the mobile 1fr-stacking list.
  - **`.line-item div`** (the shared base row used by BOM lines, shipment lines, price history, and more) had no `min-width: 0`, so a long item/product name couldn't shrink below its own content width in the flex row and would push past the card edge instead of wrapping. This is a shared base class, so the one CSS fix (`min-width: 0`) covers every place `.line-item` is used, not just the one modal it was found in.
  - Two catalog/BOM import preview tables (`bom-import-preview`, catalog's `importRows` preview) used `.stack-table-mobile` but were missing `data-label` on their `<td>`s -- same orphaned-header bug fixed elsewhere, just two instances that got skipped in the earlier table pass.
  - Minor: `.build-confirm-list`, `.quote-hardware-summary-list`, `.camera-capture-name-row`/`.camera-capture-review-description`, and `.task-modal-footer-left` all had the same "flex row, no min-width:0/no flex-wrap" shape with genuinely variable-length text (item names, emails, "Closed by X on Y" timestamps) -- fixed with truncation or wrap as appropriate per what the text needs (short pill-like values got ellipsis truncation, important full item names got wrap instead of truncation).
  - Everything else in every modal (BOM/receive/adjust/build-confirm, device builder, work order, image previews, catalog item/import/proposal, camera capture, file/location-files/site-gallery, shipment new/detail, project & sales-quote location detail, new-quote/edit-site/site-intake, task editor) checked out already safe -- reused already-audited shared classes (`.bom-modal-grid`, `.form-grid`, `.sow-grid`, `.tag-picker-grid`, `.submittal-*`, `.source-file`, `.site-gallery-grid`, etc.) or had proper `flex-wrap`/mobile overrides already in place.
  - **Lesson for future audits:** grid-based overflow (multi-column `grid-template-columns`) and flex-based overflow (a row with unbounded text and no `min-width: 0`) are two different bug shapes needing two different automated checks -- a script that only scans for `grid-template-columns` will systematically miss the flex-row case. If writing another automated sweep, add a check for `display: flex` rules whose children include a plain (unstyled-for-shrink) text element next to a fixed-width sibling.
- Desktop is untouched throughout -- everything is inside `@media (max-width: 760px)` blocks, nothing removed or restructured for desktop users.
- **Resolved, false alarm:** E's "boxes missing from the bottom of the Project page" turned out to be a mixup between the Projects *list* page (no tile grid there, never had one) and the Project *detail* page's tile grid (still there, working) -- not a bug, no fix needed.
- **New: Portfolio Budget & Schedule panel**, Projects list page (`ProjectPortfolioHealth` component, `main.tsx`, right before `function Projects`) -- E asked what to do with the "extra room" on that page once the missing-boxes question resolved; proposed a mockup (published as a Claude Artifact, sketch only, placeholder data), E approved, then it got built for real. Two blocks: **Budget health** (Purchase Order spend per `project.ref`, new `purchaseOrders` prop threaded into `Projects`, vs `project.allocated`, target tick + green/amber/red) and **Schedule health** (`projectCompletion()`, moved to module scope so this component can share it, vs target date). See the data-model note below on `ProjectSite.due` -- the schedule side has to degrade gracefully because that field isn't a real date.

## Long-term roadmap: Ergon as the Slack/ClickUp/Drive replacement (agreed 2026-08-30, not started)

E's stated direction, from an overnight planning conversation (research → clarifying questions → design proposal → agreement, no code written for any of this yet): "we need 3 different apps and the info is spread out between Hubspot, Google Drive, ClickUp and Slack. I would like the ClickUp and Slack features with using google drive as a backup for items and not have to jump through all of these apps... eventually eliminate that app all together." HubSpot is explicitly deferred ("ignore for now" — eventual two-way sync for billing/project status is the only piece E wants from it, later). This is a multi-week initiative E already expects to take real time ("this may take weeks to build and test") — treat it as a standing roadmap to work through in phases, not a single feature request.

**The agreed design** (confirmed by E after the plan was played back):

- **Channels are one underlying concept**, surfaced in three places, not three separate systems:
  1. **Section channels** — one fixed, permanent "Discussion" tab per major app section (Sales, Purchasing, Marketing, Inventory, etc.), added to the existing sub-tab bar pattern already used in Inventory & Purchasing (Stock/Purchasing/Vendors + new Discussion tab); for single-view pages, becomes a 2-tab [Main | Discussion] layout. Singletons — always exist, never created/deleted by users.
  2. **Project channels** — every Project auto-gets one, tied to `project_id`. **Never dies.** Closing a project does not archive-and-lose its channel; the channel and its full history move with the project into wherever it lives afterward (the Client's ledger).
  3. **Client channels** — see the Clients section below. The client's own ongoing channel, plus a rolled-up view into every project channel that ever ran under that client.
  - All three share the same underlying table/shape (a channel optionally linked to a real entity, or to nobody for a real 1:1 DM — DMs already exist, migration 094).
  - **Auto-membership, not manual invites** — matches existing role/tab-access rules (whoever can already see a Project/section can see its channel) rather than a new per-channel membership system to administer.
  - The standalone Messages hub (exists today, DMs only) gets rebuilt into a real Slack-style sidebar: DMs, Projects, Sections grouped separately, each a view into the same underlying channel data the embedded tabs use.

- **Clients become a real entity — this is required, not optional, for the above to work.** Confirmed by checking the actual code: today's Client Ledger (`ClientLedger` component, main.tsx ~12922) is entirely per-*project* — `ledgerInfo`/`installedAssets` key off `project_id`, and `project.client` is just a repeated text field with zero deduplication. Two projects for the same real client ("Newport News Shipbuilding" appears on more than one project today) show as unrelated rows. To get E's actual ask ("when a project gets closed, that whole section goes under the Client as one large combined section") this needs:
  1. A real `clients` table.
  2. Existing `project.client` text values matched/linked to a real Client — auto-linked where unambiguous, but **needs a short manual reconciliation pass with E** for anything that doesn't match cleanly (e.g. is "Newport News" the same client as "Newport News Shipbuilding" and "Newport News 37th St.", or three different things?) — do not auto-guess this, it's exactly the kind of silent-wrong-merge that's expensive to untangle later. Show E the actual distinct client strings in the live data and let them confirm groupings in one pass before anything is merged.
  3. Client Ledger becomes a client-grouped view (one entry per Client, every project/site that ever ran for them rolled up underneath) instead of today's flat per-project list.
  4. The Client gets the same Discussion-tab pattern as everywhere else.

- **Search**: extend the existing global search (main.tsx ~5381, today: client-side substring match, 3 entity types only — inventory parts/POs/projects, shown as a dropdown) rather than replace it. Researched how Notion's is built/why it's well-regarded (their own writeups: workspaces under ~1,000 docs — Ergon's scale — skip vector/embedding search entirely and lean on ranking + UX):
  - Postgres full-text search (`tsvector`/`ts_rank`) across a much wider set of entities: add Tasks, Messages/channels (including closed/archived ones -- this is the actual fix for "old chats are hard to find," not the channel-merging above, which fixes *where things live* rather than *finding them regardless*), Vendors, Sales Quotes, Documents.
  - No vector DB / embeddings needed at this data volume — that would be over-building for the size of this workspace.
  - Keep the fast dropdown for quick jumps; add a real full-page results view (filterable by entity type, scopable to "just chats" vs "everything," per E's ask) for anything bigger than a few hits.
  - Rank by recency + exact-title-match boost as a first pass; usage-frequency boosting and an AI Q&A layer on top are legitimate later add-ons, not part of the initial build.

- **Google Drive**: auto-backup only (confirmed, not browse/import) — every file uploaded to Ergon (photos, documents, message attachments) also gets copied to a Drive folder automatically. Not a two-way sync, not a way to pull *existing* Drive files into Ergon.

- **No history import from Slack/ClickUp/Drive** — confirmed explicitly ("let's do 1 [fresh start] to make sure it all works... I don't want old stuff in there yet"). New activity lives in Ergon going forward; old conversations stay in Slack/ClickUp if E ever needs to look back. This alone rules out a large chunk of otherwise-plausible scope (Slack/ClickUp API integrations for pulling history) — don't build toward it unless E explicitly asks later.

- **HubSpot**: explicitly out of scope for all of the above. The only piece E wants eventually is two-way sync for **billing and project status markers** — flagged for its own separate scoping conversation later, not bundled into this initiative.

**Proposed phase order** (not yet started as of 2026-08-30; confirm with E before beginning each phase, given the multi-week scope):
1. Section Discussion tabs + Project Discussion tabs (channels tied to what already exists structurally — no new entities needed yet)
2. Standalone Messages hub rebuilt as a real channel sidebar (DMs / Projects / Sections), reusing #1's data
3. Search extended per above (dropdown + full-page, wider entity coverage, includes closed/archived channels)
4. Clients as a real entity (with the manual reconciliation pass) + per-client channels + Client Ledger becomes client-grouped
5. (Later, unscoped) Google Drive auto-backup; HubSpot two-way sync for billing/status — each needs its own scoping pass when E is ready

  **Fixed locally and kept, after correction** (small, mechanical, no schema/workflow effect — every one follows the exact `.ok` + row-count + plain-message + real-detail-in-`console.error` pattern already established and deployed in `4ea2fd1`): the pre-existing `saveInventoryItems` item-metadata row-count check tightened from `<` to `!==` (confirmed safe — a single-table `on_conflict=sku` upsert cannot legitimately return more rows than sent); `saveDeviceRecipes` (every lookup/PATCH/POST/DELETE checked, **plus a same-day correction**: a preflight now rejects the entire save before any write if a component name resolves to zero or more than one catalog item, closing a real silent-loss path where an unresolved component used to be dropped from what gets written *and* dropped from what the cleanup step considers "still wanted," so the cleanup would delete the existing component with nothing written to replace it); `recordNotificationDelivery` and `addTaskActivity` (best-effort logging, stays non-throwing by design — **corrected same day**: the first version of `addTaskActivity`'s log line included the activity's own business message as if it were diagnostic detail; both now read and log the real failed response body instead).

  **Reverted, not kept — a real design flaw in the first attempt, found by review**: `updateProjectLedgerInfo`/`updateInstalledAsset`/`updateProjectStakeholder` were given the same write-verification treatment, with their `main.tsx` callers changed to capture the prior array state and restore it on a caught failure. **This was unsound and has been fully reverted** (all three persistence functions, all three `main.tsx` handlers, and the test file `src/single-record-update-verification.test.ts` deleted) — capturing "the array as it was" and restoring it later is not a safe concurrency primitive: if a second edit lands while the first request is still in flight, an older failed request's revert can silently overwrite the second edit's already-applied change with stale data. Review also found two of the three writers aren't even live today: `handleUpdateInstalledAsset` has no call site anywhere in `main.tsx`, and `onUpdateProjectStakeholder` is wired into `<Projects>` as a prop but never actually invoked downstream. All three remain confirmed, documented, unfixed findings — real gaps, just needing a deliberate concurrency-safe caller design (e.g. revert only the specific field that failed, or a per-id in-flight guard) before attempting again, not a repeat of this approach.

Severity downgraded from Medium to Low in the audit doc.

  **`saveProjectSites`'s write map — corrected same day.** The first version claimed a site could be "silently dropped because of a constraint or RLS" from the `projects` upsert while the rest of the batch succeeded. That's not how a multi-row `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` actually fails — it's one atomic statement; a real constraint/RLS rejection fails the whole statement (already caught by the existing `.ok` check), not just the offending row. Corrected to describe the missing row-count check as defensive verification for a possible integrity anomaly, not a proven, already-occurring partial-write mechanism. See `PRODUCT_ERROR_VISIBILITY_AUDIT.md`'s write-map table for the corrected wording.

  **`PRODUCT_PROJECT_BOM_ATOMIC_REPLACE_PLAN.md` substantially revised, same day, still 100% design-only, no migration created.** The original validation pass found the `task_hardware_dependencies.project_bom_line_id` FK risk but under-weighted it as "a future dormant concern." Review determined the design itself needed to change: the RPC is now **reconcile-by-id**, not delete-then-reinsert — a retained BOM line is updated in place (same id, so the FK is never touched for it), a line with no id is inserted, and only ids the user actually removed are deleted. This requires `BomLine` to carry a stable line id and a stable catalog item identifier (SKU or `inventory_item_id`) as a precondition, promoted from "preferred later" to load-bearing — the ambiguous-name rejection behavior (option (b)) is now a fallback path for a line without one, not the primary mechanism. `EC008` confirmed as the next free error code; the two-session concurrency test's methodology is unchanged and still applies to the reconciliation design. Verification cases updated to test id preservation, cross-project id reassignment rejection, and FK survival across a save.

  **`restoreFullBackupSnapshot`'s resumable-design conclusion — corrected same day.** The first version claimed a full restore retry was already safe and idempotent by construction. Corrected: confirmed real complications that break that claim outright — `project_bom_lines` replacement creates new line ids on every run (so a retry doesn't reproduce the same identities); `task_hardware_dependencies` can lose its BOM-line reference as a direct consequence; `saveRestoredProjectDocuments` regenerates `document_number` from `Date.now()` on every call (confirmed by direct read — a retry of the exact same snapshot produces a *different* document number every time); `saveRestoredPurchaseRequests`/`saveRestoredProjectDocuments`'s own writes remain entirely unchecked; and other generated/timestamp values can differ between attempts. Revised conclusion: a full retry is a **promising recovery direction for cleanly-keyed top-level records**, not proven end-to-end. Checkpoint/resume design stays explicitly open, not closed or rejected, until a real end-to-end retry test verifies content, row identities, dependent FKs, and duplicate counts.

No component-testing package was added, per instruction — `main.tsx`'s remaining caller-side behavior (every debounced-save catch, `importBackup`'s catch) remains verified by direct code reading only, restated explicitly rather than silently left implicit.

  **Second correction round, same day (2026-09-11), after a further review found five more real problems:**
  1. **`saveDeviceRecipes` had a second, distinct silent-loss gap**: the first preflight caught an unresolved/ambiguous component *name*, but not the same resolved `inventory_item_id` appearing twice within one recipe's own component list — that shape can trigger a real Postgres uniqueness violation on `(equipment_type_id, inventory_item_id)` in the bulk upsert, potentially *after* that recipe's `equipment_types` write already committed. A second preflight now rejects the whole save (all recipes) if any recipe has a duplicate component, scoped per-recipe so two different recipes sharing a component is correctly not flagged. Quantities are never auto-combined — a business decision, not made here. 3 new tests.
  2. **The `saveDeviceRecipes` atomicity claim was corrected, in the source comments and this document and the audit doc**: this pass fixes failure *visibility* and *input validation*, not atomicity. If recipe 2 of 3 fails, recipe 1's writes remain committed; within one recipe, its `equipment_types` write can commit before a later component write fails. Whole-call and per-recipe atomicity both remain explicitly open follow-up work (an RPC, not built here).
  3. **`PRODUCT_PROJECT_BOM_ATOMIC_REPLACE_PLAN.md` corrected further**: a non-null BOM line id that doesn't belong to the target project is now always a rejection (the prior version could be read as treating it as "insert new," which is wrong); duplicate non-null line ids in one payload are now rejected; malformed ids/line objects are validated before any resolution or reconciliation logic runs; the RPC's return value now includes the full saved line collection (real generated ids for new lines included), not just counts, closing an internal inconsistency where the retry discussion assumed ids the return shape never actually provided; the `task_hardware_dependencies` FK is now described as a real, present-tense schema reference rather than anything implying it doesn't currently reference anything; client-supplied `inventory_item_id`/`sku` is now explicitly untrusted input the RPC must verify (existence, exactly-one-match for `sku`, mutual consistency if both given) rather than assumed correct. New verification cases added for all of the above.
  4. **Inaccurate row-count comments in `persistence.ts` corrected**: the `saveInventoryItems` item-metadata and balance write comments no longer claim RLS or a constraint can silently drop individual rows from a multi-row PostgREST write (a single atomic statement doesn't fail that way). Now described as an unexpected response cardinality / integrity anomaly worth defensive verification, not a demonstrated mechanism. The checks and runtime behavior are unchanged — only the comment's claim about *why* a mismatch might occur.
