- **(migration `128_harden_project_insert_trigger_chain.sql` DRAFTED -- fixes the exact bug that stopped migration 127's own live production verification. Migration 127 was NOT edited or rerun -- it is already applied. Local/static checks only; migration 128 itself has NOT been run, and the production verification has NOT been rerun.) Migration 127's live verification reached the real INSERT into `projects` and failed -- traced to a second instance of the migration-123 bug class, this time in the trigger chain, not a called helper function.**

  **Root cause**: `assign_project_ref()` (migration 067, a BEFORE INSERT trigger on `projects`) has no `SET search_path` of its own. Fired from inside `create_project_from_quote()`'s own `set search_path=''` context (migration 127), it inherited that empty search_path -- its unqualified reference to `project_ref_counters` could not resolve, exactly the way `get_users_by_role()` failed inside `respond_to_submittal()` before migration 123 fixed it. This is precisely the bug class this session's own `create_project_from_quote()` work went out of its way to avoid by never calling the unhardened `has_role()` -- it was still live one layer down, in a trigger neither of us had reason to suspect until production actually exercised it.

  **Full trigger-chain trace** (not just the one function that failed): every `... insert on ...` trigger definition in every migration file was grepped, then checked against all six tables `create_project_from_quote()` writes to (`projects`, `project_scope_of_work`, `project_bom_lines`, `project_locations`, `project_location_items`, `project_conversion_receipts`). Result: **exactly two** INSERT-firing triggers exist across all six tables, both on `projects`:
  1. `assign_project_ref()` (migration 067) -- BEFORE INSERT, plain (security invoker), unqualified `project_ref_counters` reference. The confirmed failure.
  2. `create_project_channel()` (migration 101) -- AFTER INSERT, already `SECURITY DEFINER`, unqualified `channels` reference. Not yet observed failing (the BEFORE INSERT trigger above fails first, masking it), but the identical bug -- SECURITY DEFINER does not by itself protect against inheriting an unsafe ambient search_path; only a function's own `SET search_path` does.

  Every other trigger on these six tables is a `BEFORE UPDATE "set updated_at"` trigger (`project_scope_of_work`, `project_bom_lines`, `project_locations`) -- never fired by an INSERT, so never reachable from this function at all. `project_location_items` and `project_conversion_receipts` have no triggers whatsoever. **No third blocker exists** -- migration 128 touches exactly these two functions and nothing else.

  **Fix**: both functions redefined with `SET search_path = ''`, every table reference fully schema-qualified (`public.project_ref_counters`, `public.channels`), and (`assign_project_ref()` specifically) an explicit target-table alias (`as prc`) in its `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE` clause -- a schema-qualified correlation name is not valid syntax there at all, so this is a correctness fix, not just a hardening one. `pg_catalog.now()`/`pg_catalog.lpad()` explicitly qualified too, on top of relying on `pg_catalog`'s own always-implicit search-path membership, per E's explicit request. Neither function's signature, return shape, project-number format, counter behavior, channel naming, or conflict-handling changed at all. `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` alone preserves each function's identity (same schema+name+signature = same oid), so neither existing trigger (`projects_assign_ref`, `projects_create_channel`) needs to be dropped or recreated. Both functions also had direct EXECUTE revoked from `public`/`anon`/`authenticated` -- they're trigger-only functions, never meant to be called directly by any client, and revoking this grant does not affect trigger firing at all (the same already-validated pattern `guard_workspace_id_mutation()`, migration 117, already relies on -- trigger invocation is performed by the executor, not as an ACL-checked call by the DML-issuing role).

  **Test script extended** (not a new file, per instruction) -- `migration_127_conversion_tests.sql`'s Section 4 (fresh conversion) now also proves: the created project's reference matches the real `PRJ-YYYY-NNNN` format the trigger actually produces; exactly one project channel was created, with the correct `project_id`/`type='project'`/name; both trigger functions are confirmed pinned to an empty search_path (via `pg_get_functiondef()`, the same proven pattern migration 124's own Section 9b established); `anon`/`authenticated` are confirmed to have zero direct EXECUTE on either function (via `has_function_privilege()`) -- while Section 4's own successful conversion is itself the proof that trigger firing keeps working despite the revoked grants, not merely an assertion. Section 5 (idempotency) now also confirms a retry changes neither the project's reference number nor its channel count. The script's header now explicitly records that the earlier failed run committed nothing at all -- no project row, no `project_ref_counters` increment, no channel, and (via the same rollback) no synthetic workspace, temporary role grant, or workspace-status change either.

  **Files**: `backend/supabase/migrations/128_harden_project_insert_trigger_chain.sql` (new), `backend/supabase/migration_127_conversion_tests.sql` (extended in place), `PRODUCT_SHARE_LINK_EXPIRATION_REVOCATION_DECISION.md` (the design-only policy-closure plan renumbered a fourth time, 128 -> 129, since this real fix needed the 128 slot -- same pattern as every prior renumbering, explained inline).

  **Verification**: local/static checks only, as instructed -- `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vitest run` -- 178 tests, all passed (unchanged, no frontend files touched this turn); `vite build` clean. Migration 128 itself was **not** run, and `migration_127_conversion_tests.sql` was **not** rerun against production -- both require E's own action.

  **Explicitly NOT done**: migration 127 was not edited or rerun (it is already applied, exactly as E instructed); migration 128 was not run; the verification script was not rerun; nothing committed, pushed, or deployed.

- **(Overnight autonomous pass, 10-task list, run unsupervised per E's explicit strict boundaries -- nothing run in Supabase, nothing committed/pushed/deployed, no production data touched, no second workspace, no Phase 3/Billing/share-link work.) Migration 127's test script corrected, one in-migration preflight assertion added, one dead function removed, three real frontend gaps closed with new tests, the error-visibility audit extended with a disaster-recovery implementation plan and a System Health design, and a read-only dependency audit completed.**

  **Task 1 -- corrected a wrong assumption in migration 127's own test script.** Section 4b's tamper-resistance tests previously expected an authenticated UPDATE/DELETE against `project_conversion_receipts` to silently match zero rows (RLS row-invisibility). That's only true if the role still holds UPDATE/DELETE privilege -- migration 127 also `REVOKE ALL`s from `authenticated`/`anon`, so Postgres actually rejects all four of SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE at the **grant layer** with "permission denied," before RLS ever runs. The test script now asserts that correctly (a caught exception for all four, not a row-count check for two of them), added an explicit SELECT attempt (previously untested), and added `has_table_privilege()` + `information_schema.role_table_grants` checks confirming `anon`/`authenticated` hold zero privileges at all. Migration 127's own comment and the test script's header wording were corrected to match: "only the database owner and a trusted service-role connection... can access the table," not an RLS-only claim.

  **Task 2 -- full line-by-line static review of both files** against SQL syntax/SQLSTATE validity, `SECURITY DEFINER search_path` safety, grants, workspace matching, PM/admin authorization, atomic rollback, the unique indexes and concurrent-conversion handling, pre-127 project handling, receipt-same-transaction guarantee, soft-delete exclusion, FK/delete behavior, test-fixture rollback safety, and the skip-produces-hard-failure invariant. One concrete defect found and fixed: Section 3b's skip message incorrectly named Section 8b as gated by the same fixture, when 8b (the ambiguous-membership test) actually runs unconditionally using a different, always-available fixture user. Everything else reviewed clean -- no functional changes to migration 127 beyond Task 1's test-script correction and Task 4's preflight addition (below).

  **Task 3 -- full frontend validation, plus three real, previously-unprotected gaps closed.** `tsc`, `vitest` (now 178 tests), `vite build`, `eslint`, and the existing Playwright smoke suite (6 specs, runs against a local dev server with no real backend -- confirmed safe, confirmed passing) all clean. While confirming the requested behaviors, found and fixed: (1) `runCreateProjectFromQuote`'s loading state (`isCreatingProject`) had no `try/finally` -- an unexpected throw (not the normal RPC-failure path, which never throws, but a genuinely unexpected one) would leave the button stuck on "Creating..." forever; now wrapped in `try/finally`. (2) The success message named only photo counts, never BOM-line/location/location-item counts, even though the RPC already returns them -- a PM had no way to tell exactly what transferred without opening the project; now included. (3) The status line had no screen-reader announcement (`role="status" aria-live="polite"` added -- this codebase had zero existing ARIA-live usage anywhere). The message-building logic that used to live only inside main.tsx (untestable) is now a pure, exported function (`buildProjectConversionStatusMessage`, `src/quote-conversion-flow.ts`) with 9 new tests covering every branch, including "never claims an unverified project is complete." Also found genuinely dead code as a byproduct: `deleteStorageObject` (a helper `cleanupOrphanedProjectLocationImage`'s own comment claimed was shared with the Files viewer and Photo Gallery) had zero real call sites even at the start of this session -- removed, and both places that repeated the stale claim were corrected.

  **Task 4 -- migration 127 now fails with a clear, actionable, in-migration message** if production already has two or more projects sharing the same `source_sales_quote_id` (the one realistic way the new unique index could fail to apply), instead of a raw, unexplained Postgres "could not create unique index... is duplicated" error -- no separate preflight script to run and interpret by hand. The message names exactly how many quotes are affected and their ids, and explicitly does not attempt to resolve the conflict automatically. (The migration's other new unique index, on `project_location_images`, cannot have this problem -- its column is new, so no existing row can already violate it.)

  **Task 5 -- read-only implementation plan for `restoreFullBackupSnapshot`** (the Critical, previously-flagged disaster-recovery path), added to `PRODUCT_ERROR_VISIBILITY_AUDIT.md`. Traced every sub-write it makes across 6 tables (`inventory_items`, `inventory_balances`, `equipment_types`, `projects`, `project_scope_of_work`, `project_bom_lines`, `purchase_requests`, `project_documents`) and found the same unverified-write pattern `createProjectFromClosedWonQuote` had, spread across multiple functions instead of one. No Storage/file operations are involved (the snapshot is relational data only). Plan covers: checking every response, a structured `{restored, skipped, failed}` result type, a real reconciliation report named by record, plain-language failure wording, the BOM-lines delete/reinsert ordering gap, and the tests required before any of it is implemented. **Nothing implemented or run** -- audit and plan only, per instruction.

  **Task 6 -- extended `PRODUCT_ERROR_VISIBILITY_AUDIT.md`** (not a new document) with new findings from tonight's scan: `saveProjectSites` (the live Projects-page save path, not just restore) has the identical unchecked-`project_scope_of_work`-write pattern migration 127 just fixed elsewhere, plus an unprotected BOM-lines delete/reinsert -- flagged **Critical**, since this is a daily-use live path, not just disaster recovery. `saveInventoryItems`'s stock-balance write is unchecked even though the item-metadata write beside it is well-guarded (checked, retried, row-count-validated) -- flagged Critical (the number a physical pull decision gets made from). `saveDeviceRecipes` silently skips a failed new-recipe insert with no count, and never checks an existing-recipe update at all -- High. `recordNotificationDelivery` (the audit's own recommended System Health data source) can itself silently fail to record -- Medium, undermines Task 7's design. `releaseTransactionLock` is fire-and-forget with no traced expiry safety net -- Medium, open question rather than confirmed bug. **None of these were fixed** -- every one is a live, business-critical write path, not the kind of small, contained, behavior-preserving change the overnight boundary allows; all documented only, kept explicitly separate from migration 127's own diff.

  **Task 7 -- System Health design proposal**, added to the same audit doc: what should alert (repeated channel failures, a cron run that threw, repeated rate-limit trips from one actor, a sustained Redis fallback) versus what's merely informational; the exact plain-language shape of what an admin would see (what happened / who was affected / first-seen + most-recent / occurrence count / status / a safe retry-or-acknowledge action, with technical detail behind a collapsed disclosure, never in the primary line); and a note that the alert channel itself can't depend solely on the notification system it might be reporting as broken. **Not built** -- design only, for E's review and separate scoping.

  **Task 8 -- usability review of the conversion flow** (button/status clarity, duplicate-click protection, loading/disabled states, confirmation wording, partial-failure recovery, existing-project warning clarity, keyboard/screen-reader behavior, small-screen alert readability, "can the PM tell what transferred"). Duplicate-click protection and loading/disabled states were already solid (and Task 3 closed the one real gap in the loading-state guarantee). Two low-risk, presentation-only fixes made (see Task 3's items 2 and 3 above -- the transfer-count detail and the ARIA-live region are exactly this task's own asks, landed once, not twice). **Open questions, not resolved** (process decisions, not mine to make): the confirm-dialog wording doesn't mention that re-clicking on an already-converted quote is safe (idempotent) -- a small copy tweak worth considering; `window.alert()`'s native rendering on a small screen is OS-controlled and can't be improved without replacing it with a custom modal, a real UI investment, not an overnight change.

  **Task 9 -- read-only `npm audit`, classified, nothing installed/upgraded.** 7 findings (2 moderate, 5 high). Four are transitive **build-tool-only** dependencies (`browserslist`, `baseline-browser-mapping`, `nanoid`, `postcss` -- all pulled in by `vite`/`@vitejs/plugin-react`'s own toolchain, confirmed via `npm ls`, never present in the shipped `dist/` bundle or any deployed serverless function) -- accepted low risk, fixes available via `npm audit fix` whenever convenient, not urgent. Three are real production dependencies with genuine exploitability confirmed by checking actual usage: **`pdfjs-dist`** (arbitrary JS execution on opening a malicious PDF -- this app renders real uploaded project/PO documents client-side; fix available, likely non-breaking within the pinned major version) and **`nodemailer`** (four real CVEs in domain-validation/content-access logic -- this app sends real outbound email server-side; fix available) are both **immediate-action** recommended. **`xlsx`** (prototype pollution + ReDoS -- confirmed exploitable, since `XLSX.read()` is used to parse uploaded spreadsheet files at `main.tsx:11837,18628`, not just to export) has **no fix available at all**, meaning remediation requires either accepting the risk with compensating controls (e.g. restricting bulk-import to trusted staff only) or evaluating a replacement library -- flagged as the one finding needing a real decision, not just a version bump.

- **(migration `127_atomic_project_conversion.sql` REVISED a second time after E's review -- four more corrections, all addressed. Locally tested via tsc/vitest/vite build and a further-revised transaction-safe SQL test script. Still NOT run in Supabase, NOT deployed, NOT committed -- awaiting E's review of this revision.) A second review of the first revision (previous entry, below) found the reliability fixes were real but four implementation details weren't -- the structure-verified claim was tamperable, a whole class of workspace-resolution failures fell into the generic error bucket, "logged" cleanup failures weren't actually being logged, and a raw database error was reaching the user. All fixed in place.**

  **1. Made `structure_verified` tamper-resistant.** The previous revision derived it from `projects.conversion_schema_version` -- a plain column any PM/admin can already write via the ordinary "pm and admin write projects" RLS policy (migration 023), so nothing stopped someone from marking an incomplete legacy project "verified" by hand. Replaced with a new `project_conversion_receipts` table: row level security enabled, **zero policies** -- Postgres's real, enforced default-deny means no statement from any role except the table owner can touch it, so only `create_project_from_quote()` itself (running as its security-definer owner) can ever write a row there. `structure_verified` is now derived from that receipt's existence, never from anything the ordinary Projects write path can reach. New Section 4b in the SQL test script proves this empirically: an authenticated PM's direct INSERT is rejected outright (RLS's WITH CHECK has nothing to pass); an authenticated admin's direct UPDATE and DELETE both match zero rows (RLS's missing USING policy makes the row invisible to the statement, not an error) -- confirmed via `GET DIAGNOSTICS ROW_COUNT`, not by catching an exception, since that's genuinely how Postgres RLS behaves for UPDATE/DELETE versus INSERT. The real receipt is confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged after all three attempts.

  **2. Surfaced workspace-resolution failures safely.** `resolve_caller_workspace_id()` (migration 117, untouched) rejects a missing, suspended, or ambiguous membership with a plain, uncoded exception (SQLSTATE P0001, plpgsql's default) -- previously that fell into the same generic "check your connection" bucket as a real network failure. Now caught inside `create_project_from_quote()` (`exception when sqlstate 'P0001'`) and re-raised as a new stable code, **EC007**, with the message "Your workspace access is unavailable or ambiguous. Contact an administrator." Added to the frontend's code-to-reason map (`workspace_unavailable`) and its test table. The SQL test script now asserts EC007 specifically (via `GET STACKED DIAGNOSTICS ... RETURNED_SQLSTATE`) for all three real trigger cases: no membership at all (a new sub-test, Section 3b, using a fixture user before any workspace is granted), a suspended sole membership (Section 3d), and an ambiguous double-active membership (Section 8b) -- distinct from EC002 (Section 3c), which is specifically a real, resolvable, single active membership that just isn't the quote's own.

  **3. Made orphan cleanup failures actually verified and logged.** `cleanupOrphanedProjectLocationImage()` previously delegated to the existing shared `deleteStorageObject()` helper, which swallows network errors and never checks `response.ok` -- so the earlier claim that cleanup failures were logged was false for almost every real failure. It now does its own dedicated `fetch` (the shared helper is untouched -- it's relied on elsewhere, by the Files viewer and the Photo Gallery bulk-delete, with its own best-effort contract that isn't part of this fix), inspects the response, and logs status + body on a non-2xx result or the error itself on a network rejection -- still never throwing to the caller. Two new tests cover both cases directly, spying on `console.error` to confirm the failure is genuinely logged, not just silently absorbed.

  **4. Stopped showing raw database errors to users.** A failed photo-row insert previously appended the raw PostgREST message (which can include a constraint or schema detail) straight into the alert shown to the user. That message is now only ever logged to the console; the user sees a stable, plain message: "The file copied, but its photo record could not be saved. Try Create Project again." Existing tests updated to match; the console-logging is separately asserted.

  **Files**: `backend/supabase/migrations/127_atomic_project_conversion.sql` (revised in place -- new `project_conversion_receipts` table + RLS, `structure_verified` derivation changed, `resolve_caller_workspace_id()` call now wrapped and translated), `backend/supabase/migration_127_conversion_tests.sql` (revised in place -- new Section 4b for tamper-resistance, Sections 3b-3d restructured for precise EC002-vs-EC007 coverage, Section 8b now asserts EC007), `src/persistence.ts` (`cleanupOrphanedProjectLocationImage` rewritten with its own fetch, `EC007`/`workspace_unavailable` added, the raw-error-in-user-message removed), `src/project-conversion.test.ts` (updated/extended, 22 tests).

  **Verification**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vitest run` -- 18 files, **169 tests, all passed** (up from 167); `vite build` clean (the same one pre-existing, unrelated chunk-size warning).

  **Explicitly NOT done**: migration 127 has not been run in Supabase; nothing has been committed, pushed, or deployed; no SQL has been handed to E to run yet, per explicit instruction to wait for this review to be approved first.

- **(migration `127_atomic_project_conversion.sql` REVISED after E's review -- eight required corrections, all addressed. Locally tested via tsc/vitest/vite build and a fully revised transaction-safe SQL test script. Still NOT run in Supabase, NOT deployed, NOT committed -- awaiting E's review of this revision.) Migration 127's first draft (previous entry, below) had a real nested-search_path authorization bug, no workspace-ownership enforcement, and several other gaps E's review caught before anything was run. All fixed in place (127 was never applied, so revising the same file is a normal pre-review edit, not a retroactive change to an applied migration).**

  **1. Fixed the PM authorization check (nested search_path bug).** `create_project_from_quote()` no longer calls `has_role('pm')` at all -- that function has no `SET search_path` of its own and references `app_user_roles` unqualified, so calling it from inside this function's `search_path=''` context would fail exactly the way `get_users_by_role()` did before migration 123 fixed it. `has_role()` itself is untouched, per the standing "no broad rewrite" instruction (`PRODUCT_SHARE_LINK_EXPIRATION_REVOCATION_DECISION.md` Part 13.2). Instead, the check is now inlined, fully qualified (`public.workspace_member_roles`/`public.workspace_members`), and workspace-scoped: `is_app_admin(auth.uid())` OR a real `workspace_member_roles` row with `role_key='pm'` tied to the caller's own resolved workspace membership.

  **2. Enforced workspace ownership.** The caller's active workspace is resolved via the already-hardened `resolve_caller_workspace_id()` (migration 117, `search_path=''`, fully qualified internally -- confirmed safe to call from here, unlike `has_role()`) and required to match `sales_quotes.workspace_id` before the quote is even read further. This applies to admin too, not just PM -- a `security definer` function bypasses RLS entirely, so nothing stops a PM/admin from a different workspace without this explicit check. `resolve_caller_workspace_id()` itself (unmodified) also rejects a suspended or ambiguous membership, and migration 127 now depends on and correctly propagates that.

  **3. Fixed the Closed-Won timing race.** `handleStatusChange` called `onUpdateStatus()` without awaiting it, so the "Create Project?" prompt and the RPC call could fire before the database write landed -- or after it silently failed (`handleUpdateSalesQuoteStatus` caught its own errors and never told the caller). `handleUpdateSalesQuoteStatus` now returns `Promise<boolean>`; the sequencing itself is extracted into a new pure, independently-tested function, `runClosedWonConversionFlow` (`src/quote-conversion-flow.ts`), which awaits the status update and only prompts/converts if it resolved `true`. 5 new tests (`src/quote-conversion-flow.test.ts`), including a delayed-resolution case and a failed-update case, both confirming the prompt and the RPC never fire.

  **4. Stopped describing every existing project as atomically complete.** A project with `source_sales_quote_id` set could have been made by the old, buggy client-side code (or by hand) -- there was no way to know its BOM/scope/locations were actually complete just because the row exists. Added `projects.conversion_schema_version` (set to `1` only on this function's own fresh-creation path, in the same transaction as every other row) and a new `structure_verified` field in the RPC's result, computed from that column's presence -- never inferred from row counts. `main.tsx` now shows a distinct, explicit message for an unverified existing project ("...it predates this reliability fix and its BOM, scope of work, and locations are not verified complete -- review it manually") instead of the normal success message. This migration does not attempt to repair or backfill any older project's structure -- that's explicitly out of scope, a separate decision.

  **5. Made photo retries safe after a copy-succeeds-but-row-fails.** `persistence.ts` now deletes the just-copied Storage object (`deleteStorageObject`, already existed, reused) whenever its `project_location_images` row can't be saved, so a failed insert no longer leaves an orphaned file behind. A uniqueness conflict (HTTP 409 on the new `(project_location_id, source_quote_image_id)` unique index -- another tab or a concurrent retry that already won) is now confirmed via a follow-up `SELECT` and counted as `alreadyCopiedPhotos`, not a false failure, with the redundant copy cleaned up the same way. 4 new tests cover cleanup-on-failure, a failed cleanup not compounding into a worse error, the uniqueness-conflict-confirmed-as-already-copied path, and the uniqueness-conflict-but-row-not-found edge case.

  **6. Removed the migration-087 fallback.** That retry-without-`origin` path existed for a schema that predates this project by 40 migrations and no longer applies -- retrying every 400 could mask a real error. `project_location_images` insert failures are now reported with the actual server error message instead.

  **7. Improved error reporting.** `createProjectFromClosedWonQuote` now returns a discriminated `ProjectConversionOutcome` (`{ok: true, ...}` or `{ok: false, reason, message}`) instead of `null` on every failure alike. The RPC tags every expected rejection with a stable custom SQLSTATE (`EC001` not authorized, `EC002` wrong workspace, `EC003` quote not found, `EC004` not Closed - Won, `EC005` name collision, `EC006` a lost create/create race) -- the frontend surfaces the RPC's own message ONLY for these known, pre-written-safe codes; anything else (an unrecognized code, a raw constraint-violation message, a network failure) still falls back to the original generic "check your connection" text, so no internal detail is ever echoed to the user. 6 new tests cover all six known codes plus the two fallback cases.

  **8. Rewrote the SQL test fixtures for workspace-scoped authorization.** The PM test user is now a real member of the test quote's own active workspace with a `workspace_member_roles` row (temporarily granted, in sync with a matching `app_user_roles` grant, if no real one exists -- never a workspace-only grant that could never happen through the real bridge). New coverage: a same-workspace PM succeeds (Section 4, now correctly scoped); a real PM/admin from a genuinely different workspace is rejected with SQLSTATE EC002 (Section 3b -- needs a real user with zero pre-existing workspace membership, skipped by name if none exists rather than faked); that same caller's membership going suspended is also rejected (Section 3c); ambiguous active membership (two simultaneous active workspaces) is rejected (Section 8b -- reuses the quote-creator user, no special fixture needed); every rejection re-confirms zero project rows were written; an older, non-RPC-created project reports `already_existed=true, structure_verified=false` rather than complete (Section 8a).

  **Files**: `backend/supabase/migrations/127_atomic_project_conversion.sql` (revised in place), `backend/supabase/migration_127_conversion_tests.sql` (revised in place), `src/persistence.ts` (`createProjectFromClosedWonQuote` rewritten again for the new outcome type + cleanup/conflict handling), `src/main.tsx` (`handleUpdateSalesQuoteStatus` returns a boolean, `handleStatusChange` now uses `runClosedWonConversionFlow`, `runCreateProjectFromQuote`'s messaging handles the unverified-structure and known-failure-reason cases), `src/quote-conversion-flow.ts` (new), `src/quote-conversion-flow.test.ts` (new, 5 tests), `src/project-conversion.test.ts` (rewritten for the new contract, 25 tests).

  **Verification**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vitest run` -- 18 files, **167 tests, all passed** (up from 151); `vite build` clean (the same one pre-existing, unrelated chunk-size warning).

  **Explicitly NOT done**: migration 127 has not been run in Supabase; nothing has been committed, pushed, or deployed; the pre-existing `project_locations`/`project_location_items`/`project_location_images` RLS gap (flagged in the previous entry) remains flagged, not fixed; no Billing/down-payment approval gate was added to conversion, per E's explicit instruction to keep that out of this migration.

- **(migration `127_atomic_project_conversion.sql` DRAFTED -- fixes the sale-to-project conversion reliability issue. Locally tested via tsc/vitest/vite build and a full transaction-safe SQL test script. NOT run in Supabase, NOT deployed -- awaiting E's review.) The Sales Quote -> Project "Create Project" flow can no longer fail silently or leave a project half-built.**

  **Origin**: `PRODUCT_ERROR_VISIBILITY_AUDIT.md`'s 2026-09-08 overnight-pass addendum flagged `createProjectFromClosedWonQuote` (`persistence.ts`) as **Critical** -- "partial multi-step write with no rollback." The old implementation was a long sequence of separate PostgREST calls with no shared transaction: the top-level `projects` insert was checked, but `project_scope_of_work`'s insert result was discarded entirely, the BOM-lines bulk insert's result was discarded entirely, a failed location insert just `continue`d to the next location (silently dropping that location's items and photos), a failed location-items bulk insert was discarded entirely, and a failed photo copy or photo-row insert just `continue`d to the next photo. A single network hiccup partway through could leave a half-built project in production with the success message ("Created Project X") identical whether everything copied over or almost nothing did.

  **Fix**: migration 127 moves every one of those writes (project, scope of work, BOM lines, locations, location items) into one new security-definer function, `create_project_from_quote(quote_id)`, executed as the single implicit transaction every PostgREST RPC call runs in -- either the whole structure is created correctly, or (on any error) none of it is. There is no longer any code path that can leave a project half-built. The function is also idempotent (keyed by a new unique index on `projects.source_sales_quote_id`): calling it again for an already-converted quote returns the existing project instead of creating a duplicate or re-running any insert, matching the existing "Create Project" button's own retry-friendly UX ("safe to click again if it didn't work the first time"). It also now rejects a project-name collision with a clear error instead of the old `on_conflict=project_name` upsert silently merging into (and overwriting fields on) an unrelated existing project.

  **Photos are the one piece that can't move into that same SQL transaction** -- copying a storage object is an HTTP call to the Storage API, not something a plpgsql function can wrap. So photo copying stays client-side, called after the RPC has already committed the project's full structure. Two changes make that safe instead of just "not atomic": (1) the RPC always returns, per location, the set of source photo ids already copied over (via a new `project_location_images.source_quote_image_id` column, mirroring the `source_quote_location_id` pattern `project_locations` already used), so a retry after a partial photo failure skips photos that already succeeded instead of re-copying and duplicating them -- enforced not just by client convention but by a real unique index too. (2) The frontend (`persistence.ts`) now tracks every photo's copy outcome individually and returns a real report (`totalPhotos`/`copiedPhotos`/`alreadyCopiedPhotos`/`failedPhotos`) instead of swallowing failures with `continue`. `main.tsx`'s `runCreateProjectFromQuote` now builds a real status message from that report -- "Created Project X -- 6 of 8 photos copied" plus a `window.alert` naming exactly which photos failed and why, telling the user the project/BOM/scope/locations are complete and only those specific photos need a retry or manual upload -- replacing the old message that was identical whether everything or almost nothing succeeded.

  **Bundled correctness fixes** (free byproducts of rewriting the copy as `INSERT ... SELECT` from the authoritative `sales_quote_*` tables instead of trusting whatever the client happened to have loaded, found while doing this rewrite, not separately investigated): every soft-deleted quote/location/item/BOM-line (`deleted_at is not null` -- migration 088) is now correctly excluded from copying, which the old code never checked at all; **`camera` and `vpu` location items are now copied** -- the old code only ever copied `sign`/`sensor`/`misc` (`[...signLines, ...sensorLines, ...miscLines]`), silently dropping every camera and VPU line item on every single conversion since migrations 071/076 added those types; each location item's `location_label`/`accessory_catalog_item_id`/`accessory_qty` (migration 071) are now copied, previously always dropped; each location's own `address` (migration 074) is now copied, previously always dropped and easy to mistake for `projects.site_address` (which is a *different*, quote-level field -- the test script's own fixture caught this exact confusion mid-build).

  **Authorization**: `create_project_from_quote()` requires `is_app_admin(auth.uid()) or has_role('pm')`, matching the existing RLS policy on `projects`/`project_scope_of_work`/`project_bom_lines` (migration 023) exactly -- being security-definer, the function bypasses RLS entirely, so it re-asserts the same check RLS would have made rather than silently widening who can convert a quote. It also now requires the quote to actually be Closed - Won server-side, matching the only UI path that offers this action at all.

  **Explicitly NOT fixed here, flagged instead** (a separate, pre-existing authorization gap, not a reliability/atomicity one -- fixing it would be scope creep on a reliability fix): `project_locations`/`project_location_items`/`project_location_images` still have the original wide-open `authenticated write ... using(true)` policies from migration 064, never narrowed to pm/admin the way `projects`/`project_scope_of_work`/`project_bom_lines` were in migration 023. Any authenticated user can currently write directly to these three tables today, bypassing the pm/admin gate that governs the parent `projects` row. Worth a future migration, not part of this one.

  **Files**:
  - `backend/supabase/migrations/127_atomic_project_conversion.sql` -- the schema changes (the two new unique indexes, the `source_quote_image_id` column) and the `create_project_from_quote()` function itself. Full rationale in the file's own header comment. **Not run in Supabase yet.**
  - `backend/supabase/migration_127_conversion_tests.sql` -- transaction-safe (`begin;`/`rollback;`, never commits), following this session's established discipline: real existing users for every authorization check (admin, a real-or-temporarily-granted PM, a non-privileged user), synthetic `ZZ_TEST_`-prefixed quote/location/item/BOM-line/image fixtures created fresh inside the same rolled-back transaction. Covers: non-admin/non-pm rejection, anon rejection at the grant layer, non-Closed-Won rejection (even for an admin), a fresh conversion's full correctness (including the camera/vpu/location_label/address bundled fixes and soft-delete exclusion, all snapshot- or exists-verified), idempotent re-conversion (called as admin, proving the OR-condition's other half), photo-retry-safety (manually marks one of two photos as already-copied, confirms the RPC reports exactly that one back, not a coarse per-location flag), the project-name-collision rejection (with an unrelated project's row snapshotted before/after to prove it wasn't touched), and a structural check that the race-safety unique index actually exists. **Not yet run against Supabase** -- written and carefully reviewed, but only E running it in Studio (after 127 itself is applied) constitutes real verification, per this session's standing rule that only a live "ALL TESTS PASSED" run counts as confirmed, not my own reading of the SQL.
  - `src/persistence.ts` -- `createProjectFromClosedWonQuote` rewritten: one call to `rpc/create_project_from_quote`, then per-photo copy+insert with `source_quote_image_id` tagging and full failure tracking. Returns a new `ProjectConversionResult` type (`project`, `alreadyExisted`, `bomLineCount`, `locationCount`, `locationItemCount`, `totalPhotos`, `copiedPhotos`, `alreadyCopiedPhotos`, `failedPhotos`) instead of a bare `ProjectSite | null`.
  - `src/main.tsx` -- `onCreateProjectFromClosedWonQuote`'s type (both declaration sites), `handleCreateProjectFromClosedWonQuote`, and `runCreateProjectFromQuote` all updated for the new return type; the status message now reports the photo-copy outcome instead of being identical on partial vs. full success.
  - `src/project-conversion.test.ts` (new, 9 tests) -- covers the frontend half only (the one piece the SQL transaction can't reach): RPC call shape, zero-photo conversion, successful photo copy + `source_quote_image_id` tagging, skipping already-copied photos, a failed storage copy tracked as a reported failure (not swallowed), a failed image-row insert tracked the same way, the migration-087 400-retry-without-`origin` path, skipping a location the RPC didn't report without crashing, and `alreadyExisted` passthrough.
  - `PRODUCT_SHARE_LINK_EXPIRATION_REVOCATION_DECISION.md` -- Part 12's policy-closure plan (never a real file) renumbered a third time, 127 -> **128**, since this real fix needed the 127 slot -- same renumbering pattern as when 125 and 126 each did the same thing before it, both explained inline.

  **Verification so far**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vitest run` -- 17 files, **151 tests, all passed** (up from 142); `vite build` clean (the same one pre-existing, unrelated chunk-size warning). The SQL test script has **not** been run -- it depends on migration 127 existing in Supabase first, which it doesn't yet.

  **Explicitly NOT done, per E's instruction**: migration 127 has not been run in Supabase; nothing has been deployed; the project_locations/items/images RLS gap noted above was flagged, not fixed. Awaiting E's review of the migration file and the test script before either is run.

- **(migration `124_workspace_authorization_bridge.sql` REVISION 2 — 7 real issues found in E's review of the first draft, all corrected; still DRAFTED ONLY, not run in Supabase, nothing committed) Correction pass on the workspace-authorization bridge below.** Full detail in `PRODUCT_SHARE_LINK_EXPIRATION_REVOCATION_DECISION.md` Part 11 (Part 10 is marked superseded, kept only as the historical record of what draft 1 contained). The seven fixes: (1) `is_app_admin(uuid)` — called by every bridge function AND every existing RLS policy in the app — was itself unhardened (no `search_path=''`, unqualified `app_admins` reference), the exact bug class found live during migration 123; hardened, same signature/logic, verified all 18 existing callers unaffected. (2) `bridge_set_secondary_roles()` had no protection against demoting a primary role into a secondary one and would silently create an empty workspace membership; now verifies primary-role existence, workspace-membership existence, legacy/workspace primary-role agreement, and rejects the primary role appearing in the secondary set — all before writing anything. (3) **The real fifth live writer, `setUserAllowedViews()`, was missed in draft 1** despite draft 1's own documentation claiming all five were handled — corrected with a new `bridge_set_user_allowed_views()` RPC (legacy-only, requires exactly one primary-role row, fails clearly on zero/duplicate) and the doc's "four" language fixed to "five" throughout. (4) `active_workspace_id()` only checked the *active* workspace count — a second, merely-*suspended* workspace would have slipped through; now requires exactly one workspace row *in total*, which must be active. (5) `bridge_revoke_admin()` had no protection against removing the last global admin, which would have locked the app's own admin-management screen with no recovery path; fixed, including self-revocation. (6) Test script (`migration_124_bridge_tests.sql`) rewritten to cover every RPC's real authenticated execution path, all four workspace-guard scenarios, anonymous-execution rejection, actual `information_schema.role_routine_grants` verification, and explicit SKIPPED-section tracking (the final message can no longer claim unconditional success if a section lacked its real-data prerequisite). (7) Standalone drift report (`drift_report_standalone.sql`) now shows every workspace's own status (not just an active count), joins `app_known_users` for readable emails, and includes explicit `workspace_id` on workspace-side rows. **Local verification after all seven fixes**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vitest run` — 15 files, 136 tests, all passed (up from 133); `vite build` clean. The SQL test script still cannot be run against real data — depends on migration 124 already existing in production. Nothing deployed, run, or committed.

- **(migration `124_workspace_authorization_bridge.sql` DRAFTED — first draft, since corrected above — locally tested via tsc/vitest, NOT run in Supabase — awaiting real drift-report results and E's review) Full share-link expiration/revocation design work led to discovering the app has TWO parallel, unsynced authorization systems — a transitional bridge between them is now designed and locally prepared.**

  **Origin of this work**: a read-only design pass on proposal/submittal share-link expiration and revocation (`PRODUCT_SHARE_LINK_EXPIRATION_REVOCATION_DECISION.md`) led into a walkthrough of 8 core decisions, then 7 follow-up conflicts, then this bridge work — because implementing E's chosen submittal-authority model (Sales owns quote-stage submittals; a *specific assigned PM*, tied to a real account, takes over after a handoff gated on billing clearance + a tracked conversion approval + PM assignment) required a real "assigned PM" field tied to an actual user account. Tracing that requirement surfaced a major finding: **migration 115 (Phase 1 workspace foundation: `workspaces`/`workspace_members`/`workspace_member_roles`, "approved by E after three review rounds") is already live in production, seeded with a real workspace row (renamed by migration 116 to "Ergon Test Workspace"/`ergon-test`) — but it has never been wired into any actual authorization check anywhere in the app.** `has_role()`/`is_app_admin()` (migrations 023/012), which every single existing RLS policy in the app still calls, read the *older* `app_user_roles`/`app_admins` tables exclusively.

  **E's decision**: not to migrate every existing RLS policy as part of this link-management/Billing work (that's Phase 3's job), and not to treat two permanent parallel authorization systems as acceptable architecture either. Instead, a **documented, bounded, temporary compatibility bridge** — full 9-point policy recorded in the decision doc's Part 9.7.1: the workspace tables are the destination authority for every *new* feature this project builds (capabilities, billing clearance, assigned-PM authority, link management); existing features keep using the legacy tables until Phase 3 deliberately migrates them; any operation touching both systems goes through one reviewed, transactional, server-side function (no bidirectional triggers, no hidden sync loops); a read-only drift report exists to catch disagreement, run before activation and again after; new capabilities exist *only* in the new system; a workspace admin is never automatically promoted to a global `app_admin`; the bridge is blocked from ever supporting a second workspace while it's in effect; and a concrete removal plan is documented (Phase 3 migrates policies in tested groups, then retires the bridge writes, then deprecates the legacy tables).

  **Traced every real role-management writer before building anything** (per E's explicit instruction, not guessed): five live functions in `persistence.ts`, all direct client-side `fetch()` calls straight to PostgREST, none going through any server-side function today, all gated only by `is_app_admin()` at the RLS layer — `setPrimaryUserRole`, `setSecondaryUserRoles`, `setUserAllowedViews`, `grantAdmin`, `revokeAdmin`. **Found a sixth, dead: `setUserRole`** — zero call sites anywhere in `main.tsx`, confirmed by a fresh whole-project grep immediately before removal (re-confirmed per E's explicit "confirm again" instruction), and its `on_conflict=user_id` target hadn't matched any real constraint since migration 040 changed the table's key shape — it could never have worked correctly even if something had called it. **Removed entirely**, with a comment recording exactly why, rather than left as a landmine that could silently bypass the new bridge if ever revived.

  **What was actually built this round, all local, nothing deployed**:
  - `backend/supabase/drift_report_standalone.sql` — six-category read-only drift report (legacy users missing workspace membership; workspace users missing legacy presence; role-set differences; primary-role differences; admin-status differences; dangling/duplicate assignments), runnable immediately against production, independent of migration 124. **This is the very next thing that needs to happen** — E runs it manually and shares the real results, since drift between the two systems may already exist silently (migration 115 only ever copied data once, at the moment it ran).
  - `backend/supabase/migrations/124_workspace_authorization_bridge.sql` — `active_workspace_id()` (a hard, database-level guard refusing to proceed the moment a second active workspace exists — not just a documented rule), `bridge_set_primary_role()`, `bridge_set_secondary_roles()`, `bridge_grant_admin()`, `bridge_revoke_admin()`, `bridge_drift_report()` (a reusable, admin-gated RPC version of the standalone report). Every function `security definer` + `search_path=''` + fully schema-qualified + explicit narrow grants + server-derived `is_app_admin(auth.uid())` authorization, matching this session's established hardening discipline (migrations 119–123). `grantAdmin`/`revokeAdmin`'s workspace-side mirroring is deliberately **one-directional only** (legacy → workspace, never the reverse) per E's explicit requirement. **Deliberately does not touch any existing RLS policy** — closing the direct-write bypass on the legacy tables is a separate, later migration, only after this one is confirmed live and correct, per E's explicit two-step sequencing.
  - `backend/supabase/migration_124_bridge_tests.sql` — transaction-safe (`begin;`/`rollback;`, never commits), using real existing users rather than fabricated accounts (same discipline as migration 122's test script). Covers: non-admin caller rejected; admin caller succeeds with both systems written atomically in one call; an invalid role_key writes to neither table; the drift-report RPC correctly surfaces a deliberately-introduced mismatch; the active-workspace guard correctly refuses to proceed with a second active workspace present (inserted and tested entirely inside the rolled-back transaction). **Cannot be run yet** — depends on migration 124's functions existing in production first.
  - `src/persistence.ts` — the four live writers rewritten to call `rpc/bridge_*` instead of raw table writes; `setUserRole` deleted.
  - `src/role-bridge.test.ts` (new, 9 tests) — confirms each rewritten function calls the correct RPC with the correct body shape and still throws a clear error on failure.

  **Test evidence, real and actually run**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx vitest run` — **15 files, 133 tests, all passed** (up from 124 before this round); `npx vite build` clean (one pre-existing, unrelated chunk-size warning). The SQL test script has **not** been run — it depends on migration 124 existing, which it doesn't yet.

  **Explicitly NOT done this round, per E's own instruction**: migration 124 has not been run in Supabase; no restrictive/narrowing policy has been drafted as runnable SQL yet (only documented as a required future step); no second workspace was created; Phase 3 has not begun; nothing has been committed or pushed to git — everything above is a local working-tree change awaiting E's review of the drift-report SQL, the migration file, and the test script before anything proceeds. Full design rationale, the complete decision trail (8 core questions, 7 follow-up conflicts, this bridge's own 9-point policy, and this round's additional 7 requirements), and the plain-English summary are all in `PRODUCT_SHARE_LINK_EXPIRATION_REVOCATION_DECISION.md`, Parts 1–10.

- **(migrations `122_secure_submittal_response.sql` and `123_fix_get_users_by_role_search_path.sql` both RUN and fully verified in production; matching frontend fix pushed) Submittal-response replay and notification-dedup bugs fixed as a narrow follow-up to the proposal fix below, per E's explicit instruction.**

  **The two confirmed bugs** (full trace in `PRODUCT_TOKEN_BACKUP_CONTENT_TEST_AUDIT.md` Part E,
  written *before* any code changed, per E's explicit "first inspect and report" instruction):
  `respond_to_submittal()` (migration 025) had the identical missing status-transition guard as
  the pre-119 proposal function -- any share token could replay to overwrite an already-decided
  submittal. Separately, migration 055's notification insert used
  `on conflict (dedupe_key) do nothing`, which never matched `notifications.dedupe_key`'s real
  *partial* unique index (`... where dedupe_key is not null`, migration 024) -- the same 42P10
  class fixed for proposals in migration 121, present since 055 shipped. **This means no submittal
  response has ever successfully notified a PM or admin since that feature shipped** -- an
  independent, pre-existing gap this fix happened to surface, not introduced by tonight's work.

  **The fix** (migration 122) mirrors 119/121 exactly, with every lesson from that live debugging
  cycle built in from the start instead of found live: `drop function if exists` before each
  `create` (both functions' return shape changes), `RETURNS TABLE` columns aliased/qualified
  throughout to avoid the ambiguous-column bug, the `ON CONFLICT` clause pre-corrected to match
  the real partial index, and `authenticated` explicitly revoked alongside `revoke all ... from
  public` (this project's schema-level default privileges auto-grant `EXECUTE` to `authenticated`
  on any new function, same gotcha migration 118 first found). `respond_to_submittal()` now does
  an atomic `update ... where status = 'sent'`, returns a structured
  `(outcome, status, responded_at, approval_name, version)` result instead of `void`, and only
  fires notifications on the winning transition. `get_submittal_by_token()` gains
  `responded_at`/`approval_name`. Recipients (every `pm`-role user unioned with every admin) are
  **unchanged** -- no business-process decision made here, per explicit instruction. Token
  expiration (`expires_at` never set) and revocation remain deliberately untouched, same as the
  proposal fix -- documented as unresolved product decisions, not invented.

  **Live testing surfaced one genuinely new bug, not anticipated in the design, fixed by migration
  123**: `respond_to_submittal()`'s `search_path=''` hardening broke its own calls to
  `get_users_by_role()`/`get_admin_emails()` (migrations 042/049) -- those two functions predate
  the `search_path=''` discipline and have unqualified table references (`app_user_roles`,
  `app_known_users`) with no `SET` clause of their own, so they inherit whatever search_path is
  active in their *caller's* execution scope. Since the caller's scope was `''`, their own
  unqualified references failed to resolve (`relation "app_user_roles" does not exist`), caught
  live by the transaction-safe test script before any real submittal was ever affected. Fixed by
  hardening both functions the same way (`security definer` + `search_path=''` + fully qualified
  `public.app_user_roles`/`public.app_known_users`) -- pure hardening, no business-logic change,
  same signature so `create or replace` (no `DROP` needed). **The proposal fix (119/121) never
  hit this**: it notifies via the quote's own `created_by_email` column directly and never calls
  either function -- confirmed by re-reading `119_secure_quote_proposal_response.sql` directly,
  nothing already verified tonight is affected.

  **Tested via the same transaction-safe SQL pattern as 119/121** (`begin;`/`rollback;`, never
  commits, full script in the audit doc's Part E8) covering all scenarios required: valid
  approval/rejection/revision-request across three independent submittal fixtures; same-response
  and different-response replay, both rejected with the original state preserved; unchanged
  `status`/`response_notes`/`approval_name`/`approval_ip`/`approval_content_hash`/`responded_at`
  after replay, checked directly against the table; exactly one notification per intended
  recipient, computed dynamically against the real live `pm`+admin roster rather than fabricated
  accounts; invalid and expired tokens; an already-responded submittal still fully viewable via
  its original token; `content_snapshot` provably unchanged; the pre-existing authenticated
  pm/admin write RLS policy on `project_submittals` confirmed untouched. All scenarios passed
  clean on the second run (after migration 123 fixed the `get_users_by_role` search_path bug the
  first run surfaced).

  **Frontend** (`src/persistence.ts`/`src/main.tsx`, commit `8dd244f`): `fetchPublicSubmittal`/
  `respondToPublicSubmittal` now return discriminated outcomes matching the new RPC shape instead
  of collapsing every failure into `null`/`boolean`. `SubmittalPublicPage` mirrors
  `ProposalPublicPage`'s pattern exactly: distinguishes an invalid/expired token from a genuine
  load failure, and on a lost replay race reloads and displays the authoritative stored response
  (third-person "already approved on [date] by [name]" wording) instead of a generic error or the
  losing attempt's own input. 11 new unit tests (`src/submittal-response.test.ts`) cover every
  outcome branch. tsc/vitest both clean (14 files, 124 tests). Committed and pushed together with
  the migration files (`df72ffe`, `8dd244f`) since both migrations were already confirmed live
  before either commit was made -- no deployment-ordering hold needed this time.

  **Production verification**: new bundle (`index-BsUnoRys.js`) confirmed live at
  `ergon-ops-app.vercel.app`; `?submittal=<bad-token>` round-tripped through the real deployed RPC
  end-to-end and rendered the new dedicated "Link not found" state (proving `fetchPublicSubmittal`
  correctly mapped an empty RPC result to `invalid_token`), no console errors on that path.

- **(migrations `119_secure_quote_proposal_response.sql` and `121_fix_respond_to_quote_proposal_bugs.sql` both RUN and fully verified in production; matching frontend fix + a second dead-array removal now pushed) Live proposal-token replay vulnerability fixed and prioritized ahead of the per-workspace uniqueness work, per E's explicit instruction.**

  **Renumbering**: the per-workspace uniqueness migration (`clients.name`/`sales_quotes.quote_ref`,
  previously "migration 119" throughout `PRODUCT_PHASE2_PLAN.md`) is renumbered to **migration
  120** -- pure renumbering, no design content changed. See that document's Revision 6 note.

  **The vulnerability** (found during the 2026-09-08 overnight audit,
  `PRODUCT_TOKEN_BACKUP_CONTENT_TEST_AUDIT.md` Part A3): `respond_to_quote_proposal()` had no
  server-side status-transition guard. Anyone holding a proposal share token -- which never
  expires and can't be revoked, both deliberately untouched by this fix, see below -- could call
  the RPC directly and repeatedly flip an already-approved/rejected/revision-requested proposal,
  silently overwriting the recorded `responded_at`/`approval_name`/`approval_ip`/`response_notes`/
  `approval_content_hash`.

  **The fix, designed then implemented, self-tested via a written transaction-safe SQL script
  before being presented** (full detail in `PRODUCT_TOKEN_BACKUP_CONTENT_TEST_AUDIT.md`'s "A3
  resolution" section): `respond_to_quote_proposal()` now does a single atomic
  `update ... where status = 'sent'` -- safe against concurrent responses under Postgres's own
  MVCC/row-locking, no explicit lock needed -- and returns a distinguishable outcome
  (`success`/`already_responded`/`invalid_token`) instead of `void`, so the first valid response
  wins and every later attempt leaves the row completely unchanged. The notification insert only
  runs on the winning transition, so a replay or concurrency-loser can never create a duplicate.
  `get_quote_proposal_by_token()` gains `responded_at`/`approval_name` columns so the public page
  can show "This proposal was already approved on [date]" with real data. Both functions hardened
  to `security definer` + `search_path=''` + fully schema-qualified table references (they
  predate that discipline, established in migration 115) and their grants narrowed to `anon` only
  -- confirmed by re-reading every call site that `authenticated` was unused by any real path, the
  same minimal-surface principle migration 118 already established. **Token expiration
  (`expires_at` never set, Part A2) and revocation (Part A4) are deliberately NOT touched** --
  explicit instruction, kept as separate, undiscussed product decisions.

  **Terminology note**: the fix requirements' "pending" status maps to this schema's real
  `status = 'sent'` value -- there is no `'pending'` value in `sales_quote_proposals`, and adding
  one was considered and rejected as unnecessary schema churn.

  **Tested via a self-contained, transaction-safe SQL script** (`begin;`/`rollback;`, never
  commits, full text in the audit doc) covering all 9 required scenarios: first approval succeeds;
  replaying the same approval fails safely with the original state preserved; a different later
  status also fails safely; `revision_requested` locks its own proposal version too; invalid and
  expired tokens both return `invalid_token`; the sequential-replay test doubles as the "two

  **Live verification found and fixed two more real bugs, neither introduced by this fix, both
  now permanently recorded in `121_fix_respond_to_quote_proposal_bugs.sql`:**
  1. **Migration 119's own first run attempt failed outright** (Postgres 42P13: `CREATE OR
     REPLACE FUNCTION` can't change a function's return-row shape). Fixed by adding
     `drop function if exists ...` before each affected `create` -- directly in the still-unrun
     119 file, since nothing had committed yet. Second run attempt succeeded.
  2. **After 119 committed, live testing found `respond_to_quote_proposal()` failed on every real
     call** (Postgres 42702, ambiguous column reference: its own `RETURNS TABLE` column names
     collide with `sales_quote_proposals`' real column names, and in `plpgsql` those become
     implicit variables in scope for the whole function body). Fixed by aliasing the table and
     qualifying every reference.
  3. **The notification insert failed on every real call too, and had since migration 054**
     (Postgres 42P10: `on conflict (dedupe_key) do nothing`, copied verbatim from the original
     054, never matched `notifications`' real unique index, which is *partial*
     -- `... where dedupe_key is not null`, migration 024). **This means no real customer
     response to a proposal has ever successfully notified the quote's owner since this feature
     shipped** -- a genuine, independent, pre-existing gap this fix happened to surface, not
     something introduced tonight. Fixed by restating the matching `where` clause in the `ON
     CONFLICT` clause. The identical, still-live bug in `respond_to_submittal()` (migration
     025/041 family) is **not** fixed here -- flagged for a separate future fix, not silently
     bundled in.
  4. **Migration 119 also picked up an unwanted `authenticated` grant**, same root cause as
     migration 118 (this project's schema-level default privileges), fixed the same way
     (`revoke execute ... from authenticated`).

  Both #1 and #4's corrections were folded directly into `119_secure_quote_proposal_response.sql`
  before/immediately after its successful run (matching the standing rule: never edit an
  already-*committed* migration's SQL after the fact -- #1 was fixed before it ever committed,

- **(`af8e1b0`) Overnight autonomous work session, Priority 2: four small, isolated bug fixes, each with regression tests.** Run per E's explicit overnight authorization (git reconciliation, then a large prioritized safe-work queue) — see the morning-report entry at the top of this section once written for the full session summary; this entry covers only the shipped code fixes.

  **Note on commit granularity**: E's instructions asked for each Priority 2 fix as its own separate reviewable commit. All four touch `src/main.tsx` in overlapping/adjacent regions; splitting them into separate commits would have required manual git-hunk surgery (`git apply` on hand-edited partial patches) with real risk of silently corrupting the file during an unattended run. Bundled into one commit instead, with each fix documented separately below and in the commit message itself — flagging this deviation explicitly rather than doing it silently.

  1. **Fake `quote_ref` in proposals fixed.** `buildProposalSnapshot()` (`src/main.tsx`, formerly line 5028) fabricated a ref from `quote.id.slice(0, 8).toUpperCase()` instead of using the real `quote.quoteRef` (e.g. `SQ-2026-0001`) already on the object — found during Phase 2 research (`PRODUCT_PHASE2_PLAN.md` §3.5). Now reads `quote.quoteRef` directly. `api/send-proposal-email.js`'s matching bug (derived a fake ref from `proposal.quote_id` instead of the real one) is fixed to read `proposal.content_snapshot?.quoteRef` — the real ref *as that specific proposal actually recorded it*, which deliberately leaves every already-sent proposal's frozen `content_snapshot` untouched (only future sends get the real ref; this is a display fix, not a backfill). Two new tests added to `tests/api/send-proposal-email.test.js` covering both the fixed case and an older-proposal-with-no-`quoteRef`-in-snapshot case. No customer email was sent to verify this — confirmed by direct code/test inspection only.
  2. **Dead client-data array removed.** The unused top-level `const projects: ProjectSite[]` array (real client names/addresses, e.g. "Newport News Shipbuilding," "Straub Medical" with a real street address) is deleted from `src/main.tsx`. Independently re-verified zero live references before deleting (not just trusting the prior audit pass) via a fresh grep for the bare `projects` identifier across the whole file — every hit was either the unrelated `View`/tab-key string `"projects"`, the `globalSearchMatches(Full).projects` property, or the real `projectSites` state variable, never this array.
  3. **Emerald Queen fallback replaced with fictional content.** `emeraldQueenImportedProject` (`src/main.tsx`, real Ensight customer proposal data — real casino name, address, contact, dollar figures) is renamed `sampleDemoImportedProject` with every client-identifying field replaced by clearly fictional placeholder content of the same shape; the trigger filename check (`"emerald queen"` → `"sample demo"`) and the technical fallback mechanism itself (local-dev-only, fires when the real `/api/sales-quote-extract` call fails) are unchanged. **A second, previously unflagged instance of the same defect was found during the post-fix source scan (item 4 below) and fixed too**: `api/sales-quote-extract.js`'s real `extractQuoteData()` function — live in production, not test-only — had an `isEmeraldQueen` special case that silently substituted a hardcoded real client identity ("Emerald Queen Casino & Hotel," Tacoma WA) and an artificially inflated `"high"` confidence rating whenever the *actual PDF text* being parsed happened to contain the words "emerald queen," regardless of what the real document said. No test depended on this behavior. Removed entirely (not fictionalized) since it served no legitimate product purpose and was a real, if narrow, misextraction risk for any future customer whose real proposal happened to mention that phrase.
  4. **Verification scan**: grepped `src/`, `api/`, and the built `dist/` bundle for every real name/address this pass was scoped to (`Newport News Shipbuilding`, `Straub Medical`, `Emerald Queen`, `Steven Oakes`, `133 S. 40th`, `Santee CA`) — all clean, confirmed not shipping in source or the production bundle. Per the explicit limits for this session, no database record was touched or removed — this was a source-code-only cleanup.
  5. **Hard-coded 2026 project-reference year fixed.** `nextProjectRef()` (`src/main.tsx`) hardcoded the literal year 2026 in both its match regex and generated prefix, so it would have kept minting `PRJ-2026-####` forever past the actual 2027 calendar rollover instead of starting a fresh sequence, the same class of bug the real server-side `quote_ref` counter (migration 066) already avoids by deriving its year from `extract(year from now())`. Extracted the pure logic into a new exported `computeNextProjectRef(existingRefs, year)` in `persistence.ts` specifically so the year-boundary behavior is unit-testable without rendering the Projects component; `main.tsx` now calls it with `new Date().getFullYear()`. Five new tests in `src/project-ref.test.ts`, including two dedicated year-boundary cases (rolls over to a fresh `0001` for a new year even with existing prior-year refs present; continues an existing new-year sequence correctly once one exists).
  6. **`PRODUCT_PHASE2_PLAN.md`'s migration-119 planning note re-checked**: confirmed its `assign_sales_quote_ref()` redesign still preserves the real function's existing `if new.quote_ref is not null then return new;` early-return (already fixed in an earlier revision) — verified again against the live migration 066 source, no changes needed.

Production deploy and read-only verification are recorded separately once performed (see below/morning report).

- **(`117_clients_sales_quote_workspace_ownership.sql` and `118_revoke_workspace_ownership_function_grants.sql` both RUN by E in Supabase Studio, live-verified, `dfcf897`) Phase 2's first migration pair is complete.** Full scoping, design, and rigor are recorded in `PRODUCT_PHASE2_PLAN.md` (now Revision 4) — this entry is the production-verification summary.

  **Scoping (before any SQL was written):** two research agents plus direct migration reads established the full dependency graph for the Clients + Sales Quote cluster. Conclusion: `clients` and the complete Sales Quote graph (its six child tables — `sales_quote_locations`, `sales_quote_location_images`, `sales_quote_location_items`, `sales_quote_bom_lines`, `sales_quote_intake_responses`, `sales_quote_proposals`) are treated as **one combined vertical slice**, not split — `clients` is functionally disconnected from the live app today (no UI creates one; `client_id` on quotes/projects was only partially backfilled at migration 102's original run), so scoping it alone would carry no value. Every child table inherits ownership through its required, cascading FK to `sales_quotes`; only `clients` and `sales_quotes` themselves needed a new `workspace_id` column. `proposal_template_sections`, `public_share_tokens`, `notifications`, and `projects` were all confirmed structurally out of scope, with the `projects` gap (no `workspace_id` yet) explicitly recorded as a forward dependency for whichever future phase scopes Projects, not silently dropped.

  **Migration 117 design, after several correction rounds:** rather than have application code set `workspace_id` on insert (which would require trusting client input and remembering every writer — exactly the risk E flagged), a `before insert or update` trigger (`guard_workspace_id_mutation()`, calling `resolve_caller_workspace_id()`) derives `workspace_id` server-side from the caller's own active workspace membership, unconditionally, and **rejects any UPDATE that changes or nulls an existing `workspace_id`** with no bypass of any kind. `resolve_caller_workspace_id()` requires `workspaces.status = 'active'` and distinguishes three failure modes with three different error messages: no membership, suspended workspace, ambiguous (>1 active) membership. The whole schema/backfill/trigger/enforcement sequence runs as one atomic `begin;`/`commit;` transaction with an in-migration zero-null assertion before `NOT NULL` is applied — no nullable soak period at any point.

  **Migration 117 run and verified:** zero nulls in both new columns; all existing `clients`/`sales_quotes` rows backfilled to the Ergon Test Workspace; both columns `NOT NULL`; both triggers present and firing (`clients_guard_workspace_id`/`sales_quotes_guard_workspace_id`, each on `BEFORE INSERT` and `BEFORE UPDATE`); RLS on both tables confirmed untouched (still fully open, exactly as before — this migration adds tamper-proof ownership *metadata*, not cross-workspace access containment, which is explicitly Phase 3's job).

  **Real gap found during verification, then fixed (migration 118):** the grant-check query showed `EXECUTE` on both new functions granted to `authenticated` **and** `anon`, even though migration 117 never granted either. Root cause: this Supabase project's schema-level default privileges (`alter default privileges in schema public grant execute on functions to anon, authenticated, service_role`, standard on every Supabase project) auto-grant EXECUTE to those roles on any newly created function — migration 117's `revoke all ... from public` only revoked the PUBLIC pseudo-role's default, not a separately-recorded grant to a named role. This is the first migration in the project where a function was meant to carry no grant to `authenticated` at all, which is why the gap wasn't visible until now. Practical exposure was low (the trigger function can't be called directly by any role regardless of grants — Postgres blocks that at the language level; the resolver function being callable only ever returned the caller's own workspace id or an error, and always errored for `anon` since `auth.uid()` is null for anonymous callers) but didn't match the documented design, so `118_revoke_workspace_ownership_function_grants.sql` (two `revoke` statements, `service_role` deliberately left untouched) was created and run. Re-verified clean: only `service_role` retains the grant now.

  **Transaction-safe test script — found and fixed a second bug live, before it ran clean:** the first run failed with `new row violates row-level security policy for table "workspaces"` — correct, pre-existing behavior (only platform admins can write to `workspaces`, and `platform_admins` is empty, the same restriction that required migration 116's rename to be run manually). The bug was in the test script itself: once `role` was switched to `authenticated` to simulate the caller, it stayed `authenticated` for the fixture-setup steps too (creating throwaway workspaces, editing `workspace_members`), which need the SQL editor's own privileged, RLS-bypassing connection role instead. Corrected to capture and restore the original role around every fixture step, switching to `authenticated` only immediately around the specific `clients`/`sales_quotes` statement each test exercises. Re-run: **"Success. No rows returned," no exception raised** — since every assertion in the script raises an exception the instant it fails (proven by the earlier RLS-violation error surfacing exactly that way), a clean run with no error is confirmation that INSERT-stamping, all three failure modes (zero/suspended/ambiguous membership), UPDATE-immutability (both null and reassignment attempts), and unchanged quote/child-table/cascade-delete behavior all passed. The whole script runs inside `begin;`/`rollback;` and never commits any of its fixtures.

No RLS has been added to this cluster; that remains explicitly Phase 3's work.

- **(migration FILE created, NOT run — new `backend/supabase/migrations/115_workspaces_foundation.sql`) Phase 1 approved; final two corrections applied; migration file created.** E approved the Phase 1 architecture, bootstrap procedure, empty `platform_admins` roster, slug `ensight`, and stop-and-resolve-manually preflight approach from `PRODUCT_PHASE1_PLAN.md` Revision 3, then requested two final narrow corrections before migration 115 could be created:
  1. **Every authorization helper now evaluates the authenticated caller internally via `auth.uid()`** instead of accepting an arbitrary acting-user id parameter -- `is_platform_admin()`, `is_workspace_admin(workspace_id)`, `is_workspace_member(workspace_id)`, `is_workspace_member_owner(member_id)`, and `can_manage_workspace_member(member_id)` all dropped their second parameter. An RLS policy only ever needs to ask "can the current caller do this" -- this also further reduces the RPC information-exposure trade-off Revision 3's security analysis had accepted, since a caller invoking one of these directly can now only ever learn something about themselves.
  2. **Test B corrected to test what it claims to test** -- it previously derived the "other" workspace's id through a `select id from workspaces where slug = ...` subquery, but `workspaces`' own RLS would have hidden that row from the simulated non-member admin, making a "0 rows updated" result ambiguous (blocked by the right policy, or just never found?). Fixed: both test workspaces get fixed, known UUID literals seeded inside the transaction, and the unauthorized update addresses the second workspace by that literal directly, with `returning *` so a real zero-row result is visibly an empty result set.

  **Migration 115 was then created** (`backend/supabase/migrations/115_workspaces_foundation.sql`) with both corrections applied throughout -- `workspaces`, `workspace_members`, `workspace_member_roles`, `platform_admins` tables; six hardened `security definer` helper functions (`search_path = ''`, fully schema-qualified, explicit grants to `authenticated` only, `auth.uid()` evaluated internally); RLS policies built entirely from those helpers (no raw subquery against an RLS-protected table anywhere); triggers; and the one-time data migration seeding exactly one `ensight` workspace and carrying over every existing `app_admins`/`app_user_roles` row with no fallback role ever assigned. `PRODUCT_PHASE1_PLAN.md` updated to Revision 4 (final) with the corrected schema, rollback, security analysis, and all nine executable tests (Test B rewritten, A/C-H unchanged from Revision 3).

  **Checks run after adding the migration file**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, 76 warnings -- unchanged baseline), `npm test` -- 95/95 passing (unchanged), `npm run test:smoke` -- 6/6 passing (unchanged). A `.sql` file under `backend/supabase/migrations/` is never part of the TypeScript/JS build or test suite, so these results simply confirm the addition introduced no regression elsewhere in the repository -- expected, and confirmed rather than assumed.
