- **(no code changes — plan revision only) `PRODUCT_PHASE1_PLAN.md` Revision 3: final hardening pass before implementation approval.** E approved Revision 2 conceptually (separated membership/role tables, slug `ensight`, no fallback role, manual Studio deployment, empty `platform_admins`, out-of-band bootstrap) and requested one more hardening pass before final approval. Migration 115 still does not exist -- this is a plan-document-only revision:
  1. **Every `security definer` function now sets `search_path = ''` and fully schema-qualifies every table reference** (`public.workspace_members`, `auth.users`, etc.), closing the search-path-injection class of vulnerability. Every function's default `PUBLIC` execute grant is explicitly revoked and re-granted only to `authenticated` -- stricter than this codebase's own pre-existing `is_app_admin()`/`has_role()`, which still carry Postgres's permissive default today (noted as a possible future retrofit, not touched in Phase 1).
  2. **`get_workspace_member_owner()`/`get_workspace_member_workspace()` (both returned a raw UUID) are replaced with boolean-only helpers** `is_workspace_member_owner(member_id, user_id)` and `can_manage_workspace_member(member_id, acting_user_id)` -- neither leaks an identifier, both answer only the yes/no question the calling policy needs.
  3. **Every executable test (A-G) is now a self-contained, transaction-wrapped script** that creates its own workspace/membership/role/platform-admin fixtures inside the transaction using real existing `auth.users` ids, and ends in `rollback` -- nothing requires a pre-existing fixture, nothing (including a temporary platform-admin grant in Test E) persists. Test G, which expects a real constraint-violation error, uses a PL/pgSQL exception handler (a documented Postgres pattern -- an exception block implicitly creates its own savepoint) so the expected failure is caught and confirmed without aborting the surrounding script.
  4. **Three preflight queries added**, to run against production before migration 115 is ever created: any user with more than one existing primary role (would violate the new real unique constraint), any role_key value outside the expected list, and confirmation every referenced user actually exists. Explicit instruction: stop and resolve manually if any return rows, never auto-resolve.
  5. **Workspace seeding fixed for a missing `company_branding` row**: restructured from `insert ... select ... from company_branding` (which would silently insert zero rows if that table were ever empty) to `insert ... values (coalesce((select ...), 'Ensight Technologies'), 'ensight')` -- a scalar subquery correctly evaluates to NULL when it matches no rows, so `coalesce` reliably falls back and exactly one workspace is always created.
  6. **Documented, not fixed**: the fixed 10-value `role_key` list is explicitly noted as transitional -- must become workspace-configurable before a second company can define its own roles, deliberately not expanded in Phase 1 since only one workspace exists today.
  7. **Recorded as a future requirement**: once `resolveActiveWorkspace()` is actually wired into a route (a later phase), it must reject a resolved workspace whose `status` is `'suspended'` -- written into the design snippet now so it isn't lost by the time it matters.

  **Still not implemented and still requiring E's approval before it is**: migration 115 has not been created; no SQL has been run against any database; no RLS policy, server route, or frontend file has changed. Three decisions remain per the plan's own "Decisions still needed" section: approve the hardened schema/functions/grants/policies, confirm the preflight-remediation approach (stop-and-resolve-manually, never auto-resolve), and give final go-ahead to create migration 115.

- **(no code changes — plan revision only) `PRODUCT_PHASE1_PLAN.md` Revision 2: E's structural/security review of Phase 1 addressed before migration 115 is created.** E reviewed Revision 1 and found eight real issues, none of which are implemented yet -- migration 115 still does not exist. Each is fixed in the plan document itself, not just noted:
  1. **Membership and operational roles were conflated.** Split into `workspace_members` (membership + `is_workspace_admin` only) and a new `workspace_member_roles` table (role assignments, one row per role, mirroring `app_user_roles`' existing shape).
  2. **A fallback `'manager'` role for an admin with no operational role was wrong and is removed entirely** -- a workspace admin can now correctly exist with zero rows in `workspace_member_roles`.
  3. **"One primary role" is now a real, enforced constraint** -- a `unique` partial index on `workspace_member_roles(workspace_member_id) where is_primary`, database-enforced for every writer, unlike `app_user_roles`' own non-unique version (migration `040`'s own comment admits that one is app-layer-only).
  4. **SQL statement order corrected**: all tables, then all helper functions, then RLS enable + policies, then triggers, then the one-time data migration -- Revision 1 had a policy referencing a function that didn't exist yet at that point in the script.
  5. **A self-referencing RLS policy on `workspace_members` (querying `workspace_members` from within its own policy) is redesigned around `security definer` helper functions** (`is_workspace_admin`, `is_workspace_member`, `get_workspace_member_owner`, `get_workspace_member_workspace`) for every cross-row check, with no raw subquery against an RLS-protected table left in any policy anywhere in the design -- this exactly mirrors the recursion-avoidance mechanism `is_app_admin()` already relies on in production today (table-owner/security-definer-owner bypass of a table's own RLS), not a new, unproven pattern.
  6. **A platform-admin bootstrap/recovery procedure is now documented**: direct SQL insert into `platform_admins` by E in Supabase Studio, mirroring how `app_admins` itself was originally bootstrapped (no in-app mechanism ever existed for that either). `platform_admins` still ships empty in Phase 1; no account, including the existing admin, is granted platform-admin status by this plan.
  7. **Workspace slug settled**: `'ensight'` -- Ergon is the product, Ensight is the first customer workspace.
  8. **Eight executable SQL test blocks added** (Tests A-H in `PRODUCT_PHASE1_PLAN.md`), one per scenario E named: an ordinary member reading only their own membership, a workspace admin managing members only in their own workspace, a non-admin unable to manage memberships, a user belonging to multiple workspaces, a platform admin's intended access, no RLS recursion (with the concrete failure mode named -- a stack-depth error or a hang), the primary-role constraint actually rejecting a second primary role, and existing Ergon behavior's row counts staying identical before/after. Written using Supabase's own documented `set local role authenticated; set local request.jwt.claims ...` SQL-editor convention for simulating a signed-in user.

  **Still not implemented and still requiring E's approval before it is**: migration 115 has not been created; no SQL has been run against any database; no RLS policy, server route, or frontend file has changed. `PRODUCT_PHASE1_PLAN.md`'s own "Decisions still needed" section lists the remaining approvals (the corrected schema itself, the bootstrap procedure, confirmation that no one is bootstrapped as a platform admin in Phase 1, and final go-ahead to create the migration file).

- **(no code changes — read-only audit) First Ergon productization work package: full tenant-isolation and configuration audit.** E: "Begin the first Ergon productization work package. Read PRODUCT_PLAN.md, PRODUCT_START_PLAN.md, and HANDOFF.md first. This is initially a read-only architecture and configuration audit. Do not change production code, database policies, migrations, workflows, or existing business processes during this audit."

  **What was produced**: `PRODUCT_TENANCY_AUDIT.md` (new, repo root) — a full evidence-based inventory covering all ten areas requested: single-company/singleton/hard-coded-company assumptions; which database records have explicit workspace ownership (none do); current RLS/API/Storage/search/notification/scheduled-job/cache/export/reporting boundaries; specific cross-company exposure mechanisms; the existing no-code configuration surface (Admin panel, panel by panel); routine configuration that still requires programming; configuration needing preview/versioning/audit/rollback/approval; a proposed tenant/workspace architecture that preserves all existing Ergon/Ensight data as the first workspace; a staged (not all-at-once) migration approach; and a consolidated list of decisions for E to make before any implementation begins. Also updated `PRODUCT_START_PLAN.md`'s "First deliverables" list, marking item 2 (the tenant-isolation gap audit) delivered with a link to the new document.

  **Method**: three parallel read-only research passes covered (1) all 114 SQL migration files in `backend/supabase/migrations/` — every `create table`, all 253 `create policy` statements individually classified, every `security definer` function, every storage bucket/policy; (2) every server route (`api/*.js`, `api/_lib/*.js`, `api/cron/*.js`) and `vercel.json`; (3) `src/main.tsx` (25,213 lines) and `src/persistence.ts` (10,680 lines) for hard-coded identity/branding, the fixed constant arrays that define app structure, the Admin page's real configurable surface, search, notifications, localStorage, and reporting/exports. Findings were cross-checked against each other (e.g. the DB pass's "no company_id column anywhere" was independently corroborated by the frontend pass's persistence.ts grep) and two additional targeted follow-up reads closed gaps the three passes didn't fully resolve on their own (confirming `company_branding`'s literal singleton-table SQL, and confirming "Client Ledger" has no dedicated table — it's a UI-computed view, not a schema object).

  **Headline finding**: zero of the 86 tables in the schema have any `company_id`/`workspace_id`/`tenant_id` column, `app_admins`/`app_user_roles` are both global with no company dimension (an admin is an admin of the entire database, not one company), and the dominant RLS pattern across most business tables is still fully open to any signed-in, approved user (`using (true)`/`with check (true)`) — 137 and 66 occurrences respectively across 253 policies. This is a deliberate, well-reasoned design for a single-company internal tool (many migrations' own comments say so explicitly), not an oversight — but it is the confirmed baseline `PRODUCT_START_PLAN.md`'s own tenant-isolation requirement (its principle #10: "Do not onboard a second company until tenant isolation has been designed, implemented, and independently verified") is measured against.

  **Most concrete, specific findings** (full detail and file:line citations in `PRODUCT_TENANCY_AUDIT.md` itself):
  - `company_branding` is a literal Postgres singleton (`id boolean primary key default true`, `check (id)`) — its own migration comment states "there is exactly one 'the company' for a given deployment of this app."
  - `get_users_by_role()`/`get_admin_emails()` — the actual recipient-resolution RPCs behind most notification types — return every matching user **app-wide**, with no company filter possible in their current signature. This is the single most concrete "would actually cross-notify" mechanism found.
  - 8 of 9 Supabase Storage buckets grant full read/write to any authenticated user, scoped only by `bucket_id` — any signed-in user could read/write any other company's project documents, sales-quote images, purchase-order files, etc., if two companies shared this deployment.
  - Global-uniqueness constraints (`team_members` email, `vendors`/`locations`/`clients` name, `sku`, `po_number`, `catalog_number`, `quote_ref`) would functionally block onboarding a second company today, not just leak data — e.g. two companies could never share a team member's email address.
  - Reference-number counters (`project_ref_counters`, `sales_quote_ref_counters`) are keyed only by calendar year, not company — would collide or interleave across companies.
  - Dashboard tiles ("Inventory Value," "Recent Order Spend") and several Reports panels aggregate raw `reduce()` sums over entire unscoped in-memory arrays — assessed as the single most *immediately visible* breakage if two companies' data ever shared these tables, since it would show as an obviously-wrong blended dollar figure to any user, not just a subtle permissions gap.
  - `support@ensight-technologies.com` is hard-coded into the Web Push VAPID subject (`api/send-push.js:200`); "Ergon Ops" is a hard-coded default/fixed sender identity across 5 email-related files; real EnSight legal proposal boilerplate (six paragraphs) is seeded as literal row data in `proposal_template_sections`; a dead-but-still-shipped array in `main.tsx` contains real client names and a real street address.
  - `localStorage` (`ergon:*` keys) was audited and explicitly assessed as **not** a real multi-tenant risk — already per-browser/per-user scoped; the real risk is entirely server-side/data-layer.

  **Positive/reusable findings**: six of the twelve existing Admin panels (Standard Install Times, Project Schedule Templates, both Form Builders, Pre-Sales Rules, Site Hardware Rules) have no hidden fixed list behind their primary function and are genuinely no-code today. The Catalog Price Change Requests workflow is a working example of the exact "protected approval" pattern `PRODUCT_PLAN.md` asks for more broadly — propose, review, approve/reject, with its own audit table — cited in the audit as the template to reuse elsewhere rather than reinventing.

  **Two items the audit explicitly did not guess at**: whether editing the Proposal Template or a Schedule Template retroactively changes already-signed quotes or already-scheduled projects was not confirmed by any of the three research passes, and is listed as an open verification item in the audit rather than assumed either way.

  **Proposed (not authorized) direction**: a `workspaces`/`workspace_members`/`platform_admins` model, added alongside (not replacing) the existing role-based RLS work, staged in 8 phases from fully-additive schema setup through the highest-risk singleton-table conversion to, only at the very end, building the actual new-company onboarding flow — deliberately sequenced so no second company is ever onboarded before isolation is proven. Full phase table and 12 open questions for E's decision are in `PRODUCT_TENANCY_AUDIT.md` itself (§9/§10).

  **Nothing was implemented.** No migration, RLS policy, server route, or frontend file was changed by this work. The only files touched were the two new/updated planning documents.

- **(`3f3da20`) Durable (Upstash Redis) rate limiting, replacing the in-memory-only limiter (HANDOFF Questions/Decisions item 11) + item 10 formally closed.** E: "Keep PM access to sales-quote-extract... Mark HANDOFF Questions/Decisions item 10 resolved with that decision. Next, replace the in-memory rate limiter with durable rate limiting so protection survives serverless cold starts and multiple Vercel instances. Prioritize paid AI extraction, invitations, and outbound email/Slack/push routes."

  **Item 10**: no code change -- the role list (`sales`/`pm`/`manager`+admin) was already correct from the 2026-09-06/07 passes. E's message is the final decision closing the open question; HANDOFF's item 10 updated to reflect that directly.

  **Item 11 -- what shipped**: `api/_lib/rateLimit.js` now backs onto Upstash Redis (`@upstash/redis`, added to `package.json`) via `INCR`+`EXPIRE` fixed-window counters when `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL`/`UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` are set -- the count lives in Redis, not one function instance's memory, so it's genuinely shared across cold starts, warm instances, and regions. `checkRateLimit(key, maxCount, windowMs)` kept its exact signature (now `async`), so every call site needed only a one-line `await` added: `sales-quote-extract.js` (the paid OpenAI extraction route -- E's first-named priority), `send-invite-email.js`, `send-proposal-email.js`, `send-submittal-email.js`, `send-notification-email.js`, `send-notification-slack.js`, `send-push.js` (E's "invitations" and "outbound email/Slack/push" priorities, all 6), and `create-notification.js` (not explicitly named in the priority list, but shares the identical helper -- left on the old mechanism it would've been the one inconsistent route). No route was skipped.

  **Honest fallback, matching every other optional integration in this app**: with no Redis connected -- true right now, see the action item below -- `checkRateLimit` transparently falls back to the exact original in-memory limiter, unchanged. Zero behavior change today; this is infrastructure ready to activate, not a redesign of what already worked. If Redis IS connected but errors on one call (network blip, an Upstash outage), that single call also falls back to in-memory rather than either blocking real traffic or silently applying no limit -- logged via `console.error`, task/key info only, the Upstash token never included (same secret-handling discipline as the CRON_SECRET logging work immediately above).

  **Trade-off accepted on purpose**: fixed window, not sliding -- a caller can get up to ~2x `maxCount` through right at a window boundary. A true sliding window needs more Redis round-trips per check; not worth it for the actual threat model here (a runaway script/retry loop, not a precisely-timed attacker), consistent with this rate limiter's original "one honest layer, not the whole story" framing.

  **Tests** (`tests/api/rateLimit.test.js`, new): in-memory fallback still enforces its count and keys stay independent (unchanged behavior, just re-verified); the Redis path verified against a mocked `@upstash/redis` client (correct `INCR`/`EXPIRE` calls, count enforced, `expire` only set once per key); and -- the one test that actually proves the point of this whole change -- two, then three, separate fresh module instances (`vi.resetModules()` between them, simulating real separate cold-started Vercel functions that share no process memory) all hitting the same mocked Redis backing store correctly see each other's counts and the third is correctly blocked, which the old in-memory-only version could never do by construction. A Redis-failure case confirms the fail-open behavior and that the failure log never contains the token. 95/95 passing (90 prior + 5 new).

  **Checks**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, 76 warnings -- same baseline), `npm test` -- 95/95 passing, `npm run test:smoke` -- 6/6 passing.

  **Action needed from E to actually activate durable rate limiting** (nothing is broken without this -- the app keeps working exactly as it does today until it's done):
  1. In the Vercel dashboard: `Project Settings > Storage > Connect Database`, add a Redis store (the Upstash Marketplace integration is the standard path here) to the `ergon-ops-app` project.
  2. Set `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL` and `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` as Production env vars (the Upstash/Vercel integration usually sets these automatically once connected -- confirm the exact names match if it uses different ones).
  3. Redeploy (same env-var-bakes-in-at-build-time requirement as `CRON_SECRET` -- Redeploy from the Deployments tab, no code change needed).
  4. Optional but recommended verification afterward: hit one of the 8 routes repeatedly past its configured limit from two different requests/sessions in quick succession and confirm the 429 total reflects a SHARED count, not double the limit -- the concrete, observable difference this upgrade makes.
  - I did not create the database or click through the Marketplace integration myself -- this is shared infrastructure/billing, the same category of action as `CRON_SECRET`, E's call to make.

- **(`772cf48`, `fa4c15f`) CRON_SECRET production rollout: full 7-point live verification of `api/cron/task-overdue.js`.** E: "CRON_SECRET has now been added to the Vercel Production environment and production has been redeployed. Please verify that: [7 points -- rejects no secret, rejects wrong secret, succeeds with the real secret, creates only correct notifications, no duplicates on retry, failures logged clearly without exposing the secret, cron config points to the correct endpoint]."

  **What shipped, before live-verifying**: reviewing the route for check #6 ("failures logged clearly") surfaced a real gap -- the route already rejected bad/missing secrets correctly, but on a genuine failure (a Supabase fetch error, a per-task insert error) nothing was ever written to Vercel's function logs; only the HTTP response carried that information, invisible unless someone opened that one specific invocation. `772cf48` added `console.error` on every real failure path (the overdue-task query itself failing, a per-task insert failing, missing `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`VITE_SUPABASE_URL`) and one `console.log` summary line per successful run (`scanned=N created=N`) -- every message logs only a task id and an HTTP status/error string, **never** `req.headers`, the `Authorization` header, or `CRON_SECRET` itself (documented inline as a standing rule for this file). The 401 path deliberately logs nothing -- a missing/wrong secret is routine, expected traffic, and there is no way to log "what was sent" there without risking the secret ending up in a log line by accident.

  **A second, real finding, caught by live-testing the first version of that logging change (not by static reading) before calling it done**: running the job twice via Vercel's own dashboard "Run" button showed the SECOND run logging `[cron/task-overdue] Failed to insert notification for task ...: HTTP 409` -- which looked like a real failure but is actually **expected, routine dedupe working correctly**. Root cause: PostgREST's `Prefer: resolution=ignore-duplicates` only silently no-ops a duplicate when the request also names its target via an `on_conflict=<columns>` query param; without one (as this route -- and `api/create-notification.js` -- both send it), a retried insert against an already-used `dedupe_key` is a genuine `23505` unique-constraint violation, not a quiet upsert. The dedupe *guarantee* was never wrong (the unique index, migration 024, still blocks the second row either way) -- only the brand-new logging was about to mislabel every single routine retried day as an alerting failure. `fa4c15f` fixes this: a `409` on insert is now treated as the benign, expected case (no log at all), while any *other* insert failure still logs clearly. This is a real, previously-unnoticed nuance in how the "ignore-duplicates" dedupe mechanism has actually been achieving its guarantee this whole session (via a swallowed constraint violation, not PostgREST's upsert semantics) -- functionally harmless (zero duplicate notifications, confirmed live below), but worth knowing if this insert pattern is ever touched again; `api/create-notification.js` has the identical pattern and the identical (silent, since it has no logging at all) behavior, untouched this pass since it wasn't part of what was asked.

  **Tests** (`tests/api/cron-task-overdue.test.js`): split the old combined "wrong secret" test into a dedicated missing-header case and a wrong-secret case (both assert the response never echoes the real secret); added a role-only-assignee-excluded case; rewrote the dedupe-holds test to assert the real `409` behavior (not the originally-assumed 201-empty-array) and that it does NOT call `console.error`; added a distinct genuine-non-409-failure case proving that one DOES log clearly, with the task id and status, never the secret. 90/90 passing (85 prior + 5 new/updated).

  **Checks**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, 76 warnings -- same baseline), `npm test` -- 90/90 passing, `npm run test:smoke` -- 6/6 passing.

  **Deployed and live-verified on production (`https://ergon-ops-app.vercel.app/`, deployed commit `fa4c15f` -- Vercel dashboard confirmed `Ready` and set as the current Production deployment) 2026-09-08, all 7 points E asked for:**
  1. **Rejects requests without the secret** -- `fetch('/api/cron/task-overdue')` with no `Authorization` header at all, from the live production origin: `401 {"error":"Unauthorized."}`.
  2. **Rejects an incorrect secret** -- same call with `Authorization: Bearer definitely-the-wrong-secret-value`: `401 {"error":"Unauthorized."}`. Neither response body contains anything resembling the real secret (nothing to leak either way -- the route only ever returns a fixed literal string on this path).
  3. **Succeeds with the configured secret** -- triggered via **Vercel's own Cron Jobs dashboard "Run" button** (`POST /api/v1/projects/.../crons/run`, Vercel's own infrastructure supplies the real `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET` internally) specifically so this session never had to see, type, or handle the real secret value at any point, per E's explicit instruction. Confirmed via Vercel's function logs (`Logs`, filtered to `requestPath:/api/cron/task-overdue`): `200`, message `[cron/task-overdue] scanned=1 created=1`.
  4. **Creates only the correct overdue-task notifications** -- cross-checked against the real Tasks list before running: exactly one task was genuinely overdue with a real individual assignee ("Document photos on-site," due 2026-08-21, assignee Abhi, status In Progress) -- a second overdue-looking task ("Request for Drawings support") is assigned to a *role* ("Engineering team"), which the route's own query (`assignee_email=not.is.null`) correctly excludes before the notification logic even runs. `scanned=1 created=1` matches exactly the one real qualifying task -- not the two overdue-looking rows, not zero.
  5. **Repeated execution does not create duplicates** -- ran it twice more via the same dashboard "Run" button. Both: `200`, `[cron/task-overdue] scanned=1 created=0` -- the same task, same day, same dedupe key, zero new rows both times, and (post-`fa4c15f`) neither logged as an error.
  6. **Failures are logged clearly without exposing the secret** -- verified two ways: (a) the unit tests above assert a genuine failure logs the task id + HTTP status and never the secret string, while a routine 409 dedupe logs nothing; (b) live, the real successful/duplicate runs above show exactly the expected `scanned=`/`created=` summary lines in Vercel's actual function logs, and the two live 401 rejections show no message body beyond the fixed `Unauthorized.` string -- nothing secret-shaped appears anywhere in the real log stream at any point during this verification.
  7. **The scheduled Vercel cron configuration points to the correct endpoint** -- Vercel's own Cron Jobs settings page (`Project Settings > Cron Jobs`) lists exactly one job: `/api/cron/task-overdue`, schedule `0 13 * * *` ("At 01:00 PM" UTC), Enabled -- matching `vercel.json`'s `crons` entry exactly, confirming the deployed config was picked up correctly, not just what the repo file says it *should* be.

  **Net result: CRON_SECRET is fully configured, deployed, and live-verified end to end.** The scheduled job will now run for real at its next 13:00 UTC window (the flexible 1-hour Hobby-tier window applies) and correctly notify assignees of genuinely overdue tasks, exactly once per task per day, with clean logs on both success and real failure. This closes the last outstanding item from HANDOFF Questions/Decisions item 9.

- **(`f0465de`) DM push deep-linking to the exact conversation (HANDOFF Questions/Decisions item 12).** E, after confirming migration 114's success: "move on to the next product improvement: make DM push notifications open the exact conversation instead of only opening the Messages hub... preserve authorization and deduplication."

  **What shipped**: a DM push's `url` is now `/#messages/<conversationId>` instead of the generic `/#messages`. Three small, minimal changes, no new infrastructure:
  - `api/_lib/directMessage.js` -- `resolveDirectMessage()` already loaded the real `direct_messages` row (to verify the caller is its real sender) and already had `conversation_id` in hand from that same query; it just wasn't being returned. Now it is (`conversationId` added to the success return shape).
  - `api/send-push.js` -- `resolveDirectMessagePush()` builds `url: `/#messages/${resolved.conversationId}`` instead of the hardcoded `/#messages`. The id is exclusively the one `resolveDirectMessage` derived from the real stored row -- nothing from the request body reaches this value at any point (a new test, `tests/api/send-push.test.js`, confirms a request that also names a `conversationId` field is ignored -- the real one from the message row wins).
  - `main.tsx` -- one new route effect, right next to `selectConversation()` (the existing "remember last thread" state), reading `#messages/<id>` from `window.location.hash` on mount and on `hashchange`, calling `selectConversation(id)` when present. Deliberately does nothing when the hash is a bare `#messages` (clicking the nav tab) -- only an actual id segment triggers a selection, so this never fights the existing "restore whichever DM/channel was open last" behavior. Mirrors the app's own existing `#projects/<slug>` route (`handleProjectRoute`) rather than introducing a new URL scheme (a query param was the option sketched in the superseded HANDOFF entry, but the hash-segment pattern already existed in this codebase and reads more consistently next to it).
  - `public/sw.js` -- **confirmed no change needed.** Its `notificationclick` handler already calls `client.navigate(targetUrl)` / `clients.openWindow(targetUrl)` with whatever `url` the push payload names; deep-linking was purely a "the payload never named a specific place" gap, not a service-worker gap. Read the file start to finish to confirm this before touching anything, rather than assuming.

  **Authorization — no new server-side check needed, and confirmed why**: `resolveDirectMessage` already requires the caller be the message's own real `sender_id`, verified via `direct_messages`' own RLS (migration 094) using the caller's own token -- a `conversationId` only ever comes back for a conversation the caller genuinely sent a message in. On the client side, opening a `#messages/<id>` link for a conversation the current viewer isn't part of degrades safely to the app's existing "no conversation selected" state: `Messages`' `activeConversation` is looked up in the `conversations` array (`conversations.find((entry) => entry.id === activeConversationId)`), and that array is itself already scoped server-side to the real signed-in participant (`loadConversations`'s own RLS-backed query) -- a foreign/bogus id just isn't found there, exactly like it already behaved for any other bad id before this change. No new client-side validation was added on purpose -- it would only be redundant with (and weaker than) the RLS that already does the real job.

  **Deduplication — deliberately untouched, and why that was the correct call, not an oversight**: the more "consistent" fix would have also changed the `notifications` row's `related_entity_id` for a DM event to the conversation id (matching its `related_entity_type: "conversation"`, which currently pairs with the *message* id instead) -- rejected once traced through, because `api/create-notification.js`'s dedupe key is `${eventType}:${relatedEntityId}:${email}`, built from that exact field. Switching it to a per-conversation id would mean every message ever sent in an ongoing conversation collapses onto the SAME dedupe key -- only the very first DM in that conversation would ever notify its recipient again, silently breaking real delivery for a "cleaner" data model. `send-push.js`'s DM-mode branch gets `conversationId` straight from `resolveDirectMessage`'s own return value instead, entirely independent of the notification row -- zero change to dedupe behavior, confirmed by the unchanged dedupe-relevant tests in `tests/api/create-notification.test.js` still passing untouched.

  **Tests**: `tests/api/send-push.test.js` -- updated the existing DM-mode happy-path assertion (`payload.url` is now `/#messages/c1`, the real conversation id from the mocked `direct_messages` row, not `/#messages`); added one new test proving a request-supplied `conversationId` field is ignored in favor of the real one derived from the message (`/#messages/real-conversation`, not `/#messages/attacker-picked-conversation`).

  **Checks**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, 76 warnings -- same baseline as the prior entry, no new warnings from the new `main.tsx` effect), `npm test` -- 85/85 passing (84 prior + 1 new), `npm run test:smoke` -- 6/6 passing (unchanged, confirms no navigation/mobile-shell/auth-gate regression).

  **Deployed and live-verified on production (`https://ergon-ops-app.vercel.app/`, deployed commit `70eb552` -- Vercel dashboard confirmed `Ready` and set as the current Production deployment) 2026-09-08:**
  - **Cold load**: navigated directly to `/#messages/<realConversationId>` (a real "Nate Wolfe PM" conversation id, read from the app's own `ergon:last-thread` localStorage key) as a fresh page load, starting from a different conversation ("Abhi Manager") stored as the prior last-open thread. **Result: the app opened directly on Nate Wolfe PM, not the previously-remembered Abhi conversation or the bare Messages hub** -- proves the mount-time half of the route effect.
  - **Already-open tab (the realistic push-tap case)**: with the app already running and Abhi's conversation on screen, changed `window.location.hash` to the same `#messages/<id>` value without a page reload (simulating exactly what the service worker's `client.navigate(targetUrl)` does to an already-open tab). **Result: the UI switched live to Nate Wolfe PM** -- proves the `hashchange` half of the route effect, the more common real-world path since most push taps land on an already-open or backgrounded tab rather than a fully closed app.
  - **Bogus/foreign conversation id (authorization safety net)**: while testing, a hash value was set to a nonexistent conversation id. **Result: the UI showed the existing "Select a conversation, or start a new one" empty state -- no crash, no data leak, no broken UI** -- a live, real confirmation (not just a code-reading argument) that an unauthorized or invalid deep link degrades exactly the way the "Authorization" reasoning above predicted.
  - **Nav-tab regression check**: after a valid deep link had set the active conversation, navigated away (Tasks) and back to Messages via the normal top-nav icon (which sets a bare `#messages`, no id). **Result: Nate Wolfe PM stayed selected** -- confirms the new effect does not fight the pre-existing "remember last thread" restore behavior, exactly as designed.
  - **End-to-end pipeline regression check**: sent one more real DM from the live UI. `POST /api/create-notification -> 200` (correct real recipient, `nate@ensight-technologies.com`), `POST /api/send-push -> 200` (`{"sent":false,"reason":"...no push subscriptions..."}` -- expected, no subscribed device in this session, not an error). Confirms the server-side `url` change didn't break the notification/push pipeline itself.
  - **Console**: `read_console_messages` (errors-only filter) clean throughout all of the above.
  - **Not independently verified this pass** (same documented environment limitation as the earlier physical-device push work, "Current setup truth" above): the *exact* `url` value actually delivered inside a real push payload to a real device, and a real OS-notification tap landing on the right conversation. This remote/automated browser session cannot display OS-level push notifications at all (confirmed in an earlier session), so that last hop still needs a real device -- everything provable without one (server-side `url` construction, unit-tested; client-side routing, now live-verified above) has been.

- **(`f4b877f`) Trusted notification-creation path — closes the `notifications` INSERT gap for good (HANDOFF Questions/Decisions #9).** E: "Close the open notifications INSERT-policy gap... Design and implement a trusted notification-creation path... Audit every current createNotification() call site and migrate it to the trusted path. Do not leave a hidden client-side fallback."

  **Permission matrix — every event type, what it checks, where recipients come from:**

  | event_type | authorization | recipients | content source |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `task_assigned` | any signed-in user (matches `tasks`' own fully-open RLS) | real `tasks.assignee_email`/`assigned_role_key` (re-fetched) | real task row |
  | `task_status_changed` | any signed-in user (same) | assignee/role + `tasks.created_by_email`, one combined notification (was two separate ones client-side) | real task row |
  | `task_overdue` | **none -- no longer client-triggered at all** | `tasks.assignee_email` for every genuinely overdue task | scanned server-side, daily cron |
  | `purchase_request_status_changed` | `purchasing` role or admin (matches `purchase_requests`' real write RLS) | requester + `purchasing` role | real purchase_requests row |
  | `build_stage_changed` | `warehouse` role or admin (matches `build_transactions`' real write RLS) | `warehouse` role | real build row + a fixed 5-value stage enum (see the data-model limitation noted in item 9 above) |
  | `low_stock_reached` | any signed-in user; the low-stock claim itself is independently re-verified (real on-hand minus allocated vs. reorder point, not trusted from the client) | `purchasing` + `warehouse` roles + all admins | real inventory_items/inventory_balances rows |
  | `direct_message_received` | caller must be the message's real `sender_id` (direct_messages' own RLS, caller's own token) | the conversation's real other participant | real direct_messages row |
  | `mentioned` (task/channel_message/canvas) | task: open (matches `tasks`); channel_message: real RLS -- a non-member can't even read the row; canvas: open (matches `channel_canvas`'s own real RLS) | re-parsed `@Role`/`@FirstName` from the re-fetched real source text | real task/message/canvas row |
  | `catalog_price_change_requested` | caller must be the request's own real `requested_by_email` (matches that table's real insert RLS) | `manager` role | real catalog_price_change_requests + product_catalog rows |
  | `catalog_price_change_reviewed` | `manager` role or admin (matches that table's real review RLS) | the request's real `requested_by_email` | real (post-review) request row |
  | `user_signup_pending` | caller can only report their own real pending `app_user_status` row (self `auth.uid()` only) | every admin | real app_user_status row |
  | `quote_proposal_responded`, `submittal_responded` | **untouched by this pass** -- already fully server-side security-definer RPCs (`respond_to_quote_proposal`/`respond_to_submittal`, migrations 054/055), no client call site exists for either |

  **What shipped, file by file:**
  - `api/create-notification.js` (new) -- the only route that can write `notifications`. Body: `{eventType, relatedEntityId, relatedEntityType?, stage?}` or `{directMessageId}`. Validates `eventType` against an explicit allowlist, dispatches to the per-event handler, inserts one row per real derived recipient via the service-role key with `resolution=ignore-duplicates` (same dedupe mechanism `createNotification()` always used, just no longer client-reachable) -- a caller retrying the same real event gets the same rows, not duplicates.
  - `api/_lib/notificationEvents.js` (new) -- the dispatch table above, one handler per event type, ~350 lines. Reuses two existing security-definer SQL functions (`get_users_by_role`, `get_admin_emails`, migrations 042/049) instead of reimplementing role-membership lookups.
  - `api/_lib/directMessage.js` (new) -- the DM sender/recipient verification, extracted out of `api/send-push.js` (which had it inline) so both that route and the new `create-notification.js` share exactly one implementation instead of two copies drifting apart.
  - `api/cron/task-overdue.js` (new) + `vercel.json` (new) -- `task_overdue` moved from a client `useEffect` (ran in every signed-in user's browser, no real "acting user" concept, scanned whatever tasks happened to be loaded) to a real daily Vercel Cron job, `CRON_SECRET`-gated (no user session exists for a scheduled job). Schedule: `0 13 * * *` (13:00 UTC, once daily -- Vercel Hobby tier's own limit).
  - `main.tsx` -- `notify()` and every one of its wrapper helpers (`notifyRoleAssignment`, `notifyCurrentTaskAssignees`, `resolveMentionEmails`, `notifyPurchaseRequestStatusChanged`, `notifyBuildStageChanged`, `notifyLowStockReached`) replaced by one `triggerNotification(eventType, relatedEntityId, options?)`, which posts only the event/entity reference and dispatches email/Slack/push exactly as the 2026-09-07 pass already built (unchanged). `notifyMentions(text, relatedEntityType, relatedEntityId, sourceLabel, dedupeSuffix)` kept as a thin compatibility wrapper (same signature, `text`/`sourceLabel`/`dedupeSuffix` now unused) so the `onNotifyMentions` prop threaded through `ChannelDiscussion` in 4 places didn't need touching. Every real call site (task create/update, 4x purchase-request status changes, build stage, 5x low-stock, catalog price request/approve/reject, user sign-up, DM send) now calls `triggerNotification(...)` with just the entity id -- no more client-constructed title/body/recipient anywhere. **A real, separate correctness fix found along the way**: the old `user_signup_pending` path used the SAME `dedupe_key` for every admin being notified about one sign-up (`user_signup_pending:${userId}`, no recipient in the key) -- since `notifications.dedupe_key` has a single table-wide unique index, only the FIRST admin (in iteration order) could have ever actually gotten a row; every other admin's insert silently no-op'd as a "duplicate" that wasn't. Not something this pass introduced, but fixed as part of rebuilding this path (every dedupe key generated server-side now always includes the recipient's own email).
  - `persistence.ts` -- `createNotification()` deleted entirely (was the direct client-token insert this whole gap was about) -- not deprecated, not kept as an unused fallback, actually removed, per E's explicit "do not leave a hidden client-side fallback."

  **Migration** (`backend/supabase/migrations/114_trusted_notification_creation.sql`) -- **deliberately NOT run yet.** Sequenced this way on purpose: the new route uses the service-role key, which already bypasses RLS regardless of the `authenticated`-role policy's state, so the app works correctly against the CURRENT (pre-114) schema the moment this code deploys -- there's no need to run this first, and real risk in running it too early (if any call site had been missed, tightening RLS before deploying would have broken that flow with no fallback). Run this only after E confirms the checklist a few lines down.
    ```sql
    alter table notifications add column if not exists created_by uuid references auth.users(id);

    drop policy if exists "authenticated create notifications" on notifications;
    ```
    (This is the same file already sitting in the repo at that path -- paste is here too since it's short and this is the exact SQL to run, not a summary of it.) After running: `created_by` starts as null on all new rows until a trivial one-line follow-up (`created_by: user.id` in `api/create-notification.js`'s insert call, intentionally left out of this pass -- see that file's own comment for why) adds it back in. The `authenticated` role has NO INSERT policy on `notifications` after this runs -- only the service-role key (used exclusively by `api/create-notification.js`) can write it.

  **Tests** (`tests/api/create-notification.test.js`, `tests/api/cron-task-overdue.test.js`, new -- 28 tests): every event type's authorized-happy-path and unauthorized-actor case where a real role/relationship check applies; a caller attempting to override recipient/title/body via extra request-body fields (proven ignored, both for a role-based event and for direct_message_received); an unsupported event type and the two RPC-only event types (`quote_proposal_responded`/`submittal_responded`) both correctly rejected here; a missing `relatedEntityId`; the exact-same-event-retried-twice case (second call creates zero new rows, confirmed against a mocked dedupe-key-conflict response); the cron route's secret-gate and a real overdue-scan-and-notify pass. Deliberately not covering: literally re-testing Postgres RLS from Node (that's what live-verification against the real deployed DB is for, see below) -- "arbitrary direct notification INSERT is rejected" is verified by the code architecture itself (no other path writes this table) plus a real post-migration live check.

  **Checks**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, 76 warnings -- same baseline pattern as every prior pass, +2 from line-number shifts elsewhere in the file, not new issues), `npm test` -- 84/84 passing (56 prior + 28 new), `npm run test:smoke` -- 6/6 passing (unchanged, confirms no navigation/mobile-shell/auth-gate regression).

  2. ~~Confirm the deploy is live and working~~ -- **done, see live-verification below.**
  3. ~~Then run migration 114's SQL~~ -- **✅ RUN by E 2026-09-08, "Success. No rows returned."** The `authenticated` role now has no INSERT policy on `notifications` at all -- only the service-role key (used exclusively by `api/create-notification.js`) can write this table from this point forward. See the second live-verification pass below, done immediately after E confirmed the migration ran, before this was called done.
  4. ~~Re-verify afterward~~ -- **done, see the post-migration live-verification pass below.**

  **Live-verified on production (`https://ergon-ops-app.vercel.app/`, deployed commit `34429cb` -- Vercel dashboard confirmed this deployment `Ready` and set as the current Production deployment) BEFORE asking E to run migration 114 (pre-migration pass):**
  - **`task_assigned`**: created a real test task assigned to Abhi, then re-edited its assignee to Nate Wolfe from the live UI. Both edits fired `POST /api/create-notification -> 200`. Captured the second response directly (patched `window.fetch` in-page to read the real body, not guessed): `{"created":[{"id":"127a2620-...","recipientEmail":"nate@ensight-technologies.com"}]}` -- the recipient matches the real new assignee, proving the route re-derives it server-side from the actual `tasks` row rather than trusting anything client-supplied.
  - **`mentioned`** (task source): the same test task's description included `@Nate`; its own `create-notification` call also returned 200, consistent with the mention resolving against the re-fetched real task description.
  - **`direct_message_received`**: sent a real DM to the existing "Nate Wolfe PM" conversation from the live Messages UI. Captured response: `{"created":[{"id":"2da96e13-...","recipientEmail":"nate@ensight-technologies.com"}]}` -- correct conversation partner, derived from `direct_messages`/`conversations`, not client-supplied. The downstream `POST /api/send-push` call also fired (200, `{"sent":false,"reason":"...no push subscriptions..."}` -- expected, no subscribed device on this session). The identical notification `id` appeared twice in the captured trace for this one send (a double-fire from the in-page fetch patch, not two real sends) -- both resolved to the exact same row, which is itself a live confirmation that the dedupe-key/`resolution=ignore-duplicates` mechanism is working correctly against production, not just in tests.
  - **`task_status_changed`**: confirmed this rule's own "Active" checkbox is unchecked in Admin > Notification Rules (`ruleActive()` correctly short-circuits before any fetch) -- an administratively disabled rule, not a bug; did not force this one live since flipping E's own notification-rule configuration wasn't asked for.
  - Also confirmed **migration 114 has NOT been run** (no schema change made) and the app is fully functional against the pre-migration schema, exactly as designed.
  - **Not yet cleaned up**: a test task ("Verify create-notification live (task_assigned test)", assigned to Nate Wolfe) and two test DMs are still live in production data -- harmless, but E may want to delete them. Attempting to delete the test task from the Edit modal triggered a native `confirm()` dialog that hung this session's remote-browser automation (unrelated to the notification work) -- did not force past it rather than risk a bad click; left the task in place instead of retrying blind.

  **Live-verified on production AGAIN 2026-09-08, immediately after E ran migration 114 in Supabase Studio ("Success. No rows returned.") -- the full 7-point check E asked for, in order:**
  1. **A direct authenticated client INSERT into `notifications` is now genuinely rejected.** Reproduced the exact pre-fix exploit shape live: `POST` straight to `{supabaseUrl}/rest/v1/notifications` using the signed-in user's own real access token and the app's own real anon `apikey` (captured in-page from a real outgoing request, never printed/returned -- consistent with this environment's credential-handling rules), with a fully attacker-shaped payload (spoofed recipient, spoofed title, spoofed body). **Result: `403`, `{"code":"42501","message":"new row violates row-level security policy for table \"notifications\""}`.** Before migration 114 this exact same request would have silently succeeded (`with check (true)`) -- this is the concrete, live proof the gap named in this item's own title is now actually closed at the database level, not just "the app doesn't call that path anymore."
  2. **`task_assigned` still creates real notifications through `POST /api/create-notification`.** Re-assigned the same test task to Abhi (previously assigned to Nate) -- `200`, but `created: []`, because Abhi had already received a `task_assigned` notification for this exact task in the pre-migration pass and `task_assigned` has no extra dedupe scoping -- **this is dedupe correctly doing its job, not a failure** (see point 6). To get an unambiguous positive case, created a brand-new task assigned to Abhi: `200`, `{"created":[{"id":"2865d107-...","recipientEmail":"abhi.gaddam@ensight-technologies.com"}]}` -- a real new row, service-role insert working correctly with the `authenticated` policy gone.
  3. **@mentions still create notifications.** Covered by the same pre-migration test's `mentioned` call (200) plus the architecture itself -- `mentioned` shares the exact same insert path (`api/create-notification.js` -> service-role key) as every other event type verified working post-migration; no event-type-specific behavior differs by insert mechanism.
  4. **Direct messages still create notifications.** Sent a fresh real DM to the "Nate Wolfe PM" conversation: `POST /api/create-notification -> 200`, `{"created":[{"id":"2a9b9177-...","recipientEmail":"nate@ensight-technologies.com"}]}` -- correct conversation partner, and `POST /api/send-push -> 200` fired right after (no subscription on this session, expected).
  5. **Recipient and content remain fully server-derived**, not client-overridable -- every one of the above responses shows a recipient the client never sent (only `eventType`/`relatedEntityId`/`stage` or `directMessageId` are ever posted from `main.tsx`), matching the real stored assignee/conversation-partner data, exactly as the pre-migration pass already proved -- migration 114 changes who's *allowed* to write the table, not this derivation logic, so this was expected to be unaffected and was.
  6. **Dedupe still works, live, post-migration.** Two separate proofs in this same pass: (a) reassigning the test task back to a previously-notified recipient (Abhi) correctly returned zero new rows instead of a duplicate; (b) the brand-new task's notification id (`2865d107-...`) and the new DM's id (`2a9b9177-...`) each came back identical across every one of the 3-4 duplicate `fetch` calls this session's own in-page instrumentation happened to fire per action (a quirk of the verification tooling re-invoking the patched `fetch`, not the app) -- same real dedupe-key/`resolution=ignore-duplicates` mechanism, unaffected by dropping the `authenticated` INSERT policy since the service-role key was always the one performing the actual insert.
  7. **No console or network errors during any of this.** `read_console_messages` (errors-only filter): clean. Network trace: every `/api/create-notification` and `/api/send-push` call `200`; the only non-2xx anywhere in the trace was a `503` on `POST task_activity_log` -- the same pre-existing, unrelated transient Supabase blip already documented in the `e890698` work-log entry below (task-activity audit logging, not notifications), not a new regression from this pass.

  **Net result: migration 114 is fully applied and live-verified. The `notifications` table can now only be written by the service-role key. There is no remaining path -- client-side code or a raw authenticated REST call -- that can fabricate a notification row.** The only item left open from this whole priority is `CRON_SECRET` (a Vercel env var only E can set); everything else in HANDOFF Questions/Decisions item 9 is closed.

- **(`e890698`) Server-side authorization hardening for every outbound notification/communication route -- a deeper pass than 2026-09-06's, closing gaps that pass explicitly left open.** E: "Authentication alone is insufficient. Enforce appropriate permissions server-side," with explicit recommended defaults and a required test matrix. Full permission matrix, what changed, and why:

  | Route | Before this pass | After this pass |
  |---|---|---|
  | `send-invite-email.js` | Admin-only (`requireRole([])`), `isAllowedAppUrl` on invite link | Unchanged, added rate limit |
  | `send-notification-email.js` | Any signed-in user; recipient had to be a known Ergon user; **title/body/recipient fully caller-supplied** | Any signed-in user (unchanged); takes only `notificationId`, loads recipient/subject/body from the real `notifications` row (service-role key) -- caller-supplied content is ignored entirely; added rate limit |
  | `send-notification-slack.js` | Any signed-in user; `slackUserId` had to be on file; **title/body fully caller-supplied** | Same role posture; takes only `notificationId`, derives `slackUserId` from the notification's own `recipient_email` server-side (caller never names one); content from the stored row; added rate limit |
  | `send-push.js` | Any signed-in user; `userId` had to be a known Ergon user; **title/body/url/recipient fully caller-supplied**, most serious finding in the 2026-09-06 audit | Two modes: `{directMessageId}` (real DM -- caller's own token proves they're the message's actual sender via `direct_messages`' RLS, recipient derived from the conversation's other participant, content from the message itself) or `{notificationId}` (everything else, same server-side-lookup pattern as email/Slack); no `userId`/`title`/`body`/`url` accepted from the caller at all anymore; added rate limit |
  | `send-proposal-email.js` | Any signed-in user, no role check; **clientEmail/clientName/siteName/quoteRef fully caller-supplied** | `requireRole(["sales","pm","manager"])`; takes only `proposalId` + `shareUrl`, derives client/site/quote data from the real `sales_quote_proposals` row (caller's own token); added rate limit |
  | `send-submittal-email.js` | Any signed-in user, no role check; **clientEmail/clientName/projectName/projectRef fully caller-supplied** | `requireRole(["pm"])` (matches `project_submittals`' own write RLS exactly -- manager was never actually able to use this feature even before this change, since the DB write already blocked it); takes only `submittalId` + `shareUrl`, derives client/project data from the real `project_submittals` row; added rate limit |
  | `sales-quote-extract.js` | `requireRole(["sales","pm","manager"])` (from 2026-09-06) | Unchanged role list -- **deliberately kept `pm` despite the task's literal recommended default (sales/manager/admin only), see Questions/Decisions item 10** -- added rate limit |

  **What's still open, on purpose, not silently dropped**: `notifications`' own INSERT policy remains fully open (any signed-in user can still write a fabricated notification row addressed to anyone) -- this session closed "the delivery channel can diverge from the record," not "the record can be fabricated in the first place." See Questions/Decisions item 9 for the full reasoning and the two follow-up options (a `created_by` column + RLS tightening, or moving notification creation server-side event-by-event). Direct-message push is the one path fully closed end-to-end, because `direct_messages` already had real participant-scoped RLS to derive from -- it was both the most concretely-specified ask and the one place a complete fix was actually achievable without a schema change or a much larger rewrite.

  **Client-side changes** (`main.tsx`), all mechanical, no behavior change for a legitimate user: `notify()` now posts `{notificationId}` (or `{directMessageId}` for the push leg of a DM) instead of raw title/body/recipient fields; `handleSendDirectMessage` passes the real message id through; `handleCreateQuoteProposal`/`handleCreateSubmittal` post `{proposalId, shareUrl}`/`{submittalId, shareUrl}` instead of the quote/project fields they used to send directly (those fields still exist locally for the on-screen UI and the already-existing `createQuoteProposal`/`createSubmittal` calls that write the real row in the first place -- only the *email-sending* request body changed).

  **New shared server helpers**: `api/_lib/notificationLookup.js` (`loadNotificationById`/`hasExistingDelivery`, both via the service-role key -- see its own header comment for the full "why service role" reasoning), `api/_lib/rateLimit.js` (in-memory sliding window, see Questions/Decisions item 11 for its honest durability limits).

  **Tests added** (`tests/api/*.test.js`, new -- this repo had zero server-route test coverage before today): 47 new tests across `send-push.test.js` (13), `send-notification-email.test.js` (7), `send-notification-slack.test.js` (5), `send-proposal-email.test.js` (6), `send-submittal-email.test.js` (6), `send-invite-email.test.js` (5), `sales-quote-extract.test.js` (7, auth-gate focused -- this route's PDF-parsing internals aren't exercised). Each file covers, where applicable to that route: signed-out caller (401), signed-in but unauthorized role/relationship (403), authorized caller (200, real delivery), a caller attempting to target another user/recipient arbitrarily (proven ignored -- the test asserts the *stored* data was delivered, not the spoofed request body), an invalid/nonexistent entity id (404), and rate-limit behavior (429 after exceeding the window). Mocking approach: `tests/api/_test-helpers.js` provides a minimal `req`/`res` pair and a URL-substring-dispatching `fetch` mock (`mockFetchRouter`) so each test describes only the Supabase REST calls it cares about; `web-push`/`api/_lib/mailer.js` are `vi.mock()`'d so no real push/email attempt happens.

  **Checks**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, 74 pre-existing warnings, unchanged), `npm test` -- 56/56 passing (9 pre-existing + 47 new), `npm run test:smoke` -- 6/6 passing (unchanged, confirms no regression in navigation/mobile-shell/auth-gate from these changes).

  **⚠️ First production deploy attempt (commit `22e3c99`) failed -- caught and fixed before calling this done, real incident, not swept under the rug.** The test files were originally written directly under `api/` (alongside the real routes, matching where they test). Vercel's zero-config Node runtime treats **every top-level `.js` file directly under `api/` as its own serverless function route** -- exactly how `api/send-push.js` etc. already work -- and a `*.test.js` file with no `export default function handler(req, res)` broke that at the "Deploying outputs" stage (the Vite client build itself succeeded cleanly; the failure was Vercel's separate function-bundling step, confirmed by reading the actual Vercel deployment log via the dashboard, not guessed from the generic "Build Failed" banner alone). Files already prefixed with `_` (like `_lib/`) are exempt by Vercel's own convention -- a `*.test.js` name isn't. **Fix**: moved all 7 test files plus their shared `_test-helpers.js` to `tests/api/` (new directory, mirrors the existing `tests/smoke/` convention), fixed their relative imports back into `api/`, updated `vitest.config.ts`'s `include` to `tests/api/**/*.test.js`, and added a `tests/api/**/*.js` block to `eslint.config.js` (Node + vitest globals -- these files weren't linted at all in their old location, since `api/**` is eslint-ignored; moving them out surfaced one real `no-undef`/unused-var pair to fix). Re-verified clean: `tsc -b`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint` (0 errors), `npm test` (56/56, same tests, new location), `npm run test:smoke` (6/6). Fixed in `d754a8c` -- that's the commit that actually deployed successfully, not `22e3c99`.

  **Live-verified on production (`https://ergon-ops-app.vercel.app/`) after the corrected deploy (`2ab81f0`, confirmed Ready in the Vercel dashboard)**: fetched the live bundle and confirmed it contains `notificationId`/`directMessageId`/`proposalId`/`submittalId` (all four -- the earlier broken bundle only had two, which is what first tipped this off as a real staleness/deploy problem, not a false alarm). Called `/api/send-push` directly with the **old, now-removed request shape** (`{userId, title, body}`, no `directMessageId`/`notificationId`) and confirmed it's rejected (`400`, "directMessageId or notificationId is required") -- proves the tightened contract is actually enforced live, not just in tests. Sent two real DMs to a real conversation (Nate Wolfe PM) from the live UI: both displayed correctly with no console errors; the second one's network trace shows the real sequence working end to end -- `POST direct_messages -> 201`, `POST notifications -> 201`, `POST /api/send-push -> 200`, matching exactly the new derived-server-side flow. (One unrelated, pre-existing call in that same trace -- the client's own `recordNotificationDelivery` audit-trail write, `POST notification_deliveries` -- came back `503`, a transient Supabase blip on code this session didn't touch; it only affects delivery bookkeeping, not the actual push send, which had already succeeded by that point.) Also reloaded Dashboard: "Cloud synced" pill, no new errors, confirms no regression from this session's changes.



- **(`257dc03`) Data-trust/recovery, mobile-shell crash fixes, a lint/test/smoke baseline, and a server-side authorization audit — a large multi-priority session, all live-verified where a live target existed.** Full task spec was 7 ordered priorities (data trust, mobile shell, dashboard hierarchy, test/lint baseline, API authorization, incremental decomposition, this doc). Summary by priority:
  1. **Silent-failure fix (the exact bug named in the task).** `loadInventoryMovements`/`loadProjectDocuments` (`persistence.ts`) no longer swallow a failed request into `[]`. Added a `criticalLoadErrors` registry + `DataLoadErrorBanner` component (now its own file, `src/components/DataLoadErrorBanner.tsx`) + a degraded "Sync issue (N)" top-nav pill state. Reports > Activity Ledger and Reports > Documents now show a real inline error + Retry instead of a fake empty state when a critical loader fails (068/070 were still not live when this was built, and are now fixed as of later the same day -- see "Current setup truth" above; this error-handling code stays valuable for any future data-load failure, not just that one). New test file `src/persistence.critical-loaders.test.ts` (9 tests: success/empty/failure for both loaders, including the existing 400-column self-heal retry behavior for Documents, which was deliberately preserved not removed). Push-subscription errors were already surfaced visibly before this session (`handleSubscribeToPush`'s catch -> `messagesStatus` -> rendered in Messages) -- checked, not re-built.
  2. **Mobile shell: two real crash bugs found and fixed, plus the reported nav/install-banner bugs.** Live-verified at 390x844 and desktop (not just reasoned about):
     - **`.nav-list` (desktop tab strip) was visible as a horizontally-clipped strip on mobile, on top of the real bottom nav** -- its mobile override was `order: 3; width: 100%` instead of actually hiding it. Fixed to `display: none` below 760px (`styles.css`).
     - **Manager's center "+" action duplicated the adjacent Dashboard tab** -- see Questions/Decisions #1 above, now points at Tasks.
     - **The "Add to Home Screen" banner could overlap the bottom nav's raised center FAB button** by a few pixels -- the banner's mobile clearance calc only accounted for the flat bar height (`--bottomnav-h: 64px`), not the center button's `margin-top: -22px` raise above it. Fixed with a larger, documented clearance value (`styles.css`).
     - **Real crash #1 (Inventory & Purchasing tab): a company with zero device recipes/equipment types crashed the whole tab** (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'components')`) instead of showing an empty state -- `selectedBuildRecipe`'s three fallbacks (`.find() ?? .find() ?? [0]`) all resolve to `undefined` when `deviceRecipes` is `[]`, and `.components` was read unconditionally right after. This is a real, pre-existing production bug (not something this session introduced) that would hit **any brand-new company with no equipment types yet, or any user who opens Inventory before the async load finishes** -- a full crash requiring a page reload, not a cosmetic issue. Fixed with an inert placeholder recipe fallback (`main.tsx`, `selectedBuildRecipe`) so the panel renders its existing "No equipment types yet" empty state and the "Create new equipment" onboarding path (already built, just unreachable) actually works.
     - **Real crash #2 (Projects tab): a company with zero real projects crashed the whole tab** (`reading 'id'` of undefined) for the same shape of reason -- `selectedProject` had the identical `.find() ?? [0]` gap, and its *own* initial-state fallback (`useState(initialProject?.name ?? projects[0].name)`) was worse: `projects` (no `s`-suffix collision, a real different identifier) is a **module-level hardcoded demo/seed array** (fake "NNSB 37th Street" project data) that predates the real backend, so a zero-real-projects company fell back to a fake project name that matched nothing in real `projectSites`, then crashed on `.id`. Fixed both: the `useState` fallback now uses `projectSites[0]?.name ?? ""` (never the demo array), and `selectedProject` gets the same inert-placeholder-object fix as `selectedBuildRecipe`.
     - Modals confirmed to still own the full viewport (bottom nav + install banner both hidden while any `.modal-backdrop` is open, pre-existing behavior, re-verified not regressed).
     - Desktop layout confirmed visually unchanged (nothing above was inside a desktop-reachable media query).
  3. **Dashboard "Needs Attention" panel** -- new prioritized-exceptions section (overdue/urgent tasks, overdue projects, held POs) using only existing real data, no invented metrics; removed the duplicate "Inventory Value" appearing in both the KPI row and Manager Focus; filtered cancelled purchase requests out of Recent Activity. See Questions/Decisions #6 for the all-company-vs-per-user scope call.
  4. **First test/lint baseline this repo has ever had.** `npm run lint` (eslint 9, flat config, `eslint.config.js`) -- 0 errors, 74 warnings (react-hooks v7's new `set-state-in-effect`/`purity`/`exhaustive-deps` rules deliberately left as non-blocking warnings, not silenced: they found ~40 real, pre-existing spots across a 25k-line file, but a mass fix is its own separate, risky refactor, not in scope here). `npm test` (vitest 5 + jsdom, `vitest.config.ts`, `.env.test` for dummy Supabase values -- see that file's comment for a real Vite/Vitest `import.meta.env` module-boundary gotcha worth knowing about if extending these tests). `npm run test:smoke` (new: Playwright, `playwright.config.ts`, `tests/smoke/*.spec.ts`) -- 6 browser-level tests covering the sign-in gate actually gating (against a dev server started with dummy-but-present Supabase env vars), tab navigation, and the mobile shell (single nav, no duplicate center action, modal hides the nav), including regression tests for both crash bugs above. Commands documented in `README.md`.
  5. **Server-side authorization audit, all 7 `api/*.js` routes.** Findings and fixes:
     - **`send-push.js` -- the most serious finding.** Uses the Supabase service-role key (bypasses RLS) and had zero validation on the target `userId` beyond truthy -- any signed-in account could push fabricated content to any real device. Fixed: target must be a real known user (checked with the caller's own token, service-role key never used for the check itself), plus title/body length caps and `isAllowedAppUrl` on the notification's `url`. See Questions/Decisions #4 for the still-open "should this require a real application event" question.
     - **`send-notification-email.js`/`send-notification-slack.js`** -- same "arbitrary recipient" gap, same fix shape (real-known-recipient check + length caps). See Questions/Decisions #3.
     - **`send-invite-email.js`** -- now admin-only server-side (was any signed-in user), plus email-format and `isAllowedAppUrl(inviteUrl)` checks (an attacker-controlled invite link was a real phishing vector). See Questions/Decisions #2.
     - **`sales-quote-extract.js`** -- now `sales`/`pm`/`manager`/admin only server-side (was any signed-in user calling a paid, metered AI API). See Questions/Decisions #5.
     - **`send-proposal-email.js`/`send-submittal-email.js`** -- deliberately left with an open `clientEmail`/recipient (a real external-client-facing feature, not a bug), but `isAllowedAppUrl` added on their `shareUrl` (the actual gap: an unrestricted share link host).
     - New shared helpers: `api/_lib/requireRole.js` (checks `app_admins`/`app_user_roles` via the caller's own token), `api/_lib/validateUrl.js` (host allowlist for any user-influenced URL a server route embeds in an email/notification).
  6. **Incremental decomposition, deliberately small this session, not a rewrite.** Extracted `DataLoadErrorBanner` into its own file (`src/components/DataLoadErrorBanner.tsx`, zero-dependency, purely mechanical) plus the two new `api/_lib/*.js` helpers. **Deliberately not done, and why**: `MobileBottomNav`/`InstallAppBanner` (already separate top-level functions inside `main.tsx`, `~7998`/`~8126`) are the obvious next candidates for real file extraction, but both depend on ~6 module-scope constants/types (`View`, `ALL_TABS`, `ROLE_QUICK_ACTION`, `MOBILE_NAV_TAB_ICON`, `TAB_LABELS`, `RoleMode`) that would all need exporting first -- a mechanically bigger, real-regression-risk change given how late in this session it would land, so it's flagged here as the concrete next incremental step rather than rushed. `Projects` (11724-13927, ~2200 lines) and `Inventory` are the other large single-function components worth splitting in a future pass, once there's room to verify each split thoroughly.
  7. **This doc.** Corrected the stale "migrations end at 098" banner (real highest is 113), added today's direct re-verification of 068/070/095, and added the "Questions / Decisions Needed" section above.
  - **Checks**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean (chunk-size warning is pre-existing, unrelated to this session), `npm test` (9/9 passing), `npm run test:smoke` (6/6 passing), `npm run lint` (0 errors).
  - **Pushed and deployed**: commits `257dc03` then `e6b53d4` (HANDOFF hash follow-up), pushed to `main`, Vercel auto-deployed -- confirmed by fetching the live production bundle and finding the new "Needs Attention"/"Sync issue" strings from this session's own code.
  - **Live-verified on production (`https://ergon-ops-app.vercel.app/`, real signed-in session, real company data)**: the exact bug this task named, working end to end -- Dashboard's new "Needs Attention" panel shows a real "1 Data sync issue" card with the actual live error text ("Could not load inventory movements: column inventory_movements.performe...") instead of a fake empty ledger, the top-nav pill shows "Sync issue (1)", and Reports > Activity Ledger shows the same real error + a Retry button in place of "no movements found." Also checked at desktop width with real data: Inventory & Purchasing (33 real SKUs), Projects (5 real projects), Reports > Documents (self-heals silently as designed, no false error), and Messages -- all render correctly, no console errors, no regressions from this session's changes.
  - **Live-verified production migration state directly** (068/070/095, two independent methods: raw REST calls, and live network-request capture on the real Reports page) -- see "Current setup truth" above for the exact evidence.
  - **Not independently re-verified against production at mobile width**: this session's Claude-in-Chrome browser tab would not actually resize its viewport in this sandbox (`resize_window` reported success but `window.innerWidth` stayed at `1920` afterward) -- a real tool limitation encountered late in this session, not skipped by choice. The mobile-shell fixes (nav strip, install-banner overlap, both crash-guard fixes) were instead verified thoroughly at 390x844 against a **local dev server running this exact same commit's code** (same `main.tsx`/`styles.css`, confirmed via the deployed bundle content match above) across Dashboard, Inventory & Purchasing, Projects, Messages, the More sheet, and a modal -- functionally equivalent evidence, just not a screenshot of the literal production URL at that width. If this matters, the fastest way to close the gap is simply loading the production URL on an actual phone (or narrowing an actual desktop Chrome window by hand) and comparing against the screenshots already taken locally.


- **Lock channel back (unlock-only was a real gap), persist last-open conversation, and tighten DM row density -- all live-verified with real reload tests.**
  1. **Lock a channel back to private.** E: "once i unlock it, i cant lock it again from inside the chat." `unlockChannel()` only ever went one direction. New symmetric `lockChannel()` (persistence.ts, same PATCH shape, opposite value) -- the lock icon now shows whenever a group channel is currently visible to everyone, in the same spot the unlock icon occupied when it was private. `channel_members` untouched either direction -- locking only restricts who can *newly* see it, doesn't remove anyone already in it. **Live-verified against the real PM channel**: locked it (confirmed `private: true` via a direct DB read, not just the UI), unlocked it back (confirmed `false`), restored it to how E had left it.
  2. **Persist the last-open conversation/channel across reloads.** E: "this need to keep me in the same chat every time i come back this is very annoying" (screenshot circled around the blank "Select a conversation" panel). Neither `activeConversationId` (top-level App state) nor `activeChannelId` (local state inside `Messages`) survived a reload -- always dumped back to the empty state. One shared, type-tagged `localStorage` key (`ergon:last-thread`, `{type: "conversation"|"channel", id}`) lets whichever was actually on screen restore itself on mount; writing it is centralized through a `selectConversation()`/`persistLastChannel()` pair so every existing select-a-conversation and select-a-channel call site got this for free, no new call sites needed. Cleared on an explicit "Back to conversations" click, so what's stored always matches what's actually shown. **Live-verified with a real reload**: opened Nate Wolfe's DM, did a real `navigate()` (not a soft in-app nav) to reload the whole page from scratch -- landed right back in that same DM. Did the same for the Inventory & Purchasing channel -- same result.
  3. **DM/channel conversation rows were a real 2-row-per-person layout -- collapsed to one.** E circled a single sidebar row: "if i have 20 name on here and you are taking up 3 line of space for one person, this is getting old." The row used to be avatar + name-with-pin-icon on row 1, then a whole separate always-visible row 2 for email + copy-email icon + timestamp. - **Real auth-gate bug found and fixed, PLUS a hard lesson: my own Claude-in-Chrome tab was the cause of most of what got reported as "the page refreshing on its own."** E: "you are really struggling with the images... the message page is also refreshing on its own and taking me out of the chat i was in." Two genuinely separate things came out of chasing this:
  1. **Real bug, confirmed by tracing the exact mechanism (`main.tsx`).** A pre-existing proactive access-token-refresh timer (added in an earlier session, fires roughly hourly) gives `authSession` a new object identity. That re-runs the auth-checks effect, which was flipping `authChecksReady` back to `false` at the start of every run -- and the render gate for `authChecksReady` is a full top-level early return that swaps the ENTIRE app for a "Checking your account..." screen. Every background token refresh was unmounting everything, including Messages' own local `activeConversationId`/`activeChannelId`, so it came back with no conversation selected. Fixed with a ref that only lets the full-page gate show once, on the very first real sign-in check -- the background re-verification (still useful, e.g. catching a mid-session admin/approval change) keeps running exactly as before, it just never blocks the UI again. Reset on sign-out so a different account on the same tab still gets the proper gate. This is real and correct, but it turned out **not** to be what E was actually seeing minute-to-minute (see below) -- confirmed live: `authSession.expiresAt` on a real session showed a normal ~hour-scale countdown, not a stuck short cycle.
  2. **The actual "every minute, flashes and moves" symptom: it was this session's own Claude-in-Chrome tab.** E had that exact browser tab open and was watching it while I drove it all night -- every `navigate()`, click, and reload I ran for my own verification steps was directly visible on E's screen as unexplained flashing/movement. Spent real effort chasing this as an app bug first (instrumented a MutationObserver on a fresh tab, watched it for 100+ seconds, found zero DOM churn -- correctly ruled out a code-level cause) before E clarified "well it's your tab, i'm using" and it clicked. **Lesson, now a standing rule for this session: never reuse a tab the user might be looking at for automated testing -- always open a separate new tab (`tabs_create_mcp`) for verification, and close it when done.** Applied immediately (see the messages-shell scroll fix below, done entirely in a fresh tab, closed after).
