- **Messages page forcing an unnecessary scroll on larger screens -- real bug, measured, not guessed.** E: "there is no reason i should have to scroll down the larger browsers page like this. move it all slightly up." `.messages-shell`'s height formula assumed 175px of chrome above it (top nav + page heading), but a live `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement in a fresh tab showed the real total is ~239px (top-nav 102px + `.main`'s own 28px top/bottom padding + `.page-heading` ~81px including its margin) -- a 64px shortfall that pushed the bottom of the compose row below the fold. Corrected the constant (175 -> 240px). **Live-verified precisely**: before the fix, `document.documentElement.scrollHeight` (919px) exceeded `window.innerHeight` (855px) by 64px; after, they match exactly (855 = 855, `overflowPx: 0`) and a screenshot confirms the whole panel fits with clean margin at the bottom, no scroll needed. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean, pushed and deployed (`1e29756`), done in a fresh tab per the lesson above.

Worked through the night while E slept -- listing what was captured here since E wasn't online to confirm the list first (the usual practice this session), same as always checked in code/build before shipping.
  1. **Scroll-hijack bug, found and fixed (real root cause, not a guess).** `MessageThread`'s auto-scroll effect (`main.tsx`) fired on every `messages` change -- but both the channel poll (5s) and DM poll (5s) rebuild `messages` as a brand-new array reference on *every* tick, even when nothing actually changed. That re-triggered `scrollIntoView` every single poll, yanking anyone scrolled up reading history back to the bottom every few seconds -- exactly what E described. Fixed with a ref tracking the last message's id: only scroll when it actually changes (a real new message), and even then only if you were already near the bottom (150px), matching how Slack/Teams behave. **Live-verified**: posted 10 filler messages to force real overflow, scrolled to the top, waited 12+ seconds (2+ poll cycles) via a direct `scrollTop` check -- stayed at 0, did not snap back.
  2. **Compose row misalignment, real root cause found.** The camera/attach button was explicitly sized 42x34px; the @ and emoji-toggle buttons had no size at all (just icon + 2px padding, ~21px tall) -- bottom-aligned in the same flex row, they visibly sat shorter than the camera button and the textarea. New shared `.messages-compose-icon` class gives @ and emoji the same 42x34 box. Confirmed visually after deploy -- all four controls (camera/@/emoji/send) now line up cleanly.
  3. **Dead space above the compose box on short threads, real root cause found.** `.messages-thread-scroll` (a flex column) top-aligns its content by default -- a 1-message thread left a big empty gap between the message and the compose row instead of sitting just above it like Slack/Teams. Wrapped the message list in `.messages-thread-list` with `margin-top: auto` so it bottom-anchors when shorter than the panel (deliberately not `justify-content: flex-end` on the scroll container itself -- that has a well-known browser bug that clips the top of a thread once it actually overflows and needs to scroll). **Live-verified**: a 1-message channel now shows the message sitting directly above the compose row, no gap.
  4. **Send button is now icon-only** (a bare `Send` paper-plane icon, `.messages-send-button`, 42x42px matching the other compose icons) -- E: "both example i gave you did not say send, they had icons." Matches Slack/Teams exactly.
  5. **New Links tab per channel** (Discussion/Photos/**Links**/Tasks/Canvas) -- from the real Teams "Shared Resources" screenshot (Canvases/Slides/Links circled). Zero new backend: scans every loaded message's body for a URL (`extractMessageLinks`, a shared regex) and lists them newest-first with hostname, sender, and timestamp -- exact same "derive from existing message data" approach the Photos tab already used for images, so it shipped and works immediately, no migration needed. **Live-verified**: posted a message with a real URL, opened the Links tab, confirmed it listed the hostname + sender + timestamp correctly.
  6. **Message reactions (migration 113, confirmed run by E 2026-09-02 ~1am) -- the single most recognizable Slack feature this app was missing.** Hover-free, always-visible small "add reaction" icon under each message; click opens the same `QUICK_EMOJI` grid the compose box already uses; clicking an emoji adds a reaction pill (emoji + count), clicking your own pill removes it -- a real toggle, not a one-way action. Two mirrored tables (`channel_message_reactions` / `direct_message_reactions`), same "channel mirrors DM" convention this schema has kept since migration 094/101, with RLS scoped identically to each side's own message-visibility rules (private-group membership for channels, conversation participancy for DMs) -- a reaction can never leak visibility the message itself wouldn't already give a reader. Full SQL is in `backend/supabase/migrations/113_message_reactions.sql` if it's ever needed again.

     - **Post-migration: hit a transient Supabase write-path 503 first, then confirmed it cleared on its own.** Right after E ran the migration, 3 separate write attempts (2 POSTs + 1 DELETE on `channel_message_reactions`) all came back `503`, while every `GET` on the same table succeeded and a plain `INSERT` to `channel_messages` at the same moment also succeeded -- narrowed it to specifically **upsert-style writes** (`Prefer: resolution=...`), since the pre-existing `app_known_users` heartbeat upsert (untouched tonight) 503'd the same way at the same time. Waited a few minutes and retried: **fully resolved, confirmed both directions with real DB reads, not just UI state.** Reacted to a real channel message -- reloaded the page fresh and queried `channel_message_reactions` directly, a real row was there (`created_at: 2026-09-03T01:02:13Z`). Removed it via its pill -- queried again, table back to empty. Repeated the same add/remove/verify cycle against `direct_message_reactions` in a real DM thread (Nate Wolfe) -- identical result, row appeared then disappeared on direct query both times. **Reactions are fully live and working, both channel and DM, add and remove, confirmed via the database itself.** No console errors throughout. The 503 window was real but self-resolved -- nothing left to do here.

  - **Found while researching the ask, not requested but load-bearing for it**: `knownUsers` -- the directory that powers DM conversations, the People Picker, and @mention autocomplete -- was only ever populated by `reloadAdminDirectory()`, which only runs `if (adminFlag)`. Migration 094 had already widened `app_known_users`' RLS specifically so every signed-in user could read it for messaging (`create policy "authenticated read app_known_users for messaging" ... using (true)`), but the client never actually consumed that for non-admins -- every non-admin user's messaging directory has been silently empty this whole session. Fixed by moving the load out of `reloadAdminDirectory()` into its own unconditional `useEffect`, gated only on `authSession` existing, not on admin status.
  - **Presence heartbeat**: same new effect now also calls `upsertKnownUser` (updates `app_known_users.last_seen_at`) and reloads the directory every 90s while signed in -- previously `last_seen_at` was only ever touched once, at sign-in, so there was no live data an "online" indicator could have used even for admins. `isRecentlyActive()` (new helper, main.tsx) treats "seen in the last 5 minutes" as online -- same proxy Slack/Teams use, no new websocket/Realtime infrastructure, consistent with how every other live-ish thing in this app already polls.

E then shared two real screenshots of Slack's own Profile panel (large photo, name, Contact information) as the reference shape. Built a new `"profile"` view (My Profile link in the account-menu dropdown, next to Admin) showing a large avatar (click to change), display name (editable), email (read-only, tied to the login), and groups (read-only, admin-managed elsewhere) -- deliberately not copying Slack's status/local-time/About-me/phone fields since Ergon has no backing data for any of those and inventing it would be scope beyond what was asked; the avatar + name pairing mirrors what Team Roster's own edit row already exposes.
  - **RLS**: `team_members` already had a self-service UPDATE policy scoped to the caller's own row by JWT email (migration 035, built for an unrelated reason -- letting a non-admin user's row exist at all) -- the `avatar_url` write was already covered for free. The only gap was the `avatars` **storage bucket** itself, whose write/update policies (migration 109) were admin-only. Migration 111 widens them the same way 035 widened `team_members`: additively (Postgres ORs multiple permissive policies together), so admins keep uploading for anyone via Team Roster exactly as before, while a signed-in user gains the narrow ability to write only inside the folder matching their own `team_members.id` (`buildAvatarStoragePath`'s storage path is `<team_member_id>/...`) -- they still cannot touch anyone else's avatar folder.

Fixed by querying `notification_rules?select=event_type` directly instead of trusting file history, then re-ran clean. **Lesson for next time a shared CHECK-constraint whitelist needs widening: always query the live table first, never reconstruct from migration files.**

- **@mentions confirmed live end to end**: sent a real `@Nate` message in the Sales channel, confirmed a `notifications` row was created for `nate@ensight-technologies.com` with the correct title/body/`related_entity_type`. (First attempt showed nothing -- turned out to be the recurring stale-tab issue, not a code bug: the browser tab was still running an older in-memory bundle from before the fix's deploy. `window.location.reload()` picked up the fresh one and the retest worked immediately.) Push-delivery tracking (`notification_deliveries`) came back empty for that notification -- likely the pre-existing VAPID-not-configured gap noted elsewhere in this file, not something introduced here; not chased further since the actually-requested behavior (an alert reaching the person) is satisfied by the in-app bell.
- **Avatars confirmed live end to end**: uploaded a real test image to a roster member in Admin > Team Roster, confirmed it rendered in both the collapsed row and the expanded edit panel, confirmed it survived a full page reload (real DB round trip, not just local state). Could not confirm the "next to messages" rendering specifically -- every message in this deployment so far was sent by E's own account; no other teammate has ever posted a real message to check a non-mine bubble's avatar against. The code path (`senderAvatarFor`) is structurally identical to `senderNameFor`, which *is* proven working (real name labels render correctly on real messages) -- reasonably high confidence, just not click-through-verified for this one specific rendering spot. Test avatars cleared back to empty afterward (a 1x1 test pixel isn't a real photo for the roster).
- **Search page routing bug found and fixed live**: `#search` in the URL rendered the Dashboard instead of results. Root cause: `viewFromHash()` (main.tsx) has its own hardcoded array of valid hash values, entirely separate from the `Record<View, ...>` maps (`TAB_LABELS`, `pageTitle`'s titles, `pageSubtitle`'s subtitles) that TypeScript's exhaustiveness checking already forced an update to when `"search"` was added to the `View` union -- a plain `.includes()` check against a hardcoded array gets no such compile-time protection, so this one slipped through until actually clicking into it. Fixed, redeployed, retested: search-bar Enter, the dropdown's "See all results" link, filter-chip clicks (verified "Orders" filter narrows correctly with live counts), and clicking a result (verified it navigates to the right project) all confirmed working with real data ("Straub" -> 1 project + 3 purchase orders, exactly right).
- **(migrations 108-109 confirmed run and live-verified, see entry above) — @mentions/alerts, avatars, and a full-page Notion-style search with filters.** E: "I need to be able alert people, so tag them @Sales or @Ehren so it would send me an Alert." / "Avatars" / "Notion Style Search" (the latter two closing gaps explicitly flagged as deferred back in the Phase 3 search work).
  - **@mentions (migration 108, new `mentioned` notification event).** Routed through the exact same `notify()`/`notification_rules` engine every other event in this app already uses (migration 024) -- no new delivery system, just a new event type wired into it. `@Sales`, `@Marketing`, etc. resolve to everyone currently holding that role via `loadUsersByRole()` -- the same lookup role-assigned tasks already use (`ROLE_KEY_OPTIONS`, spaces stripped for matching); `@Ehren` resolves to a team member by first name. Defaults to in_app + push (an @mention is meant to interrupt, not sit quietly in the bell like some lower-urgency events default to) -- editable in Admin > Notification Rules like every other event.
    - Wired into all three places E named: channel messages (chat), Canvas saves, and a task's description on create/update. Dedupe keys are scoped so re-saving unchanged text never re-notifies, but real edits do -- message mentions dedupe by the message's own id (always unique), Canvas by day, task descriptions by a slice of the text itself so an actual content change re-fires.
    - `notification_rules.event_type` has a whitelist CHECK constraint (migration 024) -- had to add `'mentioned'` to it before a rule row could exist for the new event type.
    - Not built: an @-autocomplete dropdown while typing. E described the tagging mechanism, not a suggestion UI -- typing `@Name` plain-text and having it resolve on send/save is the whole ask; a live suggestion popup is a reasonable fast-follow if wanted, not assumed.
  - **Avatars (migration 109, `team_members.avatar_url` + a new public `avatars` Storage bucket).** Uploaded in Admin > Team Roster (same place Slack member ID and Display name are already edited) -- admin-only, matching who already manages the rest of the roster. The bucket is public, unlike message-attachments (deliberately private/participant-scoped) -- an avatar isn't sensitive, and public means every message bubble renders the stored URL directly with zero signed-URL round trips. Renders next to the sender's messages in both DM threads and channel Discussion (`MessageThread`'s new `senderAvatarFor` prop) -- a plain person-icon placeholder shows until someone has one uploaded.
  - **Full-page search results with filters (view `"search"`, not a nav tab -- reached by pressing Enter in the search bar or "See all results" in the existing dropdown).** Reuses the exact same client-side filter predicates the dropdown already had (Phase 3, no new backend query) just without the 5-per-group cap, plus filter chips by entity type with live counts (All/Projects/Tasks/Parts/Orders/Vendors/Sales Quotes/Documents/Messages). Message search specifically gets a higher limit (40 vs. the dropdown's 8) via a new optional `limit` param on `searchMessages()`, fetched only when the search page is actually open so the dropdown's own lighter fetch is untouched.
  - `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.
- **(migration 107 needs to be run) — Two real RLS bugs found live-testing the group-channel feature, both fixed, not guessed.** E confirmed 104-105 ran, then confirmed 106 ran; both times the very next step was to actually exercise the feature in the deployed app rather than trust the migration alone (same discipline as every migration this session).
  - **Bug 1 (fixed by 106, confirmed via live retest): creating a channel failed with "new row violates row-level security policy."** Root cause: channel creation is two requests (create the channel, then add members) and Postgres RLS requires a freshly-inserted row to satisfy the table's SELECT policy for `RETURNING` to succeed -- at that exact moment the creator isn't in `channel_members` yet. Migration 106 lets the creator always see a channel they created regardless of membership-row timing. Retested after 106 ran: create, message, and add-member all worked correctly, live, with real data (verified against the actual Sales/Marketing/Inventory tasks and a real project's Canvas save/reload).
  - **Bug 2 (migration 107, not yet run): Unlock silently does nothing.** Clicking Unlock always left the channel private -- no error, just no effect. Diagnosed by making the same PATCH request directly (bypassing the app) and getting `200` with an empty body -- meaning the request "succeeded" but matched zero rows. Checked every migration back to 101: `channels` has only ever had SELECT and INSERT policies, **never an UPDATE policy** -- Postgres RLS defaults to deny for any command with no matching policy, so `unlockChannel()` has been a no-op since the feature was written, both before and after 106. Migration 107 adds the missing UPDATE policy (same broad "any authenticated user" posture as the table's own INSERT policy).
  - **Not yet re-verified**: Unlock itself, since it needs migration 107 first. Everything else in this feature set (create, message, add people, Tasks/Files/Canvas tabs, Active/Closed grouping, collapsible sections) is live-verified with real data.
- **(migrations 104-105 need to be run) — Freeform group channels, Canvas, Active/Closed grouping, collapsible sidebar sections.** E went through the earlier Slack-screenshot analysis point by point: "I know we talked about creating new tabs independently I don't see that" / "Create group chats that are private or can be unlocked, also be able to add people to them" / "Be able to shrink chat sections" / "yes to this" (to starting with Active/Closed grouping and/or freeform channels) / "Canvas -> a persistent notes/scope doc pinned to the channel". Four real features, all built this pass:
  - **Freeform group channels (migration 105, `channels.type = 'group'`).** The "create your own channel" half of the roadmap that migration 101 never covered (that one is only the fixed Section/Project/Client channels). Private by default -- a real `channel_members` table, the first channel type that isn't broadly team-visible. A "New Channel" button in the Messages hub opens a name field + a checkbox list of everyone known to Ergon (same list DMs already use) for initial members; creator is always auto-added. **Unlock** flips `private` to `false`, making it exactly as broadly visible as a Section/Project/Client channel from then on (one-way -- no re-lock button, matches how E described it: "private or can be unlocked"). **Add people** works on any group channel, locked or not.
    - **Security note, real and load-bearing, not cosmetic**: locking down `channels` alone isn't enough -- `channel_messages` (both read and insert) and the `message-attachments` storage policies all got the same membership check, otherwise a private group's messages/files would still be readable by anyone who knew or enumerated the channel id, even with the channel row itself hidden. Every policy fully qualifies its column references (`c.id`, `m.channel_id`, `channel_messages.channel_id`, `storage.objects.name`) rather than relying on scope fallback -- direct application of the exact bug class migration 103 found and fixed earlier this session (an unqualified `name` inside a correlated subquery silently bound to the wrong table's own column).
    - Sidebar gets a 4th group, "Groups" -- a lock icon replaces the usual hash icon for channels still private. `persistence.ts`: `createGroupChannel`, `unlockChannel`, `loadChannelMembers`, `addChannelMember`; `Channel` type gained `private`/`createdBy`.
  - **Canvas (migration 104, new `channel_canvas` table).** One persistent text doc per channel -- goals, decisions, links, anything worth pinning where the whole channel can find it later, per E's own description. A 4th tab on every `ChannelDiscussion` (Discussion/Tasks/Files/Canvas -- unlike Tasks/Files, Canvas needs no optional props since it only depends on the channel id, so it's always available everywhere `ChannelDiscussion` is embedded, not just the 6 sites with task/document data wired up). Loaded lazily, only once the tab is actually opened. `persistence.ts`: `loadChannelCanvas`/`saveChannelCanvas`, upserting on `channel_id` (its own primary key).
  - **Active/Closed project channel grouping in the Messages hub sidebar** -- mirrors the existing Projects page pattern (`ProjectSite.status === "Closed"`), no new state needed, just a second bucket alongside the existing "Projects" group.
  - **Collapsible sidebar groups** -- every group header (Sections/Projects/Closed Projects/Clients/Groups) is now a toggle button with a chevron; collapsed state remembered per-user in localStorage the same way the DM pin list already is.
  - `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.
  - **Deliberately not built this pass, queued next**: user avatar images next to messages (E: "Add user images to the main setup that will carry next to these messages") -- needs a real upload flow (Storage bucket + policies, an Admin/Team Roster UI, a render change in `MessageThread`), independently sizable. The Notion-style full search page with filters (E: "we talked about like Notion with filters") -- the dropdown-only search extension from Phase 3 is still the only search UI; a dedicated results page was flagged as deferred back then too, and stays deferred here.

Answer: **List -> Tasks** and **Folder -> Documents** both do, cleanly, since Ergon already tracks `task.section`/`task.projectRef` and `document.project`. Workflow and Link were judged low-value/no-equivalent and skipped (not contradicted by E, but also not explicitly confirmed -- a real next-step question, not a forgotten one).
  - `ChannelDiscussion` (main.tsx) now takes optional `tasks`/`taskActivity`/`projectSites`/task-CRUD-handler props and optional `documents`/`onDownloadDocument` props. When present, a small Discussion/Tasks/Files tab bar appears above the thread; when absent (a call site that doesn't have that data handy), the component renders exactly as before -- no call site was forced to change behavior, only the 6 that could sensibly supply the data now do.
  - **Tasks tab** reuses the existing `TaskMiniPanel` component verbatim (same one the Project detail page's own "Project Tasks" panel uses) -- no new task UI written. Project channels filter by `task.projectRef` via the channel's linked `ProjectSite`; **Client channels aggregate across every `ProjectSite` with that `clientId`** (see below) and filter tasks whose `projectRef` is in that set. Section channels needed a real fix -- see below, this paragraph originally shipped with a naive equality match that was wrong.
  - **Files tab** is new lightweight JSX (no reusable Documents component existed) -- reused the same `.document-row`/`.project-doc-list` styling as the Project detail page's own document list. Only shown for Project and Client channels (Section channels have no per-section document field to filter on). Client channels aggregate by project name across every site under that client, same pattern as Tasks.
  - **Prerequisite**: `ProjectSite.clientId` didn't exist at the app level even though `projects.client_id` has existed in the DB since migration 102 -- added `client_id` to both `PROJECT_SITE_SELECT` and its pre-087 fallback, and to `mapProjectSiteRow` (persistence.ts). No migration needed, migration 102 already added the column; this was purely a read-path gap.
  - Threaded the new optional props through all 6 existing `ChannelDiscussion` call sites: the 3 section channels (Inventory, Sales, Marketing -- Tasks only, no Files), the Projects-list section channel (Tasks only), the per-project Discussion tile (Tasks + Files, already had every prop in scope via the `Projects` component), and the Messages hub's `activeChannel` panel (Tasks + Files, richest -- works for any channel type opened from the sidebar).
  - `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.
  - **Follow-up fix, same session -- E caught it: "if we need to match names, make sure they are linked or we stay consistent."** The first pass filtered a section channel's Tasks tab with `task.section === channel.sectionKey`, but these are two genuinely different vocabularies that only coincidentally share some words: `channels.section_key` matches the app's nav tabs (`inventory`, `projects`, `sales`, `marketing` -- migration 101's seed), while `EOTask.section` is a real task-categorization enum (`TaskSection` in persistence.ts: `warehouse`/`purchasing`/`inventory`/`projects`/`sales_catalog`/`sales_quotes`/`engineering`/`general`). `sales` splits into two real task sections; `marketing` has no `TaskSection` at all -- no page in the app has ever created one. Worse, the same wrong value was cast straight into `TaskMiniPanel`'s `section` prop, which is used to lock the new-task section in its create modal (`TaskEditorModal` with `lockSection` -- confirmed by reading it, the section field is not user-editable there) -- creating a task from the Sales or Marketing tab would have silently written an invalid/orphaned `section` value with no way to fix it from the UI.
    - Added an explicit `SECTION_CHANNEL_TASK_SECTIONS` map (main.tsx) instead of a naive equality check: `inventory` -> `[inventory, purchasing]` (this channel is the combined Inventory & Purchasing & Vendors tab group), `projects` -> `[projects]`, `sales` -> `[sales_catalog, sales_quotes]`. `marketing` is deliberately absent -- `channelHasTaskSupport()` now hides the Tasks tab entirely for Marketing rather than showing a tab that can never have data.
    - Every existing `TaskMiniPanel` call site in the app (`Procurement Tasks`, `Projects Tasks`, etc.) sets `hideAdd` for section-level panels and only allows inline Add for a panel scoped to one specific project (`Project Tasks`, with both `section` and `projectRef` set) -- matched that exact convention: the channel Tasks tab now sets `hideAdd` for every section and client channel, and only a Project channel gets `section="projects"` + `projectRef` + Add enabled, same as the existing per-project panel.
- **(`cd93610`, migration 103 needs to be run) — Real bug found via live post-migration verification, root cause confirmed.** E ran migrations 099-102 ("all ran - Success. No rows returned"). Live-tested every piece end to end rather than trusting the "Success" message alone:
  - **Fully verified working**: Messages hub sidebar shows all 4 Sections + all 5 Projects + all 6 Clients, exactly matching the reconciliation. Sent a real message in the Straub Medical client channel -- worked. **DM picture attachment -- fully verified end to end**: upload (200), signed URL (200), actual image fetch (200), rendered inline.
  - **Found broken**: channel picture/file attachments failed with "new row violates row-level security policy." Root cause, confirmed not guessed: migration 101's channel storage policy had an unqualified `name` inside `exists (select 1 from channels c where name like c.id::text || '/%')` -- `channels` has its own `name` column, so Postgres silently resolved the reference to `c.name` (the channel's own display name) instead of the intended `storage.objects.name`. The check was really asking "does this channel's display name look like `<its-id>/something`" -- never true. The DM/conversation version of this same policy (migration 100) happened to work only because `conversations` has no `name` column to shadow with. **Migration 103 fixes it** by explicitly qualifying `storage.objects.name` / `public.channels` instead of relying on scope fallback -- same fix pattern worth remembering for any future RLS policy that references a table with its own `name`/`id`/other commonly-reused column name.
  - **Migration 103 confirmed run by E, and the fix fully verified live** in the same session: re-uploaded a test image to the Straub Medical client channel -- all 3 steps succeeded (upload 200, signed URL 200, image fetch 200), rendered inline exactly like the DM path. Same channel, same code, only difference was the migration -- confirms the root-cause diagnosis was correct.
  - **Also verified**: Slack member ID field genuinely saves now (`PATCH team_members` -> 200, confirmed via network tab, migration 099) -- test value entered then cleared, not left in the roster.
  - **Everything from this entire session's roadmap work is now live, confirmed, and working**: channels (all 4 sections + every project + every client, real messages sending), Messages hub sidebar, message search, Slack DM groundwork's data layer, picture/file attachments (DMs and channels both). **Still not built**: the Client Ledger UI rework itself (grouping by client instead of per-project), Slack's actual bot token/OAuth setup (external, E's step), the dedicated full-page search results view. All three are real next steps, not forgotten.

- **(`b073371`, migration 100 needs to be run)** — **Picture and file uploads in Messages.** E: "for now, in the messaging, can you allow Picture and File uploads to the messages." Followed by, on the earlier Slack research: "yes, I think the full function of back and forth makes sense to do... build all the features slack does in here eventually... eliminate that app all together" -- captured as long-term direction below, not built (see note at the bottom of this entry).
  - **Migration 100** SQL:
    ```sql
    alter table direct_messages alter column body drop not null;
    alter table direct_messages drop constraint if exists direct_messages_body_check;
    alter table direct_messages add constraint direct_messages_body_check
      check (body is null or char_length(body) <= 4000);
    alter table direct_messages add column if not exists attachment_storage_path text;
    alter table direct_messages add column if not exists attachment_file_name text;
    alter table direct_messages add column if not exists attachment_mime_type text;
    alter table direct_messages add column if not exists attachment_size_bytes bigint;
    alter table direct_messages drop constraint if exists direct_messages_has_content;
    alter table direct_messages add constraint direct_messages_has_content
      check ((body is not null and char_length(body) > 0) or attachment_storage_path is not null);
    insert into storage.buckets (id, name, public)
    values ('message-attachments', 'message-attachments', false)
    on conflict (id) do nothing;
    -- (plus 2 storage.objects RLS policies, see backend/supabase/migrations/100_direct_message_attachments.sql for the full file -- long enough to be worth copying straight from there)
    ```
  - **Private bucket, not the broad-authenticated pattern used for project-documents/catalog-datasheets.** Storage paths are prefixed with the conversation id specifically so the bucket's RLS can check the requester is one of the two real participants (same guarantee `direct_messages`' own row-level security already gives the text) -- these are private DMs, org-wide file visibility isn't appropriate here.
  - **Compose row**: new attach button (camera icon, left of the textarea) opens a file picker; selecting a file shows a preview chip (thumbnail for images, filename otherwise) with a remove button, before sending. An attachment can stand alone with no caption -- Send is enabled by file-present OR text-present, not just text like before. 20MB client-side cap with a clear error if exceeded.
  - **Thread rendering**: images show inline (click for full-size in a new tab); other files show as a name + download row. Signed URLs (1hr expiry) resolve automatically as messages load, not on click, so it previews like a normal chat app.
  - **Live-verified pre-migration behavior** (this is the actual current state until migration 100 runs): attach button and preview chip work correctly client-side; clicking Send correctly fails with a visible "Could not upload "filename"." status message (bucket doesn't exist yet) -- caught, no crash, no phantom message sent. Same honest-failure shape as every other pre-migration gap this session.
  - **On the bigger ask** ("build all the features slack does... eliminate that app all together"): logged as E's actual long-term direction, not scoped or estimated yet -- a full Slack-replacement is a large, multi-part vision (threads, reactions, channels, search, mobile push, etc.), worth its own planning conversation rather than inferring scope from one line. Flag it back to E before any of that gets built, don't assume which pieces matter most.

  - **What E still needs to do to actually turn this on** (none of this is code — all external setup only E can do):
    1. Run migration 099 above.
    2. Go to `api.slack.com/apps` -> Create New App -> From scratch, name it, pick the workspace.
    3. Under **OAuth & Permissions**, add the `chat:write` Bot Token Scope, then "Install to Workspace."
    4. Copy the **Bot User OAuth Token** (starts `xoxb-`) and add it to Vercel as `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` (server-only env var, no `VITE_` prefix -- same pattern as `SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, never exposed to the client build).
    5. In Admin > Team Roster, fill in each person's Slack member ID (Slack desktop/web: click their profile -> "..." More -> "Copy member ID").
    6. In Admin > Notification Rules, check the "Slack / Teams" box for whichever events should DM people (e.g. `direct_message_received`, `task_assigned`).
    7. Test by triggering one of those events -- e.g. send someone a Message, or assign them a task -- and confirm they get a real Slack DM.
  - **Note on scope**: this only DMs the recipient of a specific Ergon notification (a new message, a task assignment, etc.) -- it does not mirror the Ergon Messages *conversation* itself into Slack as a synced thread. If E wants two-way sync (reply in Slack, it shows up back in Ergon) later, that's a materially bigger build (Slack Events API, a public endpoint Slack can call into, matching Slack users back to Ergon accounts on the way in) -- flag it explicitly if that's actually wanted, don't assume it's included in "link this chat to Slack."

Exact re-run SQL for all three is in that section.
- **Overnight follow-up sweep, no code changes.** E asked for "one more sweep of whatever you can while i sleep" right after the three fixes below. Reload-tested every remaining top-level view not yet covered this session -- Sales/Site Builder, Tasks, SaaS Calendar, Client Ledger, Messages, Dashboard, Marketing, Library, all 6 Reports sub-tabs (Procurement/Inventory/Manufacturing/Activity Ledger/Projects/Documents), plus opened a real project detail page (Straub Medical HI) and its Cost Breakdown/Margin modal. All clean -- no console errors, no further logic bugs found. Two things looked at and ruled out as non-bugs: Sales' "Avg Deal Size $0" despite one Closed-Won quote (that quote genuinely has no priced BOM lines yet, so $0 is correct, not broken); the Margin modal's `saleAmount ? ... : null` check treats a literal $0 sale the same as "not set" (shows "--" instead of a technically-computable negative margin) -- a real but extremely unlikely edge case, consistent with the same truthy-check pattern used elsewhere in this codebase, not worth the added complexity to special-case. Did not live-test typing a dollar amount into the Margin modal -- form input into a project's financial fields was blocked by the session's own auto-mode permission classifier while E was away, so that path was verified by reading the calculation code instead (`projectEstimatedProfit`/`projectGrossMarginPercent`/`projectMarginCategories`, main.tsx ~10997-11018 -- math checks out, null-safe throughout).
- **(`1e21c9d`, `e3c5f44`, `d5578a7`, no migrations)** — **Three more real bugs found via proactive live-checking**, from E asking "any other items worth you tracking down?" after the GIF clips wrapped. Same sweep-and-fix approach as the `equipment_type_id`/`withProductionLock` finds earlier this session -- reload-tested every major page instead of just reasoning about the code, all three confirmed live before and after the fix.
  - **Auth token never proactively refreshed (`1e21c9d`), the most impactful find.** Admin's user directory showed "No users have signed in yet" despite E obviously being signed in as the very account viewing that page. Network tab showed `app_known_users`, `conversations`, and `direct_messages` all returning **401** at once -- the access token had quietly expired (default ~1hr Supabase JWT lifetime) with nothing to catch it. `refreshAuthSession` already existed in `persistence.ts` and was already called, but only reactively inside the main "load remote app state" effect, itself keyed on `[authSession]` -- so it only re-checks expiry when something *else* already changed `authSession`. A tab left open with no such change lets the token age out with nothing to trigger a recheck, and since nearly every fetch in this app follows a "swallow the failure, return `[]`/`null`" pattern (see `withProductionLock` note above), that reads as "signed in, but nothing loads or saves" with zero visible error -- not a crash, just silent staleness. Added a self-re-arming `setTimeout` effect that refreshes a few minutes before `expiresAt` on its own, so elapsed wall-clock time alone triggers it, no other state change required. **Live-verified**: forced a fresh reload against the already-stale token, confirmed the refresh fired and the Admin directory populated correctly afterward (all 3 real users, roles, tab access).
  - **"Open Requests" / exposure stats double-counted requests that had already become a real order (`e3c5f44`).** Once a request is turned into a PO via "Create Purchase," it correctly disappears from the visible Request Queue (`linkedPurchaseOrderId` set -- deliberate, from an earlier E ask: "it should only be in Waiting for receiving now"), but the request's own `status` field stays `"Ordered"` rather than moving to `"Received"`/`"Cancelled"`. Every *other* place that counted "open" requests by status alone (not by the same `linkedPurchaseOrderId` exclusion) kept counting it too -- double-counted against the very order it had become. Fixed in all three places this pattern was duplicated: the Dashboard's "Open requests"/"Request exposure" tiles (every role), the Purchasing page's top "Open Requests" stat, and the Reports page's "Open Requests"/"Estimated Exposure". **Live-verified**: Purchasing's stat dropped from 35 to 34 after the fix (this dataset only had one request in that state).
  - **Schedule health showed "9162d overdue" for any un-yeared target date (`d5578a7`).** `project.due` is deliberately free text (an `<input>`, not a date picker -- e.g. "Jul 30"), and `parseProjectDueDate` already had a "best-effort, degrade gracefully" comment and a `NaN`-guard for genuinely unparseable strings like "TBD". The gap: `new Date("Jul 30")` *does* parse successfully, but JS silently defaults the missing year to **2001** -- so every un-yeared target read as ~25 years overdue instead of the real handful of days, and the `NaN` guard never caught it because the parse didn't fail, it just silently picked the wrong year. Fixed by detecting a missing 4-digit year and supplying the current year explicitly, then rolling forward one year only if that still lands 6+ months in the past (handles a date typed in December for next January without misreading a genuinely-overdue date as "next year"). **Live-verified**: "9162d overdue" / "9152d overdue" / "9146d overdue" became "31d overdue" / "21d overdue" / "15d overdue" -- correct against today's date (2026-08-30) for targets of Jul 30 / Aug 09 / Aug 15.
- **(`a9649ae`, no migration)** — **Admin force-delete for Inventory items blocked by an empty stock record.** Found while recording a how-to GIF of the Add/Delete Item flow for E (see the `gif_creator` note below) -- deleting a brand-new, never-touched test item ("Demo Test Widget," 0 stock, no movements, no BOM membership) hit "Can't delete -- this item has stock, movement, or build-BOM history." E: "i like this, but i think ADMIN should be able to delete it because I will have a lot of demo data i will have to delete eventually."
  - **Root cause of why this blocks nearly everything, not just real history**: `inventory_balances.inventory_item_id` is a required FK with no `ON DELETE` clause, and every item gets an `inventory_balances` row automatically at creation time (even at `quantity_on_hand: 0`, since that's how the column tracks stock at all) -- so the plain hard-delete effectively blocks on "does this item exist," not "does it have real history." The friendly message was accidentally misleading for this exact case.
  - **New `forceDeleteInventoryItem`** (persistence.ts): clears only genuinely **empty** `inventory_balances` rows (`quantity_on_hand = 0 AND quantity_reserved = 0`) before retrying the delete. Deliberately does **not** touch real movements, BOM membership, or purchase order lines, even for admins -- destroying those would be real data loss, not demo cleanup. If those still exist, the retry still fails with a message telling you to Retire instead.
  - **UI**: when the normal delete is blocked by exactly this error and the current user is an admin (new `isAdmin` prop threaded into `Inventory`), a follow-up `window.confirm` offers the escalation instead of a dead end.
  - **Real discovery while building/testing this, worth flagging for any future session with Chrome access**: a native `window.confirm()`/`window.alert()` dialog **fully blocks CDP automation** -- every subsequent tool call (screenshots, clicks, network reads) times out or hangs until a human manually dismisses the dialog. There is no way to dismiss a native browser dialog programmatically through these tools (`key` events sent via CDP target the page's renderer, not the browser-chrome-level dialog). If a recorded/automated flow needs to click something that triggers `confirm()`/`alert()`, either avoid triggering it, or be ready to ask E to dismiss it manually -- this actually happened live this session (see the `gif_creator` note below).
  - **Not live-tested in-browser** -- for the reason above: this feature's OWN confirm dialogs would immediately re-trigger the same blocking behavior, so verifying it end-to-end needs a human clicking through, not automation. tsc/build both clean.
- **New capability discovered/used this session: `gif_creator`.** E asked for short how-to GIF clips ("just the APP, not the whole screen") of Adding and Deleting an inventory item. `mcp__claude-in-chrome__gif_creator` records real `computer`/`navigate` tool actions in a Chrome tab and exports an animated GIF with click indicators/action labels -- scoped to one tab, not the full desktop, matching exactly what was asked. Delivered both clips: "Adding a new item" (18 frames) and "Deleting an item" (5 frames -- sparser, since several `screenshot` calls mid-recording hit unrelated CDP timeouts and didn't contribute frames; the delete itself went through cleanly). Both download to the user's real Downloads folder, which this sandbox can read directly via `Bash`/`find` and hand to `SendUserFile` -- no separate file-transfer step needed.
  - **Deleting-an-item clip note**: the item deleted for the clip ("Demo Item A," SKU-0034) had a genuinely empty stock record and deleted on the very first real delete, with no `confirm()` dialog appearing and no need for admin force-delete -- so **the admin force-delete escalation (above) is still not live-tested end-to-end**, just tsc/build-clean. Confirming it needs a human present to click through its own `window.confirm()` prompts (see the CDP-blocking note above) against an item that actually has stray `inventory_balances` rows to clear.
  - **Real, tab-specific flakiness found**: the long-lived tab from earlier in this session (open ~6+ hours) started timing out on every real click (`Input.dispatchMouseEvent` timeout, 30s) while a **brand-new tab** on the same page clicked instantly and cleanly. If `computer` clicks start timing out repeatedly on a tab that's been open a long time, opening a fresh tab (`tabs_create_mcp` + `navigate`) is a fast, effective fix -- recording state itself is scoped to the **tab group**, not the individual tab, so a fresh tab in the same group picks up an in-progress recording without needing to restart it.
- **(`f19250d`, migration 098 confirmed run 2026-08-28)** — **Fixed a misleading "Healthy" status label; added delete for Cancelled/Undone builds.** E, from a screenshot with a $0-stock item's "Healthy" pill circled: "what is gauged as Healthy, we have no parts but everything says healthy." Separately, from the Builds panel: "I need to be able to delete these [cancelled builds], it should just log if any user does, but i don't want a long list of cancelled items."
  - **Root cause of "Healthy" being wrong**: it was the fallback label for anything not Retired or over its reorder point -- but `trackReorder` defaults to `false` for every item (migration 092, deliberately, until E turns tracking on per-item -- see that migration's own work-log entry: "do it now and set them all to not track"). An untracked item's actual stock level was never being evaluated at all, it just fell straight through to "Healthy" regardless of whether stock was 0 or 1000.
  - **Fix**: new `inventoryStatusLabel(part)` helper adds a genuine fourth state, **"Not Tracked"** (neutral grey `.status`, not the green `.status.ok` "assessed and fine" implication "Healthy" carries) -- shown whenever `trackReorder` is off, with "Healthy"/"Reorder" now only appearing when tracking is actually on and a real comparison happened. Applied everywhere the old inline ternary was duplicated: the desktop table pill, the mobile card pill, the CSV export status column, and the filter-matching logic; added "Not Tracked" as a real option to both status filter `<select>`s (desktop + mobile) so it's filterable too.
  - **Builds delete**: the panel only ever shows the 6 most recent transactions -- cancelled test builds (several from this session's own testing) permanently occupied those slots with no way to clear them, pushing real Planned/Posted work out of view. New soft-delete for `build_transactions` (migration 098: `deleted_by_email`/`deleted_at`, same pattern as every other soft-deleted entity, logged to `deletion_log`). The same header-row trash icon that Cancels a Planned build now Deletes a Cancelled/Undone one instead (with a `window.confirm` first) -- one visual slot, contextual action based on status. `loadBuildTransactions` filters `deleted_at=is.null` with a 400-fallback retry (same safety-net shape used everywhere a new filter could 400 against a not-yet-migrated table) so Builds doesn't go blank if 098 hasn't run yet -- deleted builds just won't be hideable until it does.
  - Migration 098 SQL:
    ```sql
    alter table build_transactions add column if not exists deleted_by_email text;
    alter table build_transactions add column if not exists deleted_at timestamptz;
    ```
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`0a43cd6` + `96a69e9`, migrations 096/097 confirmed run 2026-08-28)** — **All 5 of E's Projects section requests, built.** See the Open/pending-items entry with the same date for E's exact wording and the pre-work investigation into what already existed. Items 1-3 needed no schema change; items 4-5 do.
  1. **Portfolio phase-count summary**: new "Projects by phase" row in `ProjectPortfolioHealth` (pill per `ProjectSite.status`: Draft/Planning/Procurement/Staging/Install Ready/Closed, computed across *all* projects, not just active ones, since Closed being one of the phases is the point). Closeout date was already shown per-project in the existing Schedule health block ("Target <date>") -- didn't need a new field.
  2. **Margin section**: new read-only block in the Cost Breakdown modal -- overall gross margin % (`(Sale - all costs) / Sale`), plus each existing cost bucket (Materials/Equipment = Allocated, Labor, Subcontractor, Travel/Other) shown as $ and % of sale. This is "cost as % of revenue per category," not an isolated margin per category -- the data model only tracks one total Sale amount, no per-category revenue split exists to compute a true category margin against. Also added a Gross Margin tile to the Cost Snapshot card for at-a-glance visibility without opening the modal. No new fields -- pure computation off existing `saleAmount`/`allocated`/`estimatedLaborCost`/`subcontractorCost`/`travelExpenses`.
  3. **Per-location cost**: new Cost column on the Locations table + an estimated-cost line in the location detail drawer header, computed by summing every line item's `qty x resolveCatalogUnitCost(catalogItem, inventoryItems)` across all 5 line types (Camera/Sign/Sensor/Misc/VPU) plus their accessories -- reuses the exact cost-resolution function the rest of the app already uses (correctly handles catalog items whose cost is linked to a live inventory item, not just the catalog's own stored value). Threaded `inventoryItems` as a new prop into `ProjectLocationsSection` for this.
  4. **Drawings document type**: `ProjectDocument.type` gains `"Drawings"` (migration 096, widens `project_documents_document_type_check`). The Project Documents modal now groups the flat list into per-type sections (`GENERAL_DOCUMENT_TYPES`: Project/Drawings/SOW/BOM/Procurement/Sales Quote) instead of one undifferentiated list, and a type picker (defaulting to Project) sits next to Upload so new files land in the right group -- previously every upload was hard-coded to `type: "Project"` with no way to pick anything else. Closeout's own 5 document types stay filtered into the Client Ledger's Closeout Vault only, not mixed into this general view. Submittals remains a fully separate feature, deliberately not folded into Documents.
  5. **Stakeholders section**: new "Stakeholders" card in the Address Book, alongside the existing fixed Client/Billing/Shipping cards -- genuinely open-ended roles (free text, with a `<datalist>` of suggestions: Property Owner, General Contractor, Electrician, Architect, Facilities Manager, Security Consultant) capturing name, phone, **email** (none of the 3 existing cards have an email field at all), and address. New `project_stakeholders` table (migration 097) -- same lazily-loaded-per-open-project, soft-deleted, deletion-logged shape as `installed_assets` (migration 089's pattern exactly, including keeping it out of the big `PROJECT_SITE_SELECT` query so an un-run migration can't blank the whole Projects page). Loaded via a `useEffect` in `Projects` keyed on `selectedProject.id`, mirroring how Client Ledger loads `installedAssets`. `updateProjectStakeholder`/`onUpdateProjectStakeholder` exist end-to-end (persistence + App-level handler) but aren't wired to a UI control yet -- only Add/Remove are exposed in the card; add inline edit if E wants to correct a stakeholder's info without delete-and-readd.
  - Migration 096 SQL:
    ```sql
    alter table project_documents drop constraint if exists project_documents_document_type_check;
    alter table project_documents
      add constraint project_documents_document_type_check
      check (document_type in (
        'sales_quote', 'sow', 'bom', 'purchase_order', 'invoice', 'field_photo', 'other', 'purchasing', 'project',
        'drawings',
        'as_built', 'om_manual', 'completion_certificate', 'network_schema', 'power_schedule'
      ));
    ```
  - Migration 097 SQL:
    ```sql
    create table if not exists project_stakeholders (
      id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
      project_id uuid not null references projects(id) on delete cascade,
      role text not null,
      name text not null,
      phone text,
      email text,
      address text,
      notes text,
      created_by_email text,
      created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
      deleted_by_email text,
      deleted_at timestamptz
    );

    create index if not exists idx_project_stakeholders_project on project_stakeholders(project_id, created_at);

    alter table project_stakeholders enable row level security;

    create policy "authenticated read project_stakeholders"
      on project_stakeholders for select to authenticated using (true);

    create policy "authenticated write project_stakeholders"
      on project_stakeholders for all to authenticated using (true) with check (true);
    ```
  - **Deploy was slow this one time (~10+ min instead of the usual ~1 min) but did land** -- bundle hash eventually changed and every item below was confirmed live, not just assumed:
    - Item 1: "Projects by phase" row visible with real counts (Draft: 1, Planning: 0, Procurement: 2, Staging: 2, Install Ready: 0, Closed: 0) -- note the Portfolio panel is collapsed by default (`usePersistedCollapse`), so it has to be expanded to see this.
    - Item 2: Cost Snapshot's new Gross Margin tile renders (correctly shows "Not set" on a project with no sale amount yet).
    - Item 3: Locations' Cost column renders (mobile card shows "COST $0" via the `data-label` stacking, matching the established convention).
    - Item 4: The Documents modal's new type `<select>` is present with all 6 options including Drawings.
    - Item 5: Full round trip tested for real -- added a test stakeholder (General Contractor / "Test Verification GC" / phone / email), confirmed it rendered, **reloaded the page fully** and confirmed it survived (proves the write actually persisted, not just local state), then deleted it and confirmed removal. Address Book's Stakeholders card is clean of test data afterward.

- **(`b8a5976`, no migration)** — **`withProductionLock`'s failure path was invisible to a logged-in user -- found live while spot-checking Inventory Adjust after the equipment_type_id fix, not asked for.** E said "anything to check?" after the equipment_type_id fix shipped; applied the same reload-verification technique to another core save path (Inventory Adjust) and hit a real, live silent failure.
  - **What happened**: a real Inventory Adjust (SKU-0020, test qty) silently did nothing -- no error, no state change, modal just closed. `read_network_requests` showed `rpc/acquire_transaction_lock` returning **401** -- the session's access token had genuinely expired mid-session (this session had been running many hours without a full reload). `withProductionLock`'s catch block only called `setAuthStatus(...)`, which per this app's own established rule (see standing rules above) only renders on the signed-out login screen -- completely invisible to anyone actually logged in, exactly the failure shape already fixed at ~30 other call sites in the 2026-08-24 audit but missed here since this is a *shared wrapper function*, not a delete/status-change function that audit's pattern search targeted.
  - **Fix**: added `window.alert(message)` alongside the existing (harmless, kept) `setAuthStatus`/`setSyncStatus` calls. `withProductionLock` guards **7 call sites** in one shared function -- `addInventoryItem`, `updateInventoryItem`, `transferInventoryToProject`, `adjustInventoryStock`, `allocateFromInventory`/`shipFromInventory`, and both build actions (`buildInventoryUnit`, `undoBuildTransaction`) -- so this one change makes lock-acquisition and auth failures visible across all of them at once, not just Inventory Adjust.
  - **Not a bug in Adjust itself** -- confirmed by reloading (which re-triggered the existing `authSession.expiresAt < Date.now() + 60_000` refresh-on-load check and re-authenticated cleanly, "Cloud synced" restored), then repeating the same adjustment with a valid session: worked correctly, persisted, reverted cleanly.
  - **Flagged, not chased further**: the refresh-on-load check only runs when `authSession` changes (effectively: on a fresh page load), not on any periodic timer while a tab sits open and idle. A real user who leaves a tab open past token lifetime and clicks something *without* reloading first would hit this same silent-401 shape today, now at least visibly instead of silently. Whether that's worth a proactive periodic refresh (vs. relying on "the alert tells you to reload") wasn't investigated further -- flag if E reports an action doing nothing after leaving a tab open a long time.
  - **Also audited** every other `= new Map(...Rows.map(...))` lookup-building save function in `persistence.ts` (`saveInventoryMovements`, `saveProjectAllocations`, `saveDeviceRecipes`, `saveProjectSites`'s BOM-line save) for the *same* "built a lookup map but forgot to use it for one field" bug shape as the equipment_type_id fix -- all resolve every field correctly. That bug was isolated, not systemic.
  - tsc/build both clean. Verified live at every step (network request inspection, not just code reading).

Investigated for real instead of rewording the copy again.

A build's equipment name isn't stored directly on the row; it's read back via a join (`equipment_type:equipment_types(equipment_name)`), so with the FK always null, every build's name reads back empty on any reload -- immediately after creation it looked fine (reading fresh local state), but a refresh always blanked it. This is what generated "recipe not found" originally and "equipment type no longer exists" after the reword -- neither was really about a renamed/deleted recipe, the link itself was never being saved.
  - **Fix**: the function already queries `equipment_types` for `finished_inventory_item_id` -- now also selects `id` and resolves `equipment_type_id` from that same lookup instead of hardcoding null.

This is exactly the class of bug that only a real reload against the live database can catch; local React state always masked it.
  - **Existing builds (0001-0008, all test data) are not recoverable** -- `equipment_type_id` has been null in the database the whole time for these, and there's no other column the original equipment link could be re-derived from. Only builds created after this fix save correctly. Not worth a migration/backfill given these are disposable test records.
  - tsc/build both clean.

- **(`26fa144`, no migration)** — **Fixed the long-open "fields extended past the background" report: Inventory table's Adjust column was absorbing the table's leftover width.** E finally got a screenshot -- Parts Inventory's desktop table, a big blank gap circled between the Status column and the Adjust button.
  - **Root cause**: `.inventory-table`'s last column (`<th></th>`, blank header -- holds the per-row `.table-actions` div with the Adjust button) had no width constraint. With `table { width: 100% }` (global) and `.inventory-table { min-width: 1180px }` but no `table-layout: fixed`, the browser's auto table-layout algorithm hands any unconstrained column a share of the table's surplus width whenever the rendered table is wider than its natural content -- this column, with `.table-actions`' own `min-width: 170px`, had one of the larger "preferred" widths among the narrow numeric columns, so it absorbed a disproportionate chunk. `.table-actions`' `justify-content: flex-end` then pushed the Adjust button to the far right edge of that oversized cell, leaving the blank gap visible in the header and before the button on every row.
  - **Fix**: `width: 1%; white-space: nowrap;` added to `.inventory-table th:last-child`/`td:last-child` (alongside the existing sticky-right rules) -- the standard trick to force an auto-layout column to shrink to its content's minimum width instead of participating in surplus-width distribution; the freed width now flows into the table's already-flexible columns (Part, Manufacturer) instead.
  - **Not reproducible below 760px** -- `.inventory-table-scroll` is hidden on mobile in favor of `.mobile-card-list`, so this was specifically a desktop/tablet-width table-layout bug, not literally a phone bug as the original vague report implied. E confirmed this Parts Inventory screenshot is the only instance actually verified; no other section confirmed as of this fix -- if E finds another spot, treat it as a new, separate report (this fix is scoped to `.inventory-table` only, not a general table-layout change).
  - **Could not visually verify in-browser** -- no login access to the live app from this sandbox (standing limitation, see Data model notes). tsc/build both clean.

Mobile keeps the standard 2-up card layout, unchanged.
