- **`01525b2`, no migration** — **Security review: every serverless API route in this app was completely unauthenticated -- fixed all 7.** E, after a real security incident on the VLTD sister project overnight: "I need you to run whatever scan you can to make sure nothing was made public if a security group scan this app online for holes."
  - **Confirmed the repo is public on GitHub** (`Ktech312/ergon-ops-app`) -- so this wasn't a theoretical "what if a scanner finds this," it was "anyone can already read exactly how to abuse this by opening the file on GitHub."
  - **The finding**: none of `send-invite-email`, `send-notification-email`, `send-notification-slack`, `send-proposal-email`, `send-submittal-email`, `send-push` (built earlier this same session), or `sales-quote-extract` checked who was calling them -- every one was a fully public POST endpoint. Worst case: `send-invite-email` let anyone on the internet use this app's own real, trusted Gmail/Resend sender to blast an email with an attacker-controlled link and subject to any address, styled exactly like a legitimate "you've been invited" email -- a phishing vector riding on a real business's sending reputation, not just spam. `send-push` (the one built today) could spam any Ergon user's phone by user id; `sales-quote-extract` would run arbitrary uploaded PDFs through parsing (and, if `OPENAI_API_KEY` is ever set, burn OpenAI credits) for anyone who found the endpoint.
  - **Fixed the same way everywhere**: new `api/_lib/requireAuth.js` -- verifies the caller sent a real Supabase user access token (`Authorization: Bearer <token>`) against Supabase's own `/auth/v1/user` endpoint before the handler does anything. No new secrets needed (reuses `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`/`VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, already set). Every route now calls `requireAuth(req, res)` first and returns a 401 if it fails; every one of the 7 client-side call sites in `main.tsx` now sends `authorization: Bearer <accessToken>`. `sales-quote-extract`'s caller (`handleSalesQuoteFile`) lives inside the `Projects` sub-component, which didn't have `authSession` in scope at all -- threaded a new `accessToken` prop through from `App` rather than restructuring where the function lives.
  - **This only proves "a real logged-in Ergon user is asking," not role/permission** -- these routes have no per-role logic today, and requiring any valid login is the same trust boundary the rest of the app already leans on everywhere else (client-side admin/role gating, not server-enforced, for almost everything). If a route ever needs stricter (e.g. admin-only) enforcement, layer a role check on top of `requireAuth`, don't replace it.
  - **Also checked and confirmed clean** (this was a full review, not just the API routes): `.git-credentials-ergon` was never tracked by git (confirmed via full history search, not just current files); `.env`/`.env.*` are gitignored and `.env.example` only has placeholders; full git history scanned for common secret patterns (API keys, private key blocks, service-role/VAPID/Gmail-password assignments) -- one hit, a `<placeholder>` in an old test-setup comment explicitly warning not to commit the real value, not a real leak; Storage buckets are correctly private except two deliberately public ones (`company-branding` logo, `catalog-datasheets` -- both make sense to be public); the old "no-login MVP" `anon`-role RLS policies from migrations 003/005/008 (early development, before real auth existed) were properly revoked in migration 009, confirmed still dropped, not resurrected by anything later; the public-facing submittal/proposal/invite share-link RPCs (`anon, authenticated` grants) are intentional and correctly token-gated, not a hole.
  - **Also fixed in the same pass**: caught myself about to commit the VAPID *private* key into `HANDOFF.md` while documenting the push-notification setup a few turns ago -- reverted before it was ever pushed (verified against full git history, confirmed clean). Real values were handed to E directly in chat instead and are not written into any committed file anywhere in this repo.
  - tsc/build both clean, no CSS changes.

- **(`0dde7fd`, migration 095 confirmed run 2026-08-27)** — **Push notifications: second half of E's DM + alerts request, built on the existing notification engine instead of a one-off system.** Real Web Push (VAPID) -- an icon on the phone home screen now gets alerted to a new direct message even with the app fully closed, matching what VLTD's alert system does.
  - **Wired into the existing `notify()`/`notification_rules` engine as a 4th channel** (`in_app`/`email`/`slack`/**`push`**), not a bypass -- every event that already fires a notification (task assigned, low stock, etc.) can now also push, admin-toggleable per event from the same Notification Rules panel (new "Push" column). New event type `direct_message_received`, seeded push-only (not in_app/email/slack -- an in-app bell entry for every DM would just duplicate the dedicated unread badge Messages already has).
  - **Real gap found and fixed while wiring this in**: `notification_deliveries.channel` and `notification_rules.event_type` both had DB CHECK constraints (migration 024) that didn't include `push`/`direct_message_received` -- would have failed every push delivery log and blocked the new event type outright. Widened both in migration 095, same "drop constraint if exists, re-add with the full list" pattern used for `purchase_orders.app_status` (migration 083).
  - **New `push_subscriptions` table** (migration 095) -- `endpoint` is the natural upsert key (unique per device+browser install), so one person can have several rows (phone + laptop both opted in) and push fans out to all of them. RLS: a session only ever manages its own rows.
  - **The one server route in this app with elevated Supabase access**: `/api/send-push.js` looks up *another* user's subscriptions to actually deliver to them, which has to bypass RLS -- uses the Supabase **service-role key** (new, server-only, never exposed to client code) instead of a user's own access token, the first time this codebase has needed one. Same honest-fallback pattern as the email/Slack endpoints (`{ sent: false, reason }` if VAPID keys or the service-role key aren't set in Vercel yet, not a fake success). Dead subscriptions (404/410 from the push service -- uninstalled, permission revoked) get cleaned up automatically on send.
  - **Real service worker, replacing the confirmed-inert stub** (`public/sw.js` -- previously existed only to satisfy PWA installability checks, no `push`/`notificationclick` handlers at all). Still does NOT cache anything (unchanged reasoning: frequent redeploys, don't risk serving a stale bundle) -- the only addition is real push handling.
  - **Client subscribe flow**: `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistration()` + `pushManager.getSubscription()` checks (never triggers the permission prompt) run automatically on login to know whether to show a "Turn on notifications" banner on the Messages page (VLTD's own UX pattern -- opt-in via a dismissible banner, not an automatic page-load prompt). Clicking "Turn On" requests permission, registers the real service worker, subscribes, and saves the subscription.
  - **VAPID keys generated this session** (`npx web-push generate-vapid-keys` via the newly-added `web-push` npm dependency) -- see the SQL/env-var handoff below, these need to go into Vercel before push actually works end to end.
  - Migration 095 SQL:
    ```sql
    create table if not exists push_subscriptions (
      id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
      user_id uuid not null references auth.users(id) on delete cascade,
      endpoint text not null unique,
      p256dh text not null,
      auth_key text not null,
      created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
      last_seen_at timestamptz not null default now()
    );

    create index if not exists idx_push_subscriptions_user on push_subscriptions(user_id);

    alter table push_subscriptions enable row level security;

    create policy "users manage their own push subscriptions"
      on push_subscriptions for all to authenticated
      using (auth.uid() = user_id)
      with check (auth.uid() = user_id);

    alter table notification_deliveries drop constraint if exists notification_deliveries_channel_check;
    alter table notification_deliveries add constraint notification_deliveries_channel_check
      check (channel in ('in_app', 'email', 'slack', 'teams', 'push'));

    alter table notification_rules drop constraint if exists notification_rules_event_type_check;
    alter table notification_rules add constraint notification_rules_event_type_check
      check (event_type in (
        'task_assigned', 'task_overdue', 'task_status_changed',
        'purchase_request_status_changed', 'build_stage_changed',
        'submittal_responded', 'low_stock_reached', 'direct_message_received'
      ));

    insert into notification_rules (event_type, channels, is_active)
    values ('direct_message_received', '{push}'::text[], true)
    on conflict (event_type) do nothing;
    ```
  - tsc/build both clean, CSS brace-balanced.

- **(`9676771`, migration 094 confirmed run 2026-08-27)** — **New Direct Messages feature: real 1:1 chat between any two signed-in users.** E: "I have a direct message and alert system built into it now [VLTD], I think we need that on this also, once the icon is on their phone, they will get alerts for messages or new tasks." Researched VLTD's actual implementation first (per standing rule) rather than guessing, then asked E to scope it -- chose "Full DM feature + push together" (the largest option) over push-alerts-only.
  - **Deliberately a different schema shape than VLTD's, not a straight port.** VLTD's `profiles.id` IS `auth.users.id` by construction, so any conversation participant is guaranteed a real logged-in user. Ergon has no equivalent -- `team_members` (migration 019) is explicitly "NOT tied to auth.users... someone can be assigned tasks before they've ever logged in," so it's the wrong identity source for a feature that has to actually deliver a message TO a real session. The real anchor is `app_known_users` (migration 012: `user_id references auth.users(id)`, auto-upserted on every login via the existing `upsertKnownUser` call) -- conversations reference that directly.
  - **`app_known_users`' existing read policy was admin-or-self only** (migration 012), which blocks the "search for who to message" picker for anyone but an admin. Migration 094 adds a second, broader SELECT policy (`using (true)`) rather than replacing the existing one -- Postgres RLS policies of the same command OR together, so this just adds broad read access on top, consistent with how `team_members`/`vendors` are already broadly readable on this small internal team.
  - **Migration 094**: `conversations` (canonical-ordered participant pair, unique constraint, `last_message_at` kept current by a trigger) + `direct_messages` (body, sender, read_at), full RLS (participants-only read/write, only the recipient can mark a message read), plus the `app_known_users` policy widening above.
  - **No `supabase-js`/realtime client exists anywhere in this app** -- every other feature is plain REST `fetch()` (see the data-model note below), so this **polls instead of subscribing to Postgres changes**: the conversation list + unread counts refresh every 20s app-wide (so the nav badge stays right even off the Messages tab), and an open thread polls every 5s. Not true realtime -- flagged here explicitly, not silently downgraded, in case E wants a real websocket subscription later (would need adding `@supabase/supabase-js` as a new dependency, a bigger change than anything else in this feature).
  - **New "Messages" nav tab**, added to every role's default tab list (E: a small team, no reason to gate person-to-person messaging by department) -- full `View`/`ALL_TABS`/`TAB_LABELS`/`viewFromHash`/mobile-nav-icon/page-title-subtitle wiring, caught by TypeScript's exhaustiveness check on the `Record<View, ...>` maps (4 spots, same mechanism used for every past tab addition this session).
  - **UI is mobile-first from the start**, not a desktop two-pane layout retrofitted later: single pane below 900px (conversation list OR the open thread, toggled by a back arrow), real two-pane side-by-side above 900px. Caught the exact same CSS specificity trap fixed in the Builds panel entry above while building the desktop override (`.messages-shell.thread-open .messages-list-panel` needing to be re-specified at matching specificity, not just `!important`'d) -- applying that lesson immediately instead of repeating the mistake.
  - **`knownUsers` state reused, not duplicated** -- it already existed app-wide (previously loaded only for the Admin directory via `reloadAdminDirectory`); Messages' own load effect now also populates it for every signed-in user (a little redundant for an admin session, simpler than threading one shared reload through both features).
  - Migration 094 SQL is below in the same entry the next session should hand to E, or paste directly:
    ```sql
    create policy "authenticated read app_known_users for messaging"
      on app_known_users for select to authenticated using (true);

    create table if not exists conversations (
      id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
      participant_a_id uuid not null references auth.users(id) on delete cascade,
      participant_b_id uuid not null references auth.users(id) on delete cascade,
      last_message_at timestamptz not null default now(),
      created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
      check (participant_a_id <> participant_b_id),
      check (participant_a_id < participant_b_id),
      unique (participant_a_id, participant_b_id)
    );

    create index if not exists idx_conversations_participant_a on conversations(participant_a_id, last_message_at desc);
    create index if not exists idx_conversations_participant_b on conversations(participant_b_id, last_message_at desc);

    create table if not exists direct_messages (
      id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
      conversation_id uuid not null references conversations(id) on delete cascade,
      sender_id uuid not null references auth.users(id) on delete cascade,
      body text not null check (char_length(body) > 0 and char_length(body) <= 4000),
      created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
      read_at timestamptz
    );

    create index if not exists idx_direct_messages_conversation on direct_messages(conversation_id, created_at);

    create or replace function bump_conversation_last_message_at()
    returns trigger as $$
    begin
      update conversations set last_message_at = new.created_at where id = new.conversation_id;
      return new;
    end;
    $$ language plpgsql security definer;

    drop trigger if exists direct_messages_bump_conversation on direct_messages;
    create trigger direct_messages_bump_conversation
      after insert on direct_messages
      for each row execute function bump_conversation_last_message_at();

    alter table conversations enable row level security;
    alter table direct_messages enable row level security;

    create policy "participants read their conversations"
      on conversations for select to authenticated
      using (auth.uid() = participant_a_id or auth.uid() = participant_b_id);

    create policy "participants create their conversations"
      on conversations for insert to authenticated
      with check (auth.uid() = participant_a_id or auth.uid() = participant_b_id);

    create policy "participants read their messages"
      on direct_messages for select to authenticated
      using (
        exists (
          select 1 from conversations c
          where c.id = conversation_id
            and (c.participant_a_id = auth.uid() or c.participant_b_id = auth.uid())
        )
      );

    create policy "participants send messages in their conversations"
      on direct_messages for insert to authenticated
      with check (
        sender_id = auth.uid()
        and exists (
          select 1 from conversations c
          where c.id = conversation_id
            and (c.participant_a_id = auth.uid() or c.participant_b_id = auth.uid())
        )
      );

    create policy "recipients mark messages read"
      on direct_messages for update to authenticated
      using (
        sender_id <> auth.uid()
        and exists (
          select 1 from conversations c
          where c.id = conversation_id
            and (c.participant_a_id = auth.uid() or c.participant_b_id = auth.uid())
        )
      )
      with check (sender_id <> auth.uid());
    ```
  - **Not yet built (this same feature request, next phase)**: push notifications, so a message actually alerts someone's phone with the app closed. Ergon's own in-app notification system (`notify()`, main.tsx) already fans out to email/Slack for many events and is more built-out than VLTD's -- adding push as a 4th channel there, wired to new-DM-message plus the existing task-assignment call sites, is next. Needs a new `push_subscriptions` table, a real service worker (Ergon's `public/sw.js` is currently a confirmed no-op, exists only for PWA installability), VAPID keys (to be generated and handed to E for Vercel env vars, same as any other secret this session), and one new send endpoint.
  - tsc/build both clean, CSS brace-balanced.

- **(`e7cc5db`, overnight autonomous work)** — **Closed two flagged gaps from the 2026-08-24 audit + mobile-audited everything built 2026-08-25, per E's "any items you can work on or confirm while i'm gone."**
  - **`setPrimaryUserRole`/`setSecondaryUserRoles`'s cleanup DELETE steps now checked** (the gap flagged and left open in the audit work-log entry): both now throw on `!response.ok` instead of silently continuing, matching every sibling write in those functions. A 0-row-affected result stays a non-error here (deliberately, unlike the delete/status-change pattern elsewhere) -- "nothing to clean up yet" is the normal case for a user's first role assignment, only a genuine request failure needed catching. Both callers already had try/catch + a real `setAdminStatus` display, so no caller changes needed.
  - **Mobile-audited today's new UI** (Tags column/filter, One-Off Dismiss button, Inventory item modal's Delete/clickable-links/price-history changes, the Builds panel): everything already using shared, previously-audited flex-wrap/grid-stacking patterns checked out safe as-is (`.table-actions`, `.report-table-row`, `.modal-actions`, the `.source-url-row`/`.price-history-row` mobile-stacking list). One real improvement made: `.price-history-row-static`'s notes/vendor/date text was ellipsis-truncated even under the mobile single-column stack, where there's actually room to show it -- added a `max-width: 760px` override to wrap instead of truncate there, matching the site's existing "wrap when there's room" convention.
  - tsc/build both clean, CSS brace-balanced.
- **(`be86252`, no migration)** — **The Builds panel fix from the entry directly below made the oversized-card bug worse, not better -- found and fixed the real cause.** E, from a screenshot with the width the buttons should actually be circled in blue: "YOU made the box bigger and worse."
  - **Misdiagnosed the first time**: the actual bug was never the side-by-side label|actions split running out of room -- it was a CSS specificity conflict. `.row-card div { display: grid; gap: 3px; }` (a generic rule for the simple label-over-value pattern every other `.row-card` usage follows) targets *every* `<div>` inside a row-card, including `.build-history-actions`. One class + one element (`.row-card div`) beats one class alone (`.build-history-actions`) in specificity, so `display: grid` always won regardless of what `.build-history-actions` said -- every action (status pill, Work Order, Request Parts, the stage dropdown, Complete, Cancel) was silently a single-column grid item stretching full-width, not a wrapped flex row. Stacking the card into a column (the earlier fix) just handed that already-broken grid a wider card to stretch into.
  - **Real fix**: `.build-history-actions` -> `.row-card .build-history-actions` (two classes, actually outranks `.row-card div`'s one-class-one-element specificity). No JSX changes needed -- the earlier column-stack layout was fine, it just needed the underlying flex to actually take effect.
  - **Lesson**: when a CSS fix doesn't visibly change anything (or makes it worse), check whether a more specific pre-existing selector is silently winning before assuming the new rule's *values* are wrong.
  - tsc/build both clean, CSS brace-balanced.
- **(`1ab12ae`, no migration)** — **One-Off Items got a "Dismiss" action; Manufactured Equipment's build list got a real name and a real layout fix.** E clarified the earlier "delete the Samsung SSD" ask: "I only want it purged from the One-off section, not the inventory section" -- don't touch the real inventory item, just stop it reappearing in One-Off Items.
  - **New `dismissOneOff`**: reuses the exact reconciliation mechanism a merge uses (marks the one-off's full current quantity as handled, same `one_off_reconciliations` insert, same visible-on-failure error handling) but skips `receiveInventoryStock` entirely -- no stock change, nothing in inventory touched. Third button on each One-Off row ("Merge Into Existing Item" / "Create New Item" / **Dismiss**), confirm dialog explains it doesn't touch inventory and that the same item name reappearing later (a genuinely new one-off) will show up again -- it's not a permanent ignore-list.

New `.build-history-row` modifier stacks label-then-actions vertically instead, giving the actions row the *full* card width to flow left-to-right in; `.build-history-actions` also switched `justify-content` from `flex-end` to `flex-start` so a wrap doesn't leave visible blank space. `.row-card` itself untouched (shared by ~9 other lists across Dashboard/Reports that were already fine).

A brand-new row (Add Price) opens directly in edit mode since it starts blank.

- **(`9409315`, no migration)** — **Found the exact bug behind two "things don't save" reports: One-Off Items merges were never actually persisting their reconciliation record, silently, every single time.** E: a tag removed from an item's tags came back after a refresh, and a Samsung 990 Pro SSD merge that "worked" the other day reappeared in One-Off Items.
  - **Root cause, One-Off merge reappearing (confirmed, not a guess)**: `mergeOneOffIntoInventory` built its `OneOffReconciliation.id` with `makeId("oor")` (a client-side string like `oor-1735099200000-a1b2c3`), matching the pattern used for `InventoryMovement.id` elsewhere -- but unlike movement ids (a plain `legacy_id` text column), `one_off_reconciliations.id` is a real `uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`. Sending a non-UUID string there fails Postgres's type check on every single insert. `logOneOffReconciliation` never checked `response.ok` (built before the 2026-08-24 site-wide write-verification audit, and outside that audit's scope since it wasn't a delete/status-change function), so this failure was completely silent -- the stock/movement side of a merge (a separate write, via `receiveInventoryStock`) always landed correctly, but the "already reconciled" record never did, so the one-off's full original quantity recomputed fresh on every reload. **Fixed**: stopped sending `id` in the insert payload (let Postgres generate the real UUID), and `logOneOffReconciliation` now throws on a non-ok response instead of swallowing it -- `mergeOneOffIntoInventory`'s caller shows the real error via `window.alert` if it ever fails again.
  - **Real consequence, flagged so it isn't missed**: because this bug means *zero* prior merges ever actually got a reconciliation record saved, re-merging an item that already looks "successfully merged" (like the Samsung 990 Pro SSD) would double-count its stock -- the earlier stock increase already landed for real. Told E not to re-click Merge on anything that reappeared from before this fix; only new merges going forward are affected by (and fixed by) this change.
  - **Tags-not-saving, investigated but not conclusively root-caused**: walked the full path (`toggleItemTag` -> `saveItem` -> `updateInventoryItem` -> the debounced whole-array `saveInventoryItems` upsert -> `loadInventoryItems`'s select/mapping) and found no equivalent smoking-gun bug -- the write payload, upsert, and read-back all look structurally correct for `inventory_tags`. Hardened `saveInventoryItems` with the same write-verification pattern as the 2026-08-24 audit anyway (checks `savedRows.length` against the number of items sent, throws if the upsert silently dropped part of the batch) so if this recurs, it'll show as a red sync-status pill instead of nothing -- ask E to watch for that pill turning red/amber next time a tag edit doesn't stick, and report back with what it says on hover; that'll pin down whether this is a real RLS/write bug or a save-before-refresh timing race (650ms debounce).
  - tsc/build both clean, no CSS changes.

Merges a saved object with the current default shape on load, so an older saved value missing a newer field (e.g. someone's saved filters from before the Tags filter existed) doesn't come back with `tag` silently `undefined` -- it fills in from the default instead.

- **(`5edb92a`, no migration)** — **Two regressions from the Tags-column change above, both fixed same-day.** E, from a screenshot: "This header does not go all the way anymore, you can see when i scroll up the adjust buttons scroll right past/through it" plus "I should not have to [use] the horizontal bar at the bottom, the page can be tightened just enough to fit without it."

Root cause: `.inventory-table th:last-child` (the header's sticky-right corner) and `.inventory-table td:last-child` (every row's sticky-right Adjust button) shared the exact same `z-index: 3` -- with two elements at equal stacking level, later-in-DOM wins, and `<tbody>` rows come after `<thead>`, so on vertical scroll the Adjust buttons painted *over* the header instead of staying under it. Fixed by giving the header's sticky corner `z-index: 4`, strictly above the body's `3`.
  - **Unwanted horizontal scrollbar** -- caused directly by bumping `.inventory-table`'s `min-width` 1180px -> 1280px in the same-day Tags change; that +100px push is what tipped the table past fitting on a normal screen. Reverted the min-width back to 1180px and, instead of growing the table to make room for Tags, scoped a tighter `max-width: 100px` to `.tag-chip-row` *inside this table specifically* (`.inventory-table td .tag-chip-row`) rather than shrinking the shared `.tag-chip-row` class everywhere (Product Catalog and Tasks also use it) -- the new column now fits inside the original width budget instead of growing past it.
  - Lesson for next time: don't reach for a blanket `min-width` bump to "make room" for a new column -- let the table's natural auto-layout redistribute space, and only add a floor if content is actually being cut off.
  - tsc/build clean, CSS brace-balanced.

- **(`a7d0dcc`, no migration)** — **Site-wide audit + fix: every delete/status-change function that could silently no-op instead of doing what it said, or fail with zero visible feedback.** Triggered by the Delete Equipment Type fix directly below -- E's follow-up: "I created that item last week, I just went to delete it today, so lets make sure this sync thing isn't a real issue. Can you do a fully system scan for anything like this, we cant have local memory on these type of issues." That correction ruled out the "raced the debounce" theory as the real cause (a week is nowhere near a race window) and reframed the actual, more serious question: is a delete/update ever silently not happening at all, app-wide?
  - **Two confirmed bug classes, audited across the whole app (not just Device Recipes):**
    1. **Trusting `response.ok` alone as proof a write happened.** PostgREST returns HTTP 200/204 for a DELETE/PATCH that matched **zero rows** (e.g. an RLS policy silently excluded the row for the calling user) exactly the same as one that changed a real row. Without `Prefer: return=representation` and checking the returned array actually has an element, a permissions-blocked delete looks identical to a real one -- local state gets optimistically updated (looks like it worked), the row survives in the database, and it's back after the next reload. This is very likely the real explanation for the week-old "New Equipment 1" not deleting.
    2. **Failures reported through `setAuthStatus`, which is invisible once logged in.** `authStatus` only renders inside `.auth-gate-status` on the signed-out login screen. Any handler that used it to report an error to an already-logged-in user was reporting to nothing -- confirmed as the second half of the Device Recipe bug, and found again on document downloads.
  - **Fixed, all following the same shape** (add `Prefer: return=representation`, check the returned row count before treating a write as real or logging it, surface failure to the caller instead of assuming success): `updatePurchaseOrderStatus` (Put On Hold/Resume -- the exact feature shape as the equipment-type bug, on code shipped just a few days ago), and 12 soft-delete functions that had **zero** check at all (`deleteScheduleTemplatePhase`, `deleteFormSchemaField`, `deletePresalesRule`, `deleteSiteHardwareRule`, `deleteTaskHardwareDependency`, `deleteInstalledAsset` -- which also had local state removed *before* the delete even awaited, fixed the ordering too -- `deleteSalesQuote`, `deleteSalesQuoteBomLine`, `deleteSalesQuoteLocation`, `deleteSalesQuoteLocationItem`, `deleteProjectLocation`, `deleteProjectLocationItem`), 8 more that already checked `response.ok` but not row count (`deletePurchaseOrderFile`/`restorePurchaseOrderFile`, `deleteSalesQuoteLocationImage`/`restoreSalesQuoteLocationImage`, `deleteProjectLocationImage`/`restoreProjectLocationImage`, `deleteProjectShipmentPhoto`, `restoreSalesQuote`), and 8 admin/permission/workflow functions (`revokeInvite`, `reviewUserApproval`, `setCatalogItemRetired`, `approveCatalogPriceChangeRequest`/`rejectCatalogPriceChangeRequest`, `updateSalesQuoteStatus`, `updateProjectDocumentStatusRemote`, `updatePurchaseRequestRemote`). Every caller of a function whose signature changed now checks the result before applying optimistic local state, and shows `window.alert(error)` on failure (or an existing visible status setter, e.g. `inviteStatus`/`catalogStatus`/`salesQuoteStatus`, where one already existed and was wired up).
  - **Also fixed**: `handleDownloadDocument`'s two failure messages used the same dead `setAuthStatus` channel -- switched to `window.alert`.
  - **Not changed, flagged instead**: `setPrimaryUserRole`/`setSecondaryUserRoles`'s cleanup DELETE steps (removing an old role row before upserting the new one) are still unverified -- lower risk since only admins call these and RLS gives admins a bypass, but if a role reassignment ever looks like it silently left a stale row, this is where to look first.
  - Split the work: a background agent handled the 21 delete/restore/hold functions (Tier 1+2 above) against a precise reference spec (`deleteEquipmentType`'s already-fixed shape); the admin/permission-sensitive functions (Tier 3) were done directly, by hand, given the security stakes (invite revocation, user approval, role changes).
  - tsc/build both clean at every stage, no CSS changes.
- **(`3d55e56`, no migration)** — **Fixed Delete Equipment Type silently doing nothing, and made the failure actually visible.** E, testing a throwaway "New Equipment 1" recipe: clicked Delete, confirmed the native popup, and it stayed right there in the list -- "this wont delete." E later clarified the item was created a week earlier, which rules out the debounce-race theory below as the real cause of this specific report -- see the audit entry above for the actual fix (row-count verification) that most likely explains it.
  - **Timing theory (real bug, but not what happened here)**: creating a new equipment type (`selectBuildRecipe`'s `__create_new_device` branch) only adds it to local React state; it isn't written to `equipment_types` until the existing 650ms debounce-save effect fires. Deleting fast enough after creating could hit `deleteEquipmentType`'s by-name lookup before the row exists remotely. Still fixed regardless (a lookup miss is now treated as "nothing to delete, done" instead of an error), since it's a real gap even though it wasn't the cause this time.
  - **The reason nobody saw an error either way**: the failure path called `setAuthStatus(...)`, invisible once logged in (see audit entry above). `handleDeleteDeviceRecipe`/`onDeleteRecipe` now return `{ ok, error }` instead of a bare boolean, and `deleteSelectedRecipe` shows `error` via `window.alert` when `ok` is false.
  - **Also hardened, per E's "make sure its logged also"**: the real DELETE now sends `Prefer: return=representation` and checks the returned row count before calling `logDeletionEvent` -- this is almost certainly what was actually happening: an RLS-blocked delete returning 200/204 with zero rows changed, indistinguishable from a real delete without this check.
  - tsc/build both clean, no CSS changes.
- **(`27e97d6`, `1ee09db`, migration 093 confirmed run 2026-08-23)** — **One-Off Items can now be reconciled two ways: merge into an existing inventory item, or create a new one.** E, looking at a Samsung 990 Pro SSD receipt sitting in One-Off Items despite an existing "Samsung 990 Pro SSD" inventory item: asked why it landed there and, once told the matching is by exact receipt-name text (a real vendor line came in worded "Samsung 990 PRO SSD 1TB M.2 drive," which doesn't exact-match). E: "we are able to open the item and either merge with existing item (which means the inventory is added, but a copy of the real receipt is left in the log as reference) or have a button to create new inventory line item."
  - **"Create New Item"** is the renamed existing "Add to Inventory" button/flow -- unchanged behavior, still self-resolving (the new item's name becomes the exact receipt text, so future receipts of that same wording match automatically and stop showing up as one-off).
  - **"Merge Into Existing Item"** (new) opens a modal to pick any non-retired inventory item; confirming calls the existing `receiveInventoryStock` under the hood (same function the real Waiting-on-Receiving flow uses) with the one-off's aggregate qty/cost and its PO order numbers as the reference -- so it lands in the Inventory Movement Ledger as a normal, fully traceable receipt, and the original PO receipt itself is never touched (it's derived data, always was going to stay as-is).
  - **Migration 093** adds `one_off_reconciliations` (item_key/item_name/qty/target_sku/order_numbers/resolved_by_email/resolved_at) purely so a merged one-off doesn't reappear at full quantity on the next render -- the One-Off list is recomputed live from PO receipts every time, so without tracking what's already been merged, the row would just come right back. Reconciled qty is subtracted from the live aggregate per exact item-name key; if more of the same misnamed item is received later, only the new unreconciled excess resurfaces (not blocked forever, not double-counted).
  - Fire-and-forget insert on merge (same `catch {}`-swallow pattern as `deletion_log`'s `logDeletionEvent`) -- a logging failure never blocks the stock update that already happened locally; `loadOneOffReconciliations` returns `[]` on any failure (including migration 093 not being run yet) so the One-Off list just behaves as it did before this feature if the table doesn't exist.
  - No new CSS -- reused `.report-table-row`/`.table-actions`, `.bom-modal-grid`, `.modal-panel`, and `.source-file`, all already mobile-audited.
  - **Caught by Supabase's own SQL editor, not by us**: the first draft of migration 093 created the table without RLS -- this HANDOFF already has a standing rule about this (see below, "Any new table needs `enable row level security` plus at least one policy") that got missed writing a brand-new small table from scratch. Fixed in `1ee09db`: same authenticated-only read/write policy pattern as `deletion_log` (migration 088).
  - tsc/build both clean, no CSS changes.
- **(`044a04c`, migration 092 confirmed run 2026-08-27)** — **Explicit "Don't track for Reorder" checkbox replaces the `reorderPoint > 0` overload from the entry directly below.** E, after that fix landed: "would it be better to have a push check box that says, 'Don't track for Reorder'?" -- yes: overloading 0 as "disabled" permanently reserved that value, so nobody could ever set a genuine "alert me the instant this hits zero" threshold. E: "do it now and set them all to not track, then i can test that feature also."
  - **Migration 092** adds `inventory_items.track_reorder boolean not null default false`. A single `ALTER ... DEFAULT false` both adds the column and backfills every existing row to "not tracked" in one statement -- satisfies "set them all to not track" with no separate `UPDATE`.
  - **All 11 sites** that used `part.reorderPoint > 0 &&` (the previous fix's guard) now read `part.trackReorder &&` instead: `lowStock`, every `notifyLowStockReached` trigger (allocate/ship/adjust/transfer/build-consume), the Inventory table's status filter/badge (desktop + mobile), the CSV export status column, and Reports' reorder filter.
  - New items default to `trackReorder: false` (`emptyItemDraft`). The Edit Inventory Item modal has a new "Don't track for Reorder" checkbox right after the Reorder point field (inverted boolean binding), and the Reorder point input is disabled while tracking is off so it can't be edited into a misleading state.
  - `persistence.ts`: `Part.trackReorder?: boolean`, `InventoryItemRow.track_reorder`, `INVENTORY_ITEM_SELECT` now includes it with a new `INVENTORY_ITEM_SELECT_PRE_092` fallback (three-tier cascade in `loadInventoryItems`: full -> PRE_092 -> PRE_091), and `saveInventoryItems`'s write payload has its own 400-fallback that strips `track_reorder` if migration 092 hasn't run yet in the target environment.
  - No new CSS -- reused the existing `.checkbox-inline` class (already styled for `.bom-modal-grid` contexts).
  - tsc/build both clean, no CSS changes.
- **(`3afeb73`, no migration)** — **Reorder point of 0 now means "don't track reordering for this item," not "flag it the instant it hits exactly zero."** Found immediately while E started testing the migration 091 work: zeroing every item's reorder point (E: "most item are still one-offs") left a bunch of `0/0` entries still showing in the Reorder List, because the original logic flagged anything at or below its reorder point -- 0 included. E's actual mental model (the common inventory-system convention) is that 0 = disabled, not a threshold of zero. Fixed everywhere a reorder-point comparison happens: `lowStock`, every `notifyLowStockReached` trigger (allocate/ship/adjust/transfer/build-consume), the Inventory table's status badge + filter (desktop and mobile), the CSV export's status column, and Reports' reorder watch/export -- all now gated on `part.reorderPoint > 0` before comparing.
  - **Separate data issue, not a code bug**: E also expected Dead Stock to be empty ("we don't have any inventory right now") but it listed real parts -- confirmed this is stale `quantity_on_hand` left over from the original seed/demo import, not anything wrong with the Dead Stock logic itself (which correctly lists anything with `stock > 0`). Gave E `update inventory_balances set quantity_on_hand = 0, quantity_allocated = 0;` to run directly -- no migration, just a data reset, so real testing starts from a clean, honest baseline (real parts, zeroed quantities) instead of "sloppy info" (E's words) left over from demo data.
  - tsc/build both clean, no CSS changes.
- **(`81e7fe0`, migration 091 confirmed run 2026-08-23)** — **Inventory now tracks Allocated separately from Stock, fixing a real double-decrement bug in the process.** E, looking at the Dashboard's Inventory Value tile: wanted an "Allocated for Projects" figure (in-house inventory not yet shipped) plus, more detailed, "how much we have in shop, how much we are waiting to ship, how long its been in the office and how much dead stock" in the Inventory tab. Investigated first (two research passes) before touching anything, since this touches core inventory math used for real purchasing/build decisions.
  - **The actual finding**: today, "pulled for a project" (BOM/task auto-allocation) and "physically shipped" both decremented `stock` immediately through the same function (`pullFromInventory`) -- no in-between state existed. Worse, **a real bug**: if the same goods got auto-allocated *and* later had their shipment marked "Shipped," stock got decremented twice for one physical departure. E confirmed fixing this and doing the full rework (not a stopgap estimate).
  - **Migration 091** adds `inventory_balances.quantity_allocated`. `Part.allocated` (optional -- see persistence.ts comment on why) is the new field; `stock` keeps meaning "physically on hand," `allocated` is what's earmarked for a project but hasn't shipped, and a new `availableOf(part)` helper (`= stock - allocated`) is what every real feasibility/ordering decision should read instead of raw `stock`.
  - **`pullFromInventory` split into two real functions**: `allocateFromInventory` (BOM/task auto-allocation, task hardware automation -- increases `allocated`, never touches `stock`, gated on `availableOf`) and `shipFromInventory` (shipment marked Shipped -- decrements both `stock` and `allocated`, floored at 0). "Transfer to Project" already correctly decremented `stock` directly (confirmed via its own code comment that it's meant as an immediate real departure, same as shipping) -- only its feasibility gate needed to switch to `availableOf`.
  - **Every "can we use/promise/build this" decision site now reads `availableOf`, not raw `stock`** (~30 sites, mapped via a dedicated classification pass first): low-stock filter and notifications, Purchasing reorder qty math, build can-build/shortage math (`buildInventoryUnit`, `buildReadinessForTransaction`, build draft rows, Reports planned-build shortages), the BOM "Pull from inventory" gate and its "N available now" copy, the Transfer-to-Project modal's max/disabled state, and every Reorder-point display/badge/CSV export across Dashboard, Inventory, and Reports. Genuine physical ledgers (receiving, cycle counts, build consume/complete, the Stock column itself) still read raw `stock`, unchanged.
  - **New tiles**: Dashboard gets "Allocated for Projects" next to Inventory Value. Inventory tab gets the fuller detail E asked for: Inventory Value (on hand), Allocated for Projects, Available Value, plus a new **Dead Stock** tile + list (on hand, no movement in 90+ days). Inventory's main table/mobile cards gained Allocated/Available columns and CSV export columns.
  - **Known real gap, flagged not silently skipped**: "how long it's been in the office" per-unit/per-lot aging genuinely doesn't exist -- stock is one running total, not batches with their own received-dates. Dead Stock uses the best available proxy (most recent movement per SKU, 90-day cutoff) -- real signal, but item-level not unit-level. True aging would need new batch/lot schema, a separate future project if wanted.
  - tsc/build both clean at every stage, no CSS changes.
- **(`12219b4`, migration 090 confirmed run 2026-08-23)** — **Client Ledger restructured: Primary List (master ledger) added above a renamed Closed Projects queue.** E, after seeing the first pass: the default list was auto-populating from every non-closed project, which isn't the intended workflow -- "this section would not be populated until the Project was signed off on and closed." Confirmed via AskUserQuestion: closing a project (PM sets status to Closed) does NOT by itself create a ledger entry -- it just makes the project eligible, sitting in a **Closed Projects** queue until someone deliberately moves it into the **Primary List** (the real master ledger). Once in the Primary List, Active Sites vs Archived Footprint is a manual choice (ongoing SaaS/support relationship vs. fully wrapped up), independent of the project's own status field, changeable later from the Ledger detail's Site Profile.
  - **Migration 090** adds `projects.added_to_ledger` (bool) and `projects.ledger_bucket` (`'active'|'archived'`, nullable) -- same lightweight, separately-loaded `ProjectLedgerInfo` side-channel as migration 089's kickoff/warranty dates, for the same reason (an un-run migration only affects the Client Ledger tab, not the whole app).
  - **Primary List** section: `+ Add` opens a picker restricted to Closed projects not already in the list, plus a Active/Archived bucket choice; the existing Active Sites / Archived Footprint / Financial Summary tabs now filter Primary List entries by that manual bucket instead of by raw project status.
  - **Closed Projects** section (renamed from what was the default "Active Sites" list in the first pass): auto-populated only by `status === "Closed"` AND not yet added; each row has a "Move to Primary List" button that opens the same Add picker, pre-filled with that project.
  - A Closed Project not yet moved into the Primary List has no Ledger detail view at all (no click-through) -- only Primary List entries get the full Site Profile/Lifecycle/Financial/SaaS/Hardware/Closeout Vault detail built in the first pass.
  - tsc/build both clean, no CSS changes.
- **(`658dcfd`, migration 089 confirmed run 2026-08-23)** — **New "Client Ledger" tab: the permanent record for a site once it closes out.** E pasted a long AI-generated brainstorm (Active/Archived/Financial tabs, Site Profile, Lifecycle Dates, Hardware EOL tracker, SaaS calendar sync, Closeout Vault) and asked for it "related to what our system already has." Ran a research pass first to map every piece onto real schema before building anything (see below for what was already real vs genuinely new), then locked scope via AskUserQuestion: full build now (not phased), serial-level EOL tracking (not just line-level), dedicated Closeout document types (not reusing generic ones), Manager+Admin only.
  - **New tab**: `client_ledger` added to `View`/`ALL_TABS`/`TAB_LABELS`/`viewFromHash`/mobile nav icon map/page title+subtitle maps, and to `manager`'s `DEFAULT_TABS_BY_ROLE` (admins get every tab automatically via the existing `isAdmin ? ALL_TABS : ...` bypass — no separate admin entry needed). `Archive` icon (new import).
  - **Active Sites / Archived Footprint / Financial Summary**: pure filters over the existing `projectSites` array (`status !== "Closed"` / `=== "Closed"`) and existing financial fields (`saleAmount`, `allocated`, `saasContractAmount` via the same MRR/ARR math `SaasCalendar` already used, factored out to a shared `saasMonthlyAmount` helper) -- **no new schema for any of this.**
  - **Per-site Ledger detail**: Site Profile and SaaS Subscription are read-only displays of fields that already exist (SaaS is explicitly "edit from the project's SaaS tile" -- deliberately not a second edit surface for the same data). Lifecycle Dates adds two real new fields -- **kickoff_date** and **warranty_expiration_date** on `projects` -- handover/completion is NOT new, it's already `saasStartDate` (stamped automatically the first time a project is set to Closed).
  - **Why kickoff/warranty live outside `ProjectSite`/`PROJECT_SITE_SELECT`**: that select is the single biggest, most-loaded query in the app (every page needs `projectSites`). Adding named columns there risks the same "whole app goes blank if the migration hasn't run" failure class fixed for 087/088 -- instead these two fields get their own tiny, separately-loaded `ProjectLedgerInfo` (`loadProjectLedgerInfo`/`updateProjectLedgerInfo`, `id,kickoff_date,warranty_expiration_date` only), so if migration 089 hasn't run, only the Client Ledger tab is affected, not the whole app.
  - **Hardware Inventory & EOL Tracker -- the genuinely new piece.** Confirmed via research: nothing anywhere tracked a specific physical unit with a serial number and install date (`project_location_items` only ever tracked a planned *quantity* per location). New `installed_assets` table (project_id, project_location_id, catalog_item_id, serial_number, install_date, notes) plus `product_catalog.expected_lifespan_years` (new "Expected lifespan (years)" field in the Catalog item editor). EOL status (`computeEolStatus`) = install date + lifespan, badged Active (`.status.ok`, >6mo out) / Approaching EOL (`.status.warn`, within 6mo) / Deprecating-Replace (`.status.danger`, new CSS class, already past) / No lifespan set (neutral, no install date or no lifespan on the catalog item). **Add Hardware supports pasting many serials at once** (one per line, same catalog item/location/install date) since a garage can easily have 50+ sensors -- avoids a one-row-at-a-time form for real-world bulk installs. Soft-deleted + logged to `deletion_log` on remove, matching migration 088's pattern (no restore UI yet, lowest priority per that audit's own reasoning -- an admin can restore via direct SQL if ever needed).
  - **Closeout Vault**: reuses the existing Project Documents system entirely (same `projectDocuments` state, same `handleCreateProjectDocuments`/`handleDownloadDocument`) -- the new part is 5 real document type categories that didn't exist before (As-Built Diagram, O&M Manual, Completion Certificate, Network/IP Schema, Power/Breaker Schedule; DB check-constraint values `as_built`/`om_manual`/`completion_certificate`/`network_schema`/`power_schedule`), with the Vault's own upload control restricted to just those 5 (the generic Project Documents upload elsewhere is untouched, still offers its original 5 types).
  - **Explicitly not built**: a "lifetime Purchase Order billings" figure in Financial Summary -- confirmed via research that POs link to a project by free-text name match, not a real foreign key, so that number wouldn't be reliable enough to present as fact. Financial Summary sticks to Sale Amount / SaaS MRR-ARR / Allocated Budget, all backed by real columns.
  - tsc/build both clean, CSS brace-balanced (one new class, `.status.danger`).
- **(`7809baa`, no migration)** — **Project detail page: Locations un-tiled back to a full always-visible section; the vacated tile slot became a new Images tile.** E: "I'd like the Locations return to full lines items like it is in the Sales Tab, they should mimic each other... Since this box will be gone, make it for images." Confirmed the interaction model via AskUserQuestion (flat grid captioned by location, not sub-grouped) before building.
  - **Locations**: removed the "Locations" tile + `showLocationsModal` wrapper; `ProjectLocationsSection` now renders directly on the page (right above the tile grid), unchanged props/component -- this is exactly what Sales already does with its own copy of the same table, so the two now genuinely mimic each other again instead of Projects hiding it behind a click-through box Sales never had.
  - **Images (new tile, same slot Locations used to occupy)**: opens `ProjectImagesModal` -- every photo across every garage/lot on this project, in two folders, **PM listed first, then Sales** (origin tag from migration 087/088). E: "Even if there are no images, the empty folder should be there" -- both folders always render regardless of count, with the count shown next to the label. Each folder expands into a flat photo grid captioned by location name, reusing the same click-to-expand lightbox + multi-select + download interaction Marketing's gallery got on 2026-08-21 (no new CSS -- same `.compact-edit-section`/`.site-gallery-grid`/`.file-preview-modal` classes).
  - The tile's own stat row shows the PM/Sales counts directly (`{label: "PM", value: n}, {label: "Sales", value: n}`) so the split is visible before you even open it.
  - tsc/build both clean, no CSS changes.

- **(`e66db8f`, `0a0cc19`, `e19da90`, migration 088 confirmed run 2026-08-23)** — **Site-wide deletion tracking: soft delete + a shared audit log across every entity that used to hard-delete with no trace.** E: "Make sure Any deleted items are tracked and logged if anyone deletes a task and doesn't close it properly (Site Wide)." Investigated first (Tasks already fully compliant), found 15 other entities that were genuine hard deletes with zero record of who/when. Confirmed via AskUserQuestion: full Tasks-style treatment.
  - **Migration 088** adds `deleted_by_email`/`deleted_at` to: `sales_quotes`, `sales_quote_locations`, `sales_quote_location_items`, `sales_quote_bom_lines`, `sales_quote_location_images`, `project_locations`, `project_location_items`, `project_location_images`, `project_shipment_photos`, `purchase_order_files`, `project_schedule_template_phases`, `form_schema_fields`, `presales_hardware_rules`, `site_hardware_rules`, `task_hardware_dependencies` -- plus a new shared `deletion_log` table (entity_type/entity_id/entity_label/action/actor_email/created_at) instead of a bespoke activity log per entity.
  - **Every delete handler for these now PATCHes the soft-delete columns instead of issuing a DB `DELETE`**, and writes a `deletion_log` row. For `purchase_order_files`, `project_location_images`, and `sales_quote_location_images` -- the three that previously also called `deleteStorageObject` -- the actual Storage file is now left alone too, so a restored photo/file isn't just an empty pointer.
  - **Full restore UI** (not just logging) on the 6 highest-value entities per the audit's own priority order: Sales Quotes and Sales Quote Location Images (new panel on Site Builder's list page), Project Locations and Project Location Images (new panel on the Projects list page), Purchase Order Files (new panel on Purchasing), and Tasks (already existed). The other 9 soft-delete and log but have no restore button yet -- lower value (config/rules/templates, or a single line within something bigger), can be added on request since the underlying mechanism is already there.
  - **New Admin "Deletion Log" panel** -- one place to see every delete/restore across all entity types (what, type, action, who, when), gated the same as other admin-only views (`isAdmin || isManagerRole`).
  - **High blast-radius safety net, same pattern as migration 087**: `deleted_at` filters got added to the loaders for Projects, Sales Quotes, and Purchase Orders -- the app's three biggest nested queries. A new `fetchWithDeletedAtFallback` helper (`persistence.ts`) retries any of these without the `deleted_at` filter(s) on a 400, so nothing goes blank if 088 hasn't run yet; applied to every loader touched, not just the big three.
  - **Not fixed as part of this**: `deleteSelectedRecipe`'s phantom-delete bug (see "Not yet fixed" note above) -- different root cause, flagged separately.
  - tsc/build both clean at every stage; no CSS changes.
- **(`8a96679`, no migration)** — **Row-action cleanup: click-to-edit rows, trash-icon deletes, and Task delete moved into the Edit modal.** E, from a Tasks List screenshot with Edit/Delete text buttons circled: "No edit button on long rows, just click the row to edit. Delete should always be a trash can icon... On the task rows, Delete only lives in Edit." Confirmed this as a standing rule (see above), not just a one-off Tasks fix.
  - **Tasks**: List view's desktop table rows and mobile cards, and Board view's `TaskCard`, all lost their Edit/Delete buttons and became click-to-open (`.clickable-row` / `role="button"` card, status `<select>` gets `stopPropagation` so it doesn't also open the editor). `TaskEditorModal` gained a real Delete affordance (trash icon in the footer, next to Close/Reopen Task) -- previously the modal had no delete path at all. Deletion was already fully tracked before this (soft delete, restorable, logged) -- see the site-wide audit note above; this only relocated the button.
  - **Project BOM lines** got the same click-row-to-edit treatment (dropped the trailing "Edit" column entirely).
  - **Every standalone text "Remove" button became a trash-can icon** (`.icon-button compact-remove`, matching the pattern already used elsewhere in the app): Create Purchase's draft line remover, PO paperwork file remove, Form Builder field remove, Schedule Template phase remove, Pre-Sales Rule remove, Site Hardware Rule remove, Task hardware-dependency unlink. Also added matching icons to the Site Gallery modal's bulk Download/Delete toolbar buttons for consistency.
  - Deliberately left untouched: `PanelHeader`'s icon-button "Edit" (already icon-only, a section header action not a row), the Sales Quote detail page's inline "Edit" link next to Site Info (a header link, not a table row).
  - tsc/build both clean, CSS brace-balanced.
- **(`48f2169`, migration 087 confirmed run 2026-08-22)** — **Sales-origin photos are now tagged and shown as a separate group from Project photos, on the same location.** E: "When a sale is closed and transferred over to a new Project, the images should transfer over also, in a folder labeled Sales, this keeps pre project and Project images separate automatically." Checked first: images already transferred on conversion (`createProjectFromClosedWonQuote`), they just weren't distinguishable at all from photos added later. Confirmed via AskUserQuestion: same location (not a separate "Sales" location/folder), tagged and grouped.
  - **Migration 087** adds `project_location_images.origin` (`'sales' | 'project'`, defaults to `'project'`), and backfills existing rows to `'sales'` where the location has a `source_quote_location_id` and the image's `uploaded_at` predates the project's `converted_from_quote_at` (upload timestamps are preserved verbatim through the copy, so this reliably identifies pre-existing conversions without guesswork).
  - `createProjectFromClosedWonQuote`'s image-copy step now stamps `origin: 'sales'` on every image it carries over; the normal upload path leaves it unset and gets the `'project'` default.
  - **Safety net, since this is unusually high blast-radius for a missing column**: `origin` sits inside the big nested `projects` select (`PROJECT_SITE_SELECT`) that the whole Projects page loads through in one call -- if the column doesn't exist yet, that entire query 400s and Projects would show empty for everyone, not just this one field. Added a full fallback select (`PROJECT_SITE_SELECT_PRE_087`) that `loadProjectSites` retries with on a 400, and a matching retry-without-`origin` on the copy-insert -- so this ships safely whether or not 087 has been run yet, it just won't show the grouping until it has.
  - **UI**: both the Site Gallery modal (Project's "Site Gallery" button) and the Project Locations detail drawer's photo/file list now split into "Sales (`n`)" / "Project (`n`)" groups whenever a location actually has any sales-tagged photos -- locations with none render exactly as before (no groups, no empty-state noise). New shared `.modal-section-subtitle` style in `styles.css`.
  - Sales Quote's own galleries are untouched -- `origin` is optional on the shared `LocationImageLike` type specifically so Sales Quote images (which never have it) keep rendering as one flat list.
  - tsc/build both clean, CSS brace-balanced.
- **(`48f2169`, no migration)** — **Marketing's photo gallery now matches Sales/PM's Site Gallery: click-to-expand, multi-select, download.** E: "I cant click the images like i can under Sales or PM. Click and in expands, multi select and download feature." The first Marketing pass (below) was read-only thumbnails with no interaction at all.
  - Clicking a photo now opens a full lightbox (reuses `ZoomablePreviewImage`, same pinch/zoom as everywhere else) with Previous/Next through that project's photo set and its own Download button.
  - Each photo gets a checkbox; a Select all / Clear / Download (`n`) row appears above the grid when a project is expanded, downloading sequentially via the existing `triggerBrowserDownload` helper (same as Site Gallery -- avoids browsers blocking a burst of simultaneous downloads).
  - Deliberately did not add Move/Delete/rename here (unlike Site Gallery) -- Marketing is a cross-project browsing view, not an editing surface, and E only asked for expand/select/download.
  - No new CSS -- reuses `.site-gallery-item`/`.site-gallery-preview-button`/`.file-preview-modal` etc. already audited for mobile.
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`6b5631d`, `93c3d36`, `3c13454`, no migration)** — **Put On Hold/Resume buttons and Hold history log, downsized and de-emphasized through a few rounds of feedback.** E, looking at the new buttons from the migration-085 work: "these will not be used or encouraged to be used" -- they were styled as bold filled-red buttons matching the weight of primary row actions (Receive, Delete), which overstated how often this should be used.
  - First pass (`6b5631d`) shrank them to match the status pill's size but over-corrected to a muted grey outline in the process.
  - E: "I didn't say change the color, i just said smaller" -- `93c3d36` restored the same red gradient `.table-action` uses everywhere else, just sized down to pill height. Lesson: a request to resize is not implicitly a request to restyle -- change only the dimension actually asked about.
  - `3c13454` also compacted the Hold history log entries (detail panel) from a full-size bold heading with who/when underneath to a single small-text line with just the reason bolded, matching the same de-emphasized treatment now applied to the buttons.
- **(`f80df5b`, no migration)** — **New Marketing section: every project's photos in one place, tagged by what's installed there.** E: "Would we be able to make a Marketing section and one spot in there would be [able] to take all the images from the Project folders, by name and by Project as the folder name and combines them for easy access. Perhaps each Folder has a description with Highlights that say Fli, LPR, Smart Signs or whatever would be a good searching point." Confirmed via AskUserQuestion: Highlights = all of them (FLI, LPR, People Counting, Signs, Cameras, Sensors, VPU); access = Marketing + Sales + Manager roles.
  - **No new migration, no new query.** `loadProjectSites()` already nests `project_locations(...)` -> `project_location_images(...)` for every project, so all photos and the structured fields needed for tags were already loaded app-wide -- this was a pure frontend feature.
  - **New `Marketing` component** (`main.tsx`, right after `ProjectShippingSection`): groups every project's photos (`ProjectLocationImage`s where `imageType === "photo"`) by project, computes each project's Highlights from `ProjectLocation.fli`/`.lpr`/`.peopleCounting` (existing structured booleans) plus presence-based tags (Signs/Cameras/Sensors/VPU, derived from whether `signLines`/`cameraLines`/`sensorLines`/`vpuLines` have any entries) -- no new data entry required anywhere. Projects with zero photos don't show up.
  - **Search box + multi-select Highlight filter chips** (reused the existing `.tag-picker-grid`/`.tag-toggle` pattern from Inventory's item tags -- no new CSS) filter the project list by name/ref/highlight text and by all selected highlight tags at once.
  - **Each project is a click-to-expand card** (reused `.compact-edit-section`/`.clickable-row`/chevron pattern) showing its highlight tags and photo count collapsed; expanding lazy-loads signed URLs only for that project's photos (same `photoUrls` state + `useEffect` pattern as `ProjectShippingSection`'s packing photos) into a `.site-gallery-grid` (already exists, used by Site Builder and Shipping) with each photo captioned by its location name.
  - **Nav/access wiring**: new `"marketing"` `View`, top-nav button (Sales/Tasks between), added to `DEFAULT_TABS_BY_ROLE` for `manager`, `sales`, and the `marketing` role per E's confirmed answer, `viewFromHash()` allow-list, and all `Record<View, ...>` exhaustiveness maps (`TAB_LABELS`, mobile nav icon, page title/subtitle).
  - tsc/build both clean. No CSS changes -- every visual piece reused an existing class.
- **(`cfabfb5`, no migration)** — **Removed Inventory's standalone "Receive" action entirely; "Adjust" now requires a reason.** E, looking at the per-row Receive button: "does it make sense to receive anything here if its not the place you order?" -- no: it opened a modal with a free-text "PO number" field that wasn't tied to any real Purchase Order, letting stock go up with zero traceability while the real receiving flow (Waiting on Receiving, migration 082) sat right next to it. E: "Remove it, you can still adjust manually in that edit mode, which should also question why and log who change it and why they did manually."
  - **Removed**: the top "Receive Stock" button, every per-row "Receive"/"Receive Pending" button (both Parts Inventory and the Finished Manufactured Equipment tab share the same row rendering), the whole Receive modal, `openReceiveModal`/`saveReceive`/`findPendingRequest`, and the now-unused `onReceiveStock`/`purchaseRequests`/`onOpenPurchasing` props on the `Inventory` component. `receiveInventoryStock` (the underlying App function) stays -- it's still how the real Waiting on Receiving flow updates stock, just no longer reachable as a free-form action from Inventory itself.
  - **"Adjust Count" is now the only way to manually change stock**, and the Reason field (already existed) is now required -- "Save Adjustment" stays disabled until it's filled in. Who did it was already logged (`InventoryMovement.createdByEmail`, stamped by the existing `recordMovements` helper) -- the gap was only that a reason was optional. Both are now enforced together.
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`cfabfb5`, migration 086 confirmed run 2026-08-20 -- data fix, not schema)** — **Fixed why ~13 raw components were showing up under "Finished Manufactured Equipment."** E: "these aren't Manufactured Equipment - the only thing that would fall under manufactured are item[s] in image 2" (the real equipment types: Enterprise VPU Server, VPU Edge Box, VPU Edge Box with Solar).
  - **Root cause, confirmed in code**: the "Finished Manufactured Equipment" tab filters `inventoryItems` by `category === "Build"` (main.tsx, `inventoryTab === "finished" ? part.category === "Build" : ...`), and separately, `category === "Build"` is what the Build system uses to find a recipe's own output SKU (`finishedPart = inventoryItems.find(part => part.name === recipe.outputName && part.category === "Build")`). So `"Build"` is supposed to mean "this item *is* a finished, buildable output" -- not "used in a build." SKU-0020 through SKU-0032 (2U Server Chassis, motherboard, CPU, RAM, GPU, both drives, PSU, CPU cooler, ribbon/fan cables, PiKVM, HDMI cable) were all seeded with `category: "Build"` from the start (found in the hardcoded seed array near the top of main.tsx, most likely the source of the original Supabase import) -- they're BOM *ingredients* for "Enterprise VPU Server," not the finished item itself, and were just mistagged.
  - **Not a code bug otherwise** -- the actual build math (can-build counts, component consumption) matches BOM components by name, not by category, so this never affected building; it only affected which items show up in that tab/filter, and anyone's mental model browsing Inventory.
  - **Migration 086 (data correction, not a schema change)** recategorizes the 13 known SKUs. My category picks are a reasonable default, not load-bearing -- category is just a coarse label with no other business logic riding on it, so E can freely re-pick any of them later via each item's existing Edit Item -> Category dropdown, no code involved. Also flagged: this only fixes the SKUs known from the seed data -- if anything else was manually miscategorized later, it isn't caught by this migration and would need a one-off Edit Item fix.
  - Migration 086 SQL:
    ```sql
    update inventory_items set category = 'Base' where sku in (
      'SKU-0020', 'SKU-0021', 'SKU-0022', 'SKU-0023', 'SKU-0024', 'SKU-0025',
      'SKU-0026', 'SKU-0028', 'SKU-0029', 'SKU-0030', 'SKU-0032'
    );
    update inventory_items set category = 'Power' where sku = 'SKU-0027';
    update inventory_items set category = 'Communications' where sku = 'SKU-0031';
    ```
- **(`2a749a39`, migration 085 confirmed run 2026-08-20)** — **Replaced the free-form Purchase Order status dropdown with a real status pill + a reasoned "Put On Hold" action.** E: "I don't think this dropdown is useful, the status is useful but not being able to change it here. the only thing that would be handy is the On hold but that should trigger a box that asked why and then is logged." Right -- Ordered comes from Create Purchase, Received comes from actually receiving (migration 082's per-line flow); letting someone just flip either via a dropdown was never honest. On Hold is the one real manual action.
  - **Status is now a plain pill** (`.status`/`.status.warn`/`.status.ok`, same classes/colors the dropdown used), not editable directly.
  - **"Put On Hold"** button (shown when Ordered/legacy-Imported/In Processing) opens a small modal requiring a reason before it'll save -- on submit, sets status to On Hold *and* logs the reason via a new `purchase_order_holds` table (migration 085). **"Resume"** (shown when On Hold) just clears it back to Ordered, no reason required -- E only asked for a reason going *into* hold, not coming out.
  - **Hold history shown in the detail panel** (`PurchaseOrderDetailPanel`), same list style as the receiving log -- every hold placed on an order, who, when, why, newest first. Not just captured, actually visible.
  - **`handleUpdatePurchaseOrderStatus` (the old generic dropdown handler) removed** -- fully dead once the dropdown was gone; every status change now goes through a real, specific action (Create Purchase, receiving, hold, resume) instead of a bare setter.
  - Migration 085 SQL:
    ```sql
    create table if not exists purchase_order_holds (
      id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
      purchase_order_id uuid not null references purchase_orders(id) on delete cascade,
      reason text not null,
      placed_by_email text,
      placed_at timestamptz not null default now()
    );
    create index if not exists idx_purchase_order_holds_order on purchase_order_holds(purchase_order_id, placed_at desc);
    alter table purchase_order_holds enable row level security;
    create policy "authenticated read purchase_order_holds" on purchase_order_holds for select to authenticated using (true);
    create policy "authenticated write purchase_order_holds" on purchase_order_holds for all to authenticated using (true) with check (true);
    ```
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`48d5d425`, migration 084 confirmed run 2026-08-20)** — **"Completed" section, search/filter, and who-purchased tracking for Purchase Orders; removed a redundant expand icon.** E, live-testing Waiting on Receiving: the small expand icon on each row "only opens Edit, I thought I asked for a receive button here" -- turned out the whole row was already clickable to expand (built earlier this session) and the icon just duplicated that ("the whole cell is a expand button, so i don't need an extra button for that, unless you give me reason to have it?" -- no good reason, removed it). Also: "I clicked received all, this should move down to another area titled Completed," plus a request for search/filter by PO, part, date, and who purchased it, "more handy in a year when there have been many products purchased."
  - **New `PurchaseOrdersTable` component** shared between two sections -- **Waiting on Receiving** (anything not yet `Received`) and a new **Completed** section (anything that is), so an order fully received via "Received All" or the status dropdown now visibly moves itself out of the active list instead of just sitting there with a green status pill. Same table/card rendering both places, so they can't drift apart in behavior.
  - **Removed the standalone expand icon** from both the desktop row and mobile card -- the row/card's own `onClick` already did this; having a second, redundant control was exactly what read as confusing/pointless.
  - **Search + date range filter** above Waiting on Receiving, applies to both sections (`poFilters`: text + from/to date) -- text matches PO number, vendor, project ref, every line item name, and (once migration 084 runs) who purchased it, in one box, same "one search field, not four separate dropdowns" pattern the Requests panel already uses.
  - **Migration 084**: `purchase_orders.created_by_email` -- didn't exist anywhere. (The table has an old `requested_by uuid -> profiles(id)` column from the very first schema, migration 001, but `profiles` isn't wired into this app's actual auth -- never populated, not usable.) `createPurchase` now stamps it from `authSession.email` on save; shown as "by {email}" under the PO number/date, matching the "Requested by X on Y" convention Purchase Requests already use. Same 400-fallback pattern as every other optional column this session -- `loadPurchaseOrders` now has a 4th cascading select tier.
  - Migration 084 SQL:
    ```sql
    alter table purchase_orders add column if not exists created_by_email text;
    ```
