- **(`7716a9c`, no migration)** — **The purchasing save-confirmation banner is now a centered, self-dismissing toast instead of an inline bar at the top of the page.** E: "i have to scroll way far up to see it and then i have to close it each time." `.purchasing-action-status` switched from `grid-column` (part of page flow) to `position: fixed` centered on the viewport (`top/left: 50%` + translate) -- stays centered on whatever the person is currently looking at regardless of scroll position, no scrolling required. A `useEffect` on `purchaseActionStatus` auto-clears it after 5s (each new message gets its own fresh window), so the manual "x" is now optional, not required. Small fade/scale-in animation, kept the existing red error variant.
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`b488ad76`, no migration)** — **New "One-Off Items" panel on the Inventory page.** E: "can there be a created list of one Offs so they are tracked, then we can determine if we need to add them to inventory in the future, in case a 1 off turns into many over time."
  - **Purely derived, no new table/migration.** Every real receipt already lives in a Purchase Order's receiving log (`purchase_order_receipts`, migration 082); anything received whose name doesn't match a real inventory item (by the same name-matching convention BOM/PO-line receiving already use) is a one-off. Recomputed live on every render by flattening `purchaseOrders[].receipts` and filtering against current inventory names -- so adding an item to Inventory naturally makes it disappear from this list on its own; nothing to manually mark reviewed or dismissed.
  - **Sorted by occurrence count first, then total quantity** -- E's stated goal was spotting "a 1 off that turns into many," so the item that's shown up the most sits at the top, not just the one with the highest total units.
  - Each row shows total units received, how many separate times, last-seen date/vendor, and a rough estimated cost (last unit cost seen on any order line for that name) -- plus a **real "Add to Inventory" button** that opens the existing Add Item modal pre-filled with the name, last cost, last vendor, and a note summarizing the purchase history (occurrences, total qty, order numbers) -- closes the loop into an actual SKU without retyping anything.
  - `Inventory` gained a new `purchaseOrders` prop (previously only had `purchaseRequests`) to compute this from.
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`ae19e1b4`, no migration)** — **Waiting on Receiving now actually updates inventory, resolving the gap flagged in the previous entry.** E confirmed: yes, update real stock, and "give the option to where it goes, by default it goes to the inventory" -- same choice Purchase Requests already had (warehouse stock vs. straight to the project), just exposed at receiving time instead of at request-creation time.
  - **`receiveInventoryStock` gained an optional `projectName` tag** -- warehouse stock still goes up by the received quantity either way; if the order has a project on it, the movement is tagged with that project so it's visible in Inventory's movement history without diverting the stock count.
  - **New `applyPurchaseOrderReceiptToInventory`** (App level) resolves a PO line's free-text item name against real inventory items (`inventoryItems.find(item => item.name === line.name)` -- same matching convention BOM lines already use, since PO lines have no real SKU reference). No match means nothing to credit -- the receiving-log entry still records the fact it arrived, but silently can't touch a stock count that doesn't exist for a custom/unlisted item. Two destinations: warehouse stock (default, tagged with project name if applicable) or, when the order has `projectRef` set, straight to the project -- mirrors `receivePurchaseRequest`'s existing direct-to-project branch exactly (a movement + a `ProjectAllocation` row, warehouse count untouched).
  - **"Goes to" picker** appears in the Line Items header of Waiting on Receiving's detail panel, but only when the order has a project ref -- nothing to route to otherwise. Defaults to "Warehouse stock" every time (E: "by default it goes to the inventory"). Applies to both the per-line "Log" action and "Received All".
  - **Linked Purchase Request now syncs to "Received" too, and notifies the PM** -- E: "the dropdown should update the PM and its status." Once every line on an order is received, if it has a `sourceRequestId`, `updatePurchaseRequestStatus(id, "Received")` fires (existing function -- local update, remote save, PM/Purchasing-team notification, BOM-line sync, all reused as-is).
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`96de261c`, no migration)** — **Requests panel cleanup, real camera capture for receiving, and Waiting on Receiving row polish.** E, after live-testing Create Purchase end to end: request rows had too many actions and a converted request still cluttered the list; "Take photo" opened a file picker instead of an actual camera; Waiting on Receiving's row wasn't clickable.
  - **A request that's become a real order now leaves the Requests list entirely**, not just gaining a tag -- E: "I also processed this and its still in requests, it should only be in Waiting for receiving now." `filteredPurchaseRequests` now excludes any request with `linkedPurchaseOrderId` set. The request record itself isn't deleted (still the audit trail), it just stops showing in the active queue once it's graduated to an order.
  - **Request rows simplified**: Edit and Receive buttons removed; the whole row is now clickable (`.clickable-row`) and opens the existing Edit modal, same as before just without a dedicated button. Only "Create Purchase" remains as a text button; "Cancel" is now a small trash-can icon (`.icon-button-sm`, matches the app's existing red icon-button theme) that asks `window.confirm(...)` before cancelling -- matches the confirm pattern already used everywhere else in the app for destructive actions (task delete, quote delete, location delete, etc.). Status `<select>` and both action buttons stop click propagation so they don't also trigger the row's open-edit-modal click.
  - **Source filter's "Project" option relabeled "From PM"** per E's own wording ("Reorder or from the PM") -- same underlying filter value, just clearer text.
  - **Real camera capture for receiving photos**, replacing the plain `<input capture="environment">` (which just opens a file picker on many browsers/desktop, not an actual live camera) -- E: "make it just like the camera on the Project capture." Reused the existing `CameraCaptureModal` (full live video capture, used by Sales Site Builder and Project Location photos) instead of building a second camera implementation: widened its `ownerType` prop and the offline-photo-queue's `PendingSitePhoto.ownerType` (persistence.ts, IndexedDB only, no migration) to add `"purchase_order"`, and added a branch in `flushPendingSitePhotos` so a receiving photo taken with no signal queues and retries exactly like Site Builder/Project photos already do. "Add file" (for PDFs) stays a plain file picker -- only "Take photo" now opens the real camera.
  - **Waiting on Receiving rows are now fully clickable** (desktop `<tr>` and mobile card both), toggling the detail panel same as the old "Details" button did. The button itself is now an icon-only `PackageCheck` icon in the app's red theme instead of the word "Details" -- E: "replace Detail icon with theme colors Receive icon." Status `<select>` stops click propagation so changing status doesn't also toggle the row; the detail panel itself also stops propagation so clicking Log/Received All/upload buttons inside it doesn't collapse the row out from under you.
  - **Flagged, not yet resolved**: removing the Request row's "Receive" button leaves a real gap -- the old Purchase Request receiving flow (`receivePurchaseRequest`) is the *only* thing that actually updates inventory stock counts and logs a movement when something arrives (direct-to-project allocation or warehouse stock increase). The new Waiting on Receiving per-line receiving (migration 082) only tracks `quantity_received` and the receiving log -- it does **not** touch inventory stock. Left the old Receive modal/functions in the code (currently unreachable from any button) rather than deleting real, still-relevant business logic without a decision. **Needs a decision from E**: should Waiting on Receiving's per-line "Log"/"Received All" also update inventory stock, matching what the old flow did? If not addressed, inventory counts will silently drift from what's actually been received once orders go entirely through the new flow.
  - Also flagged via spawn_task rather than fixed here (unrelated area, out of scope for this pass): `removeDeviceComponent` (main.tsx ~8148, Build Recipe editor) is a `Trash2` icon button that removes a component from a saved device recipe without a confirm -- inconsistent with the app's established confirm-before-delete pattern used everywhere else, per E's "site wide" ask.
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`ac31101`, migration 083 confirmed run 2026-08-20)** — **Found and fixed why Create Purchase was silently failing: a DB CHECK constraint from migration 032 rejected the new statuses outright.** E used it live: "I did create purchase under Smart Shunt, then the green pop up came up and says 'see it in Waiting below' but it never went down there and it also wasn't removed from this requests."
  - **Root cause**: migration 032 added `purchase_orders.app_status text check (app_status in ('Imported', 'In Processing', 'On Hold'))` -- the three statuses that existed before Create Purchase. Migration 081 introduced `'Ordered'`/`'Received'` as real app-level statuses (every new Create Purchase order defaults to `'Ordered'`; Waiting on Receiving's status dropdown and the receiving-log auto-flip both write `'Received'`) but never widened this constraint. **Every single Create Purchase save, every "Received All", and every manual status change to Ordered/Received has been rejected by Postgres since migration 081 shipped** -- the order was never created, the source request was never linked. This was a real, load-bearing bug, not a UI issue.
  - **Compounding UI bug, also fixed**: `submitCreatePurchase` called `onCreatePurchase(...)` without awaiting it, then unconditionally showed "Purchase saved" and closed the modal in the same tick -- regardless of whether the save actually succeeded. The real failure was silently swallowed into `setAuthStatus(...)`, which (confirmed by grep) **is never rendered anywhere in the app except the login gate** -- so a real error had nowhere visible to go. `createPurchase` (App-level) now returns `{ ok, error }`; `submitCreatePurchase` awaits it and only shows success/closes the modal on `ok: true` -- on failure it shows the actual error in the same `purchaseActionStatus` banner (now has a red error variant), and the modal stays open so nothing's lost. Added a "Saving..." disabled state on the Save Purchase button too, to prevent double-submit while awaiting.
  - **Migration 083 SQL** (run this before testing Create Purchase again):
    ```sql
    alter table purchase_orders drop constraint if exists purchase_orders_app_status_check;
    alter table purchase_orders add constraint purchase_orders_app_status_check
      check (app_status in ('Imported', 'In Processing', 'On Hold', 'Ordered', 'Received'));
    ```
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`ac31101`)** — **Requests panel: simplified the Reason filter into a "Source" filter (All/Reorder/Project), and made the project name the headline for Project-sourced requests.** E, looking at a page full of Reorder Point requests: "this Source needs a filter, all of these are reorders and that is not anything i care about right now, so i want to select, All- Reorder- Project-. Then it can say the project and by who and when." The old "Reason" filter (All/Reorder Point/Planned Build Shortage/Project BOM/Manual) is now "Source" with three buckets -- Reorder (anything not tied to a project: Reorder Point, Planned Build Shortage, Manual) and Project (Project BOM). For a Project-sourced row, the Source column's headline is now the actual project name (e.g. "Straud Medical") instead of the generic "Project BOM" label, with reason/track and the existing "Requested by X on Y" line underneath -- Reorder-type rows are unchanged (reason stays the headline, vendor/source underneath).
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`2650e56c`, migration 082 confirmed run 2026-08-20)** — **Real per-line-item receiving + a receiving log, replacing the whole-order-only status dropdown.** E: "we need to be able to accept each line item and by quantity. For example, I get a partial order with a packing slip of 2 items but not all... create a log of who checked in what and when." Also: "move the upload and camera icon to the top... create a spot that says Received All."
  - **`purchase_order_lines.quantity_received` already existed in the DB since migration 001** (`create table purchase_order_lines (... quantity_received numeric not null default 0 check (quantity_received <= quantity_ordered) ...)`) but the app never selected, read, or wrote it -- every line only ever showed its ordered qty. No migration needed for this part, just wiring: `id`/`quantity_received` added to `PURCHASE_ORDER_LINES_EMBED`, `PurchaseOrderLine` gained `id`/`receivedQty`.
  - **Migration 082 (new)**: `purchase_order_receipts` table -- one row per receiving action (item name + qty snapshotted, who, when), not just a running total. "Received All" writes one row per line that still had a remainder, so the log stays granular even for a bulk action.
  - **Each line item now shows "X of Y received"** with an inline qty input (capped at the remaining amount) + "Log" button -- logging a partial receipt (e.g. 2 of 15) updates that line's running total and appends a receiving-log entry, without touching the other lines. Once every line is fully received, the order's status auto-flips to `Received` (mirrors how `receivePurchaseRequest` already auto-computes Ordered vs Received).
  - **New "Received All" button** at the top of the Line Items section -- marks every remaining line fully received in one action, logs one entry per line that had a remainder, flips the order to `Received`. Greys out and reads "All received" once nothing is left.
  - **Upload/camera moved to the top of the panel and shrunk to two icon buttons** ("Add file" / "Take photo"), replacing the single large drop-zone at the bottom -- matches the compact `.compact-section-header` + `.hidden-file-label` pattern Shipping's "Packing photos" section already uses (`main.tsx` ~15521-15530), per E's "take the example for sale and projects camera and upload."
  - **Panel reorganized top to bottom**: ship-to/payment note -> Paperwork (upload icons + attached file list) -> Line Items (Received All + per-line receiving) -> Totals -> Receiving Log (chronological, newest first).
  - New persistence.ts functions: `updatePurchaseOrderLineReceivedQty`, `createPurchaseOrderReceipt`. `loadPurchaseOrders` now has a 3-tier select fallback (full -> no-receipts -> base-only) so it keeps working through partial migration states.
  - Migration 082 SQL:
    ```sql
    create table if not exists purchase_order_receipts (
      id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
      purchase_order_id uuid not null references purchase_orders(id) on delete cascade,
      purchase_order_line_id uuid references purchase_order_lines(id) on delete set null,
      item_name text not null,
      qty numeric not null,
      received_by_email text,
      received_at timestamptz not null default now()
    );
    create index if not exists idx_purchase_order_receipts_order on purchase_order_receipts(purchase_order_id, received_at desc);
    alter table purchase_order_receipts enable row level security;
    create policy "authenticated read purchase_order_receipts" on purchase_order_receipts for select to authenticated using (true);
    create policy "authenticated write purchase_order_receipts" on purchase_order_receipts for all to authenticated using (true) with check (true);
    ```
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`347b754`)** — **Fixed a real bug in "Create Purchase": Save silently sat disabled with no explanation.** E tried it against a live Reorder Point request and reported "open but nothing happens when i hit Save purchase, save request, receive." Root cause, confirmed in code: Save Purchase required BOTH Order # and Vendor to be non-blank, but pre-filling the form from a request (`openCreatePurchase(sourceRequest)`) only ever fills Vendor -- Reorder Point / Planned Build Shortage requests never carry a `poNumber`, so Order # opened blank every time, and the button just sat disabled with zero visual explanation. To someone clicking it, a disabled button and a broken one look identical.
  - **Now only Vendor is required.** If Order # is left blank, a placeholder (`PO-<timestamp>`) is generated on save instead of blocking -- plenty of real purchases (a quick reorder off a webstore) don't have a formal PO# typed in yet, and there's no reason to force one.
  - **Added an explicit, visible save confirmation** ("Purchase PO-XXXX saved", "Request REQ-00017 saved", "Receipt posted -- 2 of Seagate 3.5in Drive") shown as a dismissible banner between the Request Queue actions and the Requests list. Checked: **this app has no general "action succeeded" feedback pattern anywhere** -- `actionStatus` (Project page) and similar state exist but are set and never actually rendered. That's very likely why "Save Request" and "Receive" *read* as broken too, even though their underlying code was unchanged and correct -- the modal just closes and the row updates in place with nothing telling you it worked. This is page-scoped for now (Purchasing's own `purchaseActionStatus` state); flag if E wants this pattern rolled out app-wide.
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`347b754`)** — **Split the Request Queue into two sections: actions/filters, and a dedicated "Requests" panel.** E: "put it in its own Hero Pill under 'Requests' which for now only come from the PM team." The search/filter row and the Export/Queue/Create Purchase buttons now live in one panel ("Purchase Request Queue"); the actual request list is its own separate, slightly more emphasized panel ("Requests," `.requests-hero` -- heavier shadow/border, not a new component system) with a subtitle stating plainly where these come from today (PM BOMs + automatic reorders). No functional change, purely the layout split E asked for.
  - tsc/build both clean.
- **(`4358ba0a`, migration 081 confirmed run 2026-08-20)** — **Unified "Create Purchase" flow, replacing the old disconnected Purchase Request / Purchase Order split.** E pushed back hard and correctly on the day's earlier document-linking work ("you are linking orders already completed to thing i uploaded... make it make sense to me first"), then typed out the actual flow she wanted after a live walkthrough surfaced several real problems: the "+Request" button was actually a Task-request button in the wrong place; "Add Request" looked broken but was just masked by a leftover Search filter; "Queue Reorders"/"Queue Build Shortages" weren't explained; and Purchase Requests vs Purchase Orders were "two different ways to do the same thing" with no real link between them, forcing double data entry.
  - **One "Create Purchase" modal replaces both the old "Manual request" row and the old "New Purchase Order" form** -- combined fields (vendor, PO#, line items, project, ship-to, tax/shipping, status). Opens two ways: standalone from the Request Queue's new primary "Create Purchase" button (no source request), or from a **new "Create Purchase" button on each Request Queue row**, which pre-fills the same form from that request's item/qty/vendor/cost so nothing gets retyped.
  - **The originating request is never deleted or replaced** -- E: "we dont want to loose record of the request, so that info needs to stay for logging." Saving links the two records (`purchase_orders.source_request_id`, `purchase_requests.linked_purchase_order_id`), stamps the request `Ordered` + its `poNumber`, and the Request Queue row shows "Order placed: PO {number}" going forward instead of losing the request.
  - **PM notification reuses the existing mechanism** -- `notifyPurchaseRequestStatusChanged` already notifies the requester + Purchasing team on any status change; creating a Purchase from a request just triggers that same call for the "Ordered" transition. No new notification system needed.
  - **"Purchase Orders" renamed "Waiting on Receiving"** -- same table/mobile cards (still using the migration-079-era expandable "Details" row from the entry below), status options now include real `Ordered`/`Received` values (`PurchaseOrder.status` extended; legacy `Imported`/`In Processing`/`On Hold` still selectable on old rows so nothing already in the DB breaks).
  - **Real per-order paperwork, not a shared bucket** -- E: "there should be a spot for uploading or taking a picture to be connected directly to the exact order." New `purchase_order_files` table (migration 081, mirrors `project_shipment_photos`'s shape exactly) + its own private storage bucket. The "Attach Purchase Paperwork" section (built earlier today, migration 080) is now fully removed -- its filename-guess/generic-bucket approach is exactly what E was objecting to. The Create Purchase modal has a plain file-attach for the order confirmation at creation time; the "Waiting on Receiving" detail panel has a `capture="environment"` file input (camera on mobile, picker on desktop) for receiving photos/packing slips, both saving straight against that order's `id`.
  - **Deliberately scoped out of this pass** (E: "might not be perfect the first time... lets start with that"): (1) auto-extracting a form from the attached order-confirmation file (OCR/AI-fill) -- the upload works, the extraction doesn't exist yet, and the modal says so honestly rather than faking it. (2) A separate "Order History" view for fully-Received orders -- "Waiting on Receiving" currently still shows every order regardless of status (same as the old unfiltered Purchase Orders table) rather than dropping Received ones into a separate history section. Both are reasonable fast-follows, not silently dropped.
  - **`onQueueManualPurchaseRequest`/`queueManualPurchaseRequest` (the old Manual Request row's App-level handler) removed entirely** -- fully dead once its one call site was replaced.
  - Migration 081 SQL:
    ```sql
    alter table purchase_orders add column if not exists source_request_id uuid references purchase_requests(id) on delete set null;
    alter table purchase_requests add column if not exists linked_purchase_order_id uuid references purchase_orders(id) on delete set null;

    create table if not exists purchase_order_files (
      id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
      purchase_order_id uuid not null references purchase_orders(id) on delete cascade,
      storage_path text not null,
      file_name text,
      description text,
      uploaded_at timestamptz not null default now(),
      uploaded_by_email text
    );
    create index if not exists idx_purchase_order_files_order on purchase_order_files(purchase_order_id);
    alter table purchase_order_files enable row level security;
    create policy "authenticated read purchase_order_files" on purchase_order_files for select to authenticated using (true);
    create policy "authenticated write purchase_order_files" on purchase_order_files for all to authenticated using (true) with check (true);

    insert into storage.buckets (id, name, public) values ('purchase-order-files', 'purchase-order-files', false) on conflict (id) do nothing;
    drop policy if exists "authenticated read purchase-order-files objects" on storage.objects;
    create policy "authenticated read purchase-order-files objects" on storage.objects for select to authenticated using (bucket_id = 'purchase-order-files');
    drop policy if exists "authenticated write purchase-order-files objects" on storage.objects;
    create policy "authenticated write purchase-order-files objects" on storage.objects for insert to authenticated with check (bucket_id = 'purchase-order-files');
    drop policy if exists "authenticated update purchase-order-files objects" on storage.objects;
    create policy "authenticated update purchase-order-files objects" on storage.objects for update to authenticated using (bucket_id = 'purchase-order-files') with check (bucket_id = 'purchase-order-files');
    drop policy if exists "authenticated delete purchase-order-files objects" on storage.objects;
    create policy "authenticated delete purchase-order-files objects" on storage.objects for delete to authenticated using (bucket_id = 'purchase-order-files');
    ```
  - Every persistence.ts write path that touches the new columns/table has a 400-triggered fallback (matching the codebase's existing pattern for optional-column safety), so the app won't break in prod before the migration runs -- it just can't actually link/attach anything real until it does.
  - tsc/build both clean.

Correct -- it was rendering the exact same `purchaseOrders` array a second time as one large card per order (line items, ship-to, payment note, totals), tripling the page's length for no new information beyond what the summary table already showed in condensed form. Removed the standalone `panel full` section entirely. Each Purchase Orders row (desktop table + mobile card) now has a "Details" toggle that expands an inline panel with the same line-item/ship-to/payment-note/totals content, one row at a time (`expandedOrderId` state, no persistence -- collapses on nav away, matches how the rest of the page behaves). tsc/build both clean.

No migration -- pure client-side logic, existing columns. tsc/build both clean.

Fixed: row gap 10px->4px, row padding-bottom 10px->4px, input min-height 34px->27px, input padding 6px 8px->3px 7px, label-to-input gap 4px->1px.   - **VPU's is now a real per-location line type**, same pattern as Camera/Sign/Sensor/Misc (migration 071) -- just widened the existing `line_type` check constraint on `project_location_items`/`sales_quote_location_items` to include `'vpu'`, no new table. Added a VPU's `LocationItemLineSection` to both the Project and Sales Quote location-detail modals (before Cameras, matching E's listed order), filtered by the existing "VPUs" catalog category and a new "vpu accessory" tag convention (same as camera/sign/sensor accessory tags). `BOM Snapshot`'s VPU count now sums real `vpuLines` instead of the earlier BOM-text-name heuristic. **Found and fixed a real bug while wiring this in:** the location-item update/delete handlers (`handleUpdate/DeleteProjectLocationItem`, `handleUpdate/DeleteSalesQuoteLocationItem`) only mapped/filtered `signLines`/`sensorLines`/`miscLines`/`cameraLines` explicitly -- any new line type added there needs the same treatment or edits/deletes on it silently no-op in local state (still hits the DB, but the UI wouldn't reflect it until reload).

- **b7c318a, c86f7d0 (sw.js part)** — **The real reason the install banner never showed** (E correctly pushed back on an earlier wrong theory, see below): Chrome's installability check requires a manifest icon >=192px, and Ergon's `manifest.json` only had 512x512 and 180x180 (apple-touch-icon) -- 180 doesn't clear the bar, so the manifest failed the check outright, in any browser/device, and `beforeinstallprompt` never fired. Confirmed by diffing against VLTD's working `manifest.json`, which ships a real 192x192 icon. Fixed by generating `public/icon-192.png` from `favicon-512.png` (hand-rolled PNG decode/box-downsample/encode in Node, no image library was available in this sandbox -- verified by round-trip decoding the output and visually inspecting it) and swapping it into the manifest. **The inert `public/sw.js` service worker added alongside this (c86f7d0) turned out to be an incorrect first guess** -- VLTD has no service worker at all and its install prompt works fine, which disproves that theory. Left the SW in since it's harmless (no caching) and doesn't hurt, but the icon fix is what actually mattered. **Lesson: when a "steal this feature from VLTD" port doesn't work the same, diff against VLTD's actual working config before inventing a new theory** -- don't assume the test environment (DevTools emulation) is the problem without checking whether VLTD proves that environment actually works.
- **d713efa** — Recolored the "Cloud synced" header pill from red to green. It was only red because Ergon reuses its brand-red accent for "ok" status pills app-wide (`.status.ok` does the same) -- E flagged that red specifically on the sync pill reads as an alert. Scoped to just `.sync-status.synced`, not the app-wide red-for-ok convention. See "Sync status pill" note below for what this pill actually reflects.

See "Mobile bottom nav + install banner" note below for the full shape. Two real bugs found and fixed while shipping this:
  - The nav's "More" overflow sheet used `position: fixed; bottom: 100%` for its backdrop/sheet -- `fixed` positions relative to the *viewport*, not the nav bar, so `bottom: 100%` pushed the whole sheet off the top of the screen. Needed `position: absolute` so it anchors to the nav bar (itself `position: fixed`).
  - The nav (and, on inspection, *any* `position: fixed` element) vanished or rendered squished on Procurement/Admin/Reports specifically. Root cause: Admin's Team Roster table was a bare `<table>` with no scroll wrapper; on narrow screens its columns forced the whole page wider than the viewport, and `html`/`body` had no `overflow-x` guard, so that page-wide horizontal overflow corrupted where fixed-position elements rendered. Fixed at the source (`html, body { overflow-x: hidden; }`) plus wrapped the Team Roster table in a new `.table-scroll` div. See the new Standing Rule above -- any future raw `<table>` needs the same wrapper.

Without the constraint, PostgREST couldn't resolve the `build_transaction:build_transactions(build_number)` embed the app's movements query needs, so **every load of Inventory Movements silently 400'd and came back empty** -- Dashboard "Recent Activity", Reports, and Inventory's own movement ledger had all been reading nothing. Migration 075 adds the missing constraint (after nulling any orphaned refs first, defensively).

(2) **Garage/Lot Address** — new, per-`ProjectLocation`/`SalesQuoteLocation` (migration 074, `address` column), editable in the location detail modal on both the Project and Sales Quote sides. Migration 071.

- **504a2bb** — Inventory movements now record `createdByEmail` (who did it), not just `createdAt`. New `performed_by_email` column (migration 070) + `recordMovements()` helper in the `Inventory`/App component that all ~9 movement-creation call sites route through — any new movement-creating code must go through this helper too, not call `setInventoryMovements` directly.
- **2f2d98f** — **Root cause of "Adjust Inventory doesn't save."** `app_transaction_locks` (used by every Inventory/Project/Build/Purchase-Request save via `withProductionLock`) had a plain unique constraint and the client did a plain `INSERT` to acquire a lock; `releaseTransactionLock` only ever set `released_at`, never deleted the row. Result: **the first successful save on any given key locked it forever** — every save after that silently failed the lock-acquire step before ever touching data. Fixed with an `acquire_transaction_lock` upsert-reclaim RPC (migration 069) that only raises when a lock is genuinely still held. This is the "any new lock-by-key table must use upsert-reclaim" rule in Standing Rules above.
- **cc872b8** — Added a root `<RootErrorBoundary>` around the whole app (in `main.tsx`'s render call) — previously any uncaught render error unmounted to a blank white page with zero indication anything broke. Now shows "Something went wrong / Reload" and logs the real error to console.
- **659d2d1** — Product Catalog and Inventory tables: rows are now clickable (open the edit modal) instead of having per-row Edit buttons; Retire moved into the modal (was a one-tap-away destructive action on the row, now needs the item open first — E: "no accidental retirement"). Inventory: Adjust and Transfer merged into one modal (tabs), Adjust's Item field locks to the row's part instead of listing the whole catalog when opened from a row. Receive shows "Receive Pending" in amber + links to Procurement when there's an open PO for that part.
- **afe6879, a25e9b5, 386e071 (part), 8420e1f (part)** — Password reset UI (Team Roster + User Directory admin buttons, self-service, login-gate link) and visible Sent!/Failed feedback on the admin buttons (previously silent — indistinguishable from broken).
- **ed5599f** — `api/_lib/mailer.js`: shared sender for invite/notification/submittal/proposal emails. Tries Gmail SMTP (`GMAIL_USER`/`GMAIL_APP_PASSWORD` env vars) first, falls back to Resend, falls back to an honest "not configured" response. **Root cause found for "invite/reset emails don't work":** the Resend account is in sandbox mode (only verified for `eck1679@gmail.com`, confirmed via a live 403 from Resend's own API) — it can only ever email the account owner until a sending domain is verified. **E declined to set up Gmail SMTP for now** ("I don't want to create new passwords") — this is still unresolved; real invite/notification/submittal/proposal emails to anyone but E will keep silently failing (with the new honest-fallback messaging, not silently) until either Gmail SMTP is configured or a domain is verified in Resend.

## Data model / architecture notes
- Persistence: raw `fetch()` calls against Supabase REST in `src/persistence.ts` (no Supabase JS client) -- **still true as of Direct Messages (migration 094)**, which polls instead of using Postgres-changes realtime subscriptions specifically because there's no supabase-js/realtime client anywhere in this app. Adding one would be a bigger architectural change than anything else in that feature; flag it if E ever wants true realtime instead of the 5s/20s poll intervals.
- **The real "is this an actual logged-in app user" identity table is `app_known_users`** (migration 012: `user_id references auth.users(id)`, auto-upserted via `upsertKnownUser` on every login), not `team_members` (migration 019, explicitly NOT tied to auth.users -- someone can be a task-assignable "team member" without ever having logged in) and not the unused `profiles` table (migration 001, never populated -- see the much older mailer/purchase_orders notes on this). Any future feature needing "a real signed-in user" as a foreign key (Direct Messages, push subscriptions) should reference `app_known_users`/`auth.users`, not `team_members`.
- **`/api/send-push.js` is the first server route in this app using a Supabase *service-role* key** instead of a user's own access token -- every other API route either doesn't touch Supabase at all (email/Slack notification senders) or wasn't needed to look up data belonging to someone other than the calling user. `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` bypasses RLS entirely and must never get a `VITE_` prefix (which would expose it to client bundles) -- if a future feature needs the same "look something up for a user other than whoever's asking" shape, this is the reference implementation.
- Any new table needs `enable row level security` **plus** at least one policy, even if fully permissive (`using (true) with check (true)`) — otherwise triggers running as the calling user get blocked.
- Ref-number pattern to reuse for any future entity needing a stable sequential reference: create a `<entity>_ref_counters (year int primary key, next_seq int default 1)` table with RLS + policies, a `before insert` trigger doing `insert ... on conflict (year) do update set next_seq = next_seq + 1 returning next_seq - 1 into seq`, format as `'PREFIX-' || year || '-' || lpad(seq::text, 4, '0')`, and a backfill `do $$ ... $$` block for existing null rows.
- **Key-reuse table pattern** (locks, or anything else identified by a reusable key that gets "released"/soft-closed rather than deleted): never do a plain client-side `INSERT`. Write a `security definer` Postgres function that does `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE ... WHERE <row is actually free> RETURNING *`, call it via `rpc/<function_name>` from the client, and treat "no row returned" as "still locked." See migration 069 / `acquireTransactionLock` in `persistence.ts`.
- BOM lines (`BomLine` / `project_bom_lines`) have **no stable id** in the app layer — they're identified by `item` (product name) everywhere, including the new Shipping feature's line items (`item_name`, not a FK). Don't introduce a `bom_line_id` FK assuming one exists; it doesn't.
- `ProjectSite.locations` / `.shippingAddresses` / `.shipments` / `.saas*` fields are all **optional** (`?`) on the TS type specifically because a number of hardcoded/demo `ProjectSite` object literals exist elsewhere in `main.tsx` that predate the real backend cutover and don't set them. Any new field added to `ProjectSite` needs to be optional too, or those literals will fail to type-check (found this the hard way three times this session).
- **`ProjectSite.due` is a free-text display string** (`target_date_display` column -- literally named for display, not storage), not a real date. Values like `"TBD"` are normal (see `addDraftProject`). Never do `new Date(project.due)` and trust the result blindly -- see `parseProjectDueDate()` (main.tsx, right before `ProjectPortfolioHealth`) for the pattern: parse, check `Number.isNaN(parsed.getTime())`, and degrade to "no date" rather than showing a nonsense day count when it doesn't parse.
- Shared upload/signed-URL helpers (`uploadStorageObjectFile`, `getStorageObjectSignedUrl` in `persistence.ts`) are generic across buckets — new photo/file features should wrap these with a bucket-specific pair of thin functions (see `uploadShipmentPhotoFile` / `getShipmentPhotoDownloadUrl` for the pattern) rather than reimplementing upload logic.
- `.modal-panel-wide` (in `styles.css`) is the pattern for a modal that wraps a whole existing `<section className="panel ...">` (BOM table, Submittals list, etc.) instead of a small form — it neutralizes the inner section's own box styling (`.modal-panel-wide > .panel { border: none; box-shadow: none; padding: 0; }`) so it doesn't double-box. Pair with a `.modal-tile-close-row` for the close button when the wrapped content doesn't have its own `modal-header`.
- `ProjectTile` component (`main.tsx`) is the reusable stat-card-that-opens-a-modal building block established this session — reuse it for any future "collapse this section into an overview tile" work rather than hand-rolling another card pattern.
- **Offline photo queue** (`persistence.ts`: `queuePendingSitePhoto`/`flushPendingSitePhotos`, an IndexedDB-backed queue) already exists and is mature -- built specifically for "common in a parking garage" connectivity loss. `CameraCaptureModal.saveAll` (main.tsx ~13118) tries a normal upload first; on failure (offline or a real error, distinguished via `navigator.onLine` in the user-facing message) it queues the photo+blob in IndexedDB instead of losing it. A standing effect (main.tsx ~1245) flushes the queue on login/reload, on the browser's `online` event, and every 45s as a fallback (mobile browsers don't reliably fire `online`). Visible to the user via the "N photo(s) waiting to upload" pill next to the sync-status pill in the header. **Now covers Site Builder/Locations photos AND Shipping's packing photos** (`ownerType: "shipment"`, added 2026-08-19). The raw upload function (`handleUploadProjectShipmentPhoto`, and its Site Builder/Locations sibling `handleUploadProjectLocationImage`) is intentionally reused directly by `flushPendingSitePhotos` and never queues itself -- self-queueing there would duplicate a still-queued entry every time a retry fails again while offline. The queue-on-failure step lives one level up, in the function actually wired to the UI (`handleUploadProjectShipmentPhotoWithOfflineFallback` for Shipping; `CameraCaptureModal.saveAll` does the same job inline for Site Builder/Locations). Any *future* owner type needs the same split -- raw upload function stays queue-free, a thin wrapper at the UI call site does the queueing -- or it'll hit the same duplicate-on-retry bug.
- **Sync status pill** (`sync-status` div in the header, top-nav-actions) reflects exactly two things: whether the initial Supabase session/account-state load on page load succeeded, and whether a small `roleMode` preference blob saved back (`main.tsx` ~1156 and ~1202, two separate `useEffect`s). It is **not** a live tracker of every save across the app -- individual actions (inventory adjusts, project edits, etc.) report their own success/failure locally, not through this pill. If it's stuck on "Sync issue" (amber, hover for the real error), the reliable fix is reloading the page, which re-runs the session-load effect from scratch. Recolored "synced" from red to green 2026-08-18 (see work log) since the brand-red "ok" convention read as an alert here specifically.
- **Mobile bottom nav + install banner** (`main.tsx`: `MobileBottomNav`, `InstallAppBanner`, both defined right after `NavButton`) -- ported concept (not code) from the VLTD app's `BottomNav`/`PWAInstallBanner`. `MobileBottomNav` mirrors the top header's `allowedTabs` (not a hardcoded tab set) so it stays correct per role/per-user override: 2 tabs, a center action button, 1 tab, then "More" for whatever doesn't fit (plus Library/Admin). The center button is role-customizable via `ROLE_QUICK_ACTION` (a `Record<RoleMode, {view, label}>`) -- currently v1, a navigation shortcut with a role-specific label (e.g. sales sees "New Sale" but it just navigates to the Sales tab), not an auto-opened create form; E said this is fine for now and to upgrade specific roles to open a create modal directly when asked. Both components are rendered as siblings right after `</main>`, not nested inside it, so they don't inherit per-page layout quirks.
- **`InstallAppBanner` always renders now** (reworked 2026-08-18, `6ca99f0`) -- it originally only showed when the browser fired `beforeinstallprompt` (Chrome-only, gated by Chrome's own installability + engagement heuristics), which meant it could render nothing even with a fully valid manifest. E correctly called this out: whether the affordance *appears* is something the app controls, whether tapping it can do a one-tap native install is not. Now: the pill always shows (unless already running standalone or dismissed for the 7-day cooldown); tapping it does the real `deferredPrompt.prompt()` flow if Chrome has handed one over, otherwise expands plain manual steps ("open your browser menu, choose Install app / Add to Home Screen") that work on any browser. If a future report says "the pill never shows even after reload," that's the dismissed-cooldown localStorage key (`ergon:pwaInstallDismissedUntil`) or standalone-mode detection, not the install-eligibility question anymore.

- Three real, root-caused bugs, all live-verified: (1) the channel/DM poll was re-triggering auto-scroll on every tick even with no new message, yanking anyone reading history back to the bottom -- fixed; (2) the compose row's @/emoji icons were a different height than the camera/send icons -- fixed; (3) a short thread left dead space above the compose box instead of sitting flush -- fixed.
- Send button is icon-only (paper plane), matching Teams/Slack.
- **Links tab** (channels and DMs) -- derives shared URLs from message history, zero new backend.
- **Message reactions** (migration 113, confirmed run and fully live-verified both directions, channel and DM) -- Slack's signature feature, was entirely missing.
- **Channel member list** ("View members") -- Add People existed, nothing ever showed who's actually in a channel.
- **DM Photos/Links tabs** -- DMs only had raw message history before, no way to see shared images/links separately.
- **Lock a channel back to private** -- unlock only ever went one direction before.
- **Last-open conversation/channel now survives a reload** -- was always dumping back to "Select a conversation."
- **Conversation-list row density** -- was a real 2-line-per-person layout (name row + a separate always-visible email/copy/timestamp row); now one line, with secondary actions (pin/copy-email) as hover-reveal icons instead of permanent text.
- **A real page-level scroll bug on Messages** -- `.messages-shell`'s height formula assumed 175px of chrome above it; actual measured chrome is ~239px. Fixed, confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect` that the page no longer overflows the viewport at all.
- **A real bug where the whole app would flash and unmount roughly hourly** -- the proactive access-token-refresh timer was flipping a `authChecksReady` flag that gated the ENTIRE app behind a full-page "Checking your account..." screen, wiping Messages' local state every time it refreshed in the background. Fixed with a ref that only shows that gate once, on the very first real sign-in check.

After both fixes, re-tested with a live MutationObserver on a fresh tab for 100+ seconds and found zero DOM churn -- could not reproduce further. If E reports it again, the single most useful detail is whether the browser's address bar itself changes, versus just the message panel resetting -- that distinguishes a real navigation from something else.
3. **Not built, deliberately scoped out, good next requests:** typing indicators (real infra risk flagged at the time -- this app is polling-based everywhere else, a presence-broadcast feature is a different shape); pinned messages (Slack/Teams feature, same shape of work as reactions); a DM "Files" tab for non-image attachments (Photos/Links exist for DMs now, Files doesn't).
4. **Two channels both named "PM" were noticed during testing** (not investigated -- may be an intentional duplicate from earlier testing, or a real dupe worth merging/renaming; flagging since it wasn't looked into).
5. **Test messages from live verification are sitting in the real "Inventory & Purchasing" and "PM" channels** (things like "filler message 1" through 10, "isolating write-path 503 check") -- harmless, but there's still no message-delete UI (`channel_messages`/`direct_messages` both lack a DELETE RLS policy, a known long-standing gap, not built since never explicitly requested). Clear manually via Supabase Studio if they bother you, or ask for delete-message to be built.
6. **"Lock the chat" was interpreted as two things** (re-lock a channel, and see who's in it) **and both are now built** -- if E meant something further by "lock" (e.g. a genuinely read-only mode where members can see but not post), that's still open and wasn't guessed at.

- **Security review done 2026-08-27, in direct response to E's post-incident request ("run whatever scan you can... if a security group scan this app online for holes").** Found and fixed: all 7 `api/*.js` serverless routes were completely unauthenticated and publicly callable (repo is public on GitHub) -- worst case, `send-invite-email` as an open phishing relay through this app's own trusted mailer. Fixed via new `api/_lib/requireAuth.js`, applied to all 7 routes + all 7 client call sites in `main.tsx` (including threading a new `accessToken` prop through `Projects` for `sales-quote-extract`). Also confirmed clean in the same pass: no secrets anywhere in git history (incl. the VAPID-key near-miss, verified never actually committed), `.git-credentials-ergon` never tracked, `.env`/`.env.example` handled correctly, Storage buckets correctly scoped, old anon-role RLS policies confirmed still revoked, public share-link RPCs confirmed intentional. See work log and the new standing rule above (`requireAuth` required on every future `api/*.js` route). No user action needed -- ships automatically on next Vercel deploy.

- **Site-wide deletion-tracking: audited and built, 2026-08-22 (migration 088).** E: "Make sure Any deleted items are tracked and logged if anyone deletes a task and doesn't close it properly (Site Wide)." Confirmed via AskUserQuestion: full Tasks-style treatment. Full detail is in the work log below -- summary: every one of the 15 hard-delete-with-no-trace entities the audit found now soft-deletes (`deleted_by_email`/`deleted_at`) and logs to a new shared `deletion_log` table (visible in one place via Admin's new Deletion Log panel). Full restore UI (not just logging) on the 6 highest-value ones: Sales Quotes, Sales Quote Location Images, Project Locations, Project Location Images, Purchase Order Files, and Tasks (already had it). The other 9 (Sales Quote locations/items/BOM lines, Project Location Items, Project Shipment Photos, Schedule Template Phases, Form/Site-Intake Fields, Pre-sales Rules, Site Hardware Rules, Task Hardware Dependencies) soft-delete and log but have no dedicated restore button yet -- an admin can flip `deleted_at` back to null directly in Supabase if one is ever needed, or ask for restore UI to be added to a specific one.
  - **Fixed 2026-08-22 (E confirmed after asking "recipe? we shouldn't have recipes anywhere... or is it receipt?"):** "Device Recipe" is existing terminology for the Build system's assembly formula (e.g. what components make an "Enterprise VPU Server"), not a typo -- once clarified, E asked for the delete bug fixed too. `deleteSelectedRecipe` used to only remove the recipe from local React state; `saveDeviceRecipes` (the debounced whole-array sync) is upsert-only and never noticed a whole recipe had disappeared from the array, so the `equipment_types` row survived and the "deleted" recipe silently reappeared on next reload. New `deleteEquipmentType` (persistence.ts) issues a real DELETE, called directly from the delete action instead of relying on the debounced save. **Deliberately a real hard delete, not soft-delete-and-log like migration 088 above** -- Retire (`is_retired`) already exists as this entity's "hide but keep for history" path, so Delete needs to mean actually gone; still logged to `deletion_log` for accountability. `build_transactions.equipment_type_id` has no `ON DELETE` clause (defaults to RESTRICT), so deleting a recipe that's ever actually been built now fails with a friendly "has build history, use Retire instead" message rather than either silently no-op'ing (the old bug) or a raw Postgres FK error.

Fixed with `width: 1%; white-space: nowrap;` on `.inventory-table th:last-child`/`td:last-child`. See work log for full root-cause detail.

## How to verify before shipping (checklist)
1. `npx tsc -b` — must be clean.
2. `npx vite build` — must be clean.
3. If `styles.css` changed, confirm brace balance (`grep -c '{' vs '}'` or careful read).
4. Commit with a descriptive message, push, and tell the user which migration(s) (if any) still need to be run manually in Supabase Studio — don't assume they've run automatically. Paste the raw SQL into the chat message itself, not just as a file attachment (see "Current setup truth" above re: paste corruption).
5. **Immediately after that commit succeeds, replace the `(pending commit...)` placeholder in the work-log entry you just wrote with the real short hash and push a small HANDOFF-only follow-up commit** — see the standing rule above. Do this every time, same turn, not batched later.
6. For anything E reports as a mobile-only bug, ask for a screenshot before guessing at a fix — two out of three bugs in the 2026-08-14 mobile report were diagnosable from the description alone, but the third genuinely needed visual evidence to confirm even the diagnosis of the first two.
